Meanwhile the peek's right wing is the same 150pt holding a 10pt-wide equalizer (`Skeleton.swift:101`) pinned trailing (`NotchContentView.swift:213`) — 128pt of empty black opposite a title that has run out of room.
    fix: One `MarqueeText` view in `Sources/IslaKit/UI/`, used by all three sites so the behaviour cannot drift the way the lyric search once did. Measure the text against the proposed width via a `GeometryReader` on a hidden `.fixedSize()` copy; if it overflows, offset it in a repeating linear animation with a ~1.5s dwell at each end, a gradient mask at both edges, and a hard bypass to plain `.lineLimit(1)` under `accessibilityReduceMotion` (already read in all three files). Restart on `track.key` change. While there, reclaim the wasted right wing: move the artist line into the trailing wing (leading-aligned), or raise `NotchMetrics.sneakPeekExtension` — the pill is already clamped to `bodyWidth` inside `CompactMediaActivity.bodySize`.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The hand-rolled tracking table corrects a macOS behaviour that does not exist, and reaches 7 of ~87 text sites
    today: Four sites are tracked meaningfully away from the platform: the pane title (`MediaPane.swift:111`, `.islandFont(16)` → −0.2/glyph), the lock-card title (`LockScreenCard.swift:158`, 18pt → −0.35), and both lyric branches (`LyricRow.swift:55,66`, 15–16pt → −0.2). Everything else — the artist line three points beneath each title, every settings row, every button, the 27pt translation result — sits at the platform's zero. So a track title is set 0.2–0.35pt/glyph tighter than its own subtitle: 6–10.5pt of difference across a 30-glyph title, inside one header block.
    apple: macOS ships one set of metrics: whatever `NSFont.systemFont(ofSize:)` gives you is what Finder, Mail, Control Center and every AppKit label draw. Apple's published SF tracking table is a UIKit convention (`UIFont.preferredFont(forTextStyle:)` bakes it in); on macOS the system font is used as-is, so an app hand-tracking some of its strings and not others is the only thing on screen doing so.
    impact: Nothing screams, but the player and lock-card headers do not read as one block — the title is subtly tighter than its own subtitle and than every native label around it. More consequentially there is no enforced type contract: 92% of the app's text bypasses the app's only typographic abstraction, so future changes land wherever they are typed.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/UI/Theme.swift:55-59` states the premise: "Apple ships a table for this and applies it automatically to the semantic text styles — `.headline`, `.body` — but **not** to `.system(size:)`". Measured with SwiftUI's own ImageRenderer at scale 8 over a 58-glyph string, ink width in points: `.body` 364.375 vs `.system(size: 13)` 364.375 (delta +0.000); `.title3` vs `.system(size: 15)` 412.125 / 412.125; `.title2` vs `.system(size: 17)` 457.500 / 457.500; `.title` vs `.system(size: 22)` 569.125 / 569.125; `.largeTitle` vs `.system(size: 26)` 667.875 / 667.875; `.caption` vs `.system(size: 10)` 290.250 / 290.250. (`.headline` differs by 9.375pt only because macOS `.headline` is bold, not semibold.) Control: the same string at `.system(size: 13).tracking(-0.08)` measures 359.875 — 4.5pt narrower, exactly 58 × 0.08 — so the method detects a tenth of the claimed effect. On macOS, SwiftUI applies **no** tracking table to semantic styles. Adoption: `rg -c "\.islandFont\(" Sources` = 7 against `rg -c "\.font\(\.system\(size:" Sources` = 80. `Tests/IslaKitTests/TypographyTests.swift` asserts only the table's monotonic shape, never that any view applies it, and lines 32-37 assert `Theme.tracking(forSize: size) == Theme.tracking(forSize: size)` — a tautology testing nothing.
    fix: Two honest options, both better than today. (a) Delete `Theme.tracking(forSize:)` and `islandFont`, drop the four `.tracking(…)` call sites (`LockScreenCard.swift:502`, `MediaPane.swift:419`, `LyricRow.swift:66`, plus `islandFont` itself) and match the platform exactly — cheapest, and indistinguishable from AppKit. Keep the deliberate uppercase tracking at `NotchContentView.swift:162` / `TranslatePane.swift:221` / `SettingsPane.swift:389`, which is a real typographic need and should be one shared constant rather than 0.8/0.8/0.6. (b) Keep the table but make it mandatory: rename `islandFont` as the only sanctioned way to set type, route all ~80 raw `.font(.system(size:))` text sites through it, and add a source-scanning gate (a `Scripts/test-typography.sh` beside `test-branding.sh`) failing on any `.font(.system(size:` applied to a `Text`. Either way, correct the comment at `Theme.swift:55-59` and delete the tautology at `TypographyTests.swift:32-37`.
    challenges: The premise really is false and I reproduced it independently: measuring a 54-glyph string with NSAttributedString, `NSFont.preferredFont(forTextStyle:)` and `NSFont.systemFont(ofSize: samePointSize)` are byte-identical in width at every style — .body/13 = 348.194/348.194, .title3/15 = 393.853/393.853, .title2/17 = 437.401/437.401, .title1/22 = 543.786/543.786, .largeTitle/26 = 637.810/637.810, .caption1/10 = 277.334/277.334 — so the comment at Theme.swift:52-59 describes a table that is not applied on top of `.system(size:)`. Adoption is as claimed (7 `.islandFont(` uses vs 80 `.font(.system(size:`), and TypographyTests.swift:32-37 does assert `Theme.tracking(forSize: size) == Theme.tracking(forSize: size)` (the file's last line does assert something real). But the entire user-visible consequence is a title set 0.2–0.35pt/glyph tighter than its own subtitle — a few points across a whole title, at sizes where nobody can see it. No user notices, let alone complains. This is a wrong rationale comment plus a weak test, not a defect. | corrected: polish — the code comment and one tautological test assertion are wrong; the rendered difference (≤0.35pt per glyph on 4 of ~87 text sites) is imperceptible.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) No Dynamic Type, no text-size control, no minimumScaleFactor — and the "the window cannot grow" defence is contradicted by 192pt of unused window
    today: A user who has turned on Accessibility → Display → Text size, or who simply cannot read 9pt at 46% white, has no lever at all. Settings offers panel *width* — a taste setting — and nothing for legibility. With no `minimumScaleFactor` either, nothing degrades gracefully: type is one size forever.
    apple: macOS Control Center does not scale either, so a fixed accessory panel is defensible — but Apple always ships some answer. Notification banners, Spotlight and Mail honour the system text size; where a surface genuinely cannot scale, Apple's own accessory UI still never goes below the 10pt caption floor, and fixed-panel apps (Xcode's inspectors, Console) expose their own size control. An app whose most-used sizes are 9 and 10pt owes the user a way to enlarge them.
    impact: The people most likely to want a glanceable notch HUD — anyone who does not want to switch to the Music window — are exactly the people locked out if 9pt is too small. Today the only remedy is turning the app off.
    evidence: `rg -n "dynamicTypeSize|minimumScaleFactor|legibilityWeight" Sources/IslaKit` returns nothing — zero hits across all 66 files. The rationale is at `Sources/IslaKit/UI/Theme.swift:60-65`: "they scale with the user's text size, and both surfaces this app draws are windows that cannot resize… larger type would simply be cut off." But `Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchGeometry.swift:46` computes `maxBodyHeight { max(expandedSize.height, Self.tallBodyHeight) }` where `tallBodyHeight = NotchMetrics.teleprompterBody.height = 400` (`NotchMetrics.swift:24`) while the body actually drawn is `standardBodyHeight = 208` (`NotchMetrics.swift:13`). The window is cut once to `NotchMetrics.maximumWindow = 700×444` (`NotchGeometry.swift:206-219`, asserted in `NotchMetricsTests.swift:9`) for a teleprompter removed on 2026-08-22 — so 192pt of already-allocated, already-transparent, already-click-through window sits below the panel. Precedent for a user control exists: the Panel Width slider at `SettingsPane.swift:78-101` rebuilds the geometry through `refreshGeometry()`. Text size census: 13 sites at 9pt, 18 at 10pt — and AppKit's 9pt is the `.mini` control size while its smallest *text style* (`caption2`, `footnote`) is 10pt.
    fix: Add a Text Size row to `SettingsPane` beside Panel Width: a three-rung `choiceRow` (Small / Default / Large, ×1.0 / ×1.15 / ×1.3) writing a `NotchViewModel.textScale` default. Feed it through the single type entry point (see the tracking finding) so every `Text` renders at `size * textScale` — that is the payoff for routing all sites through one helper. Grow `NotchMetrics.standardBodyHeight` proportionally; the window already has 192pt of headroom, so the window contract is untouched. While there, reclaim the ghost: `NotchMetrics.teleprompterBody` / `NotchGeometry.tallBodyHeight` describe a tab removed on 2026-08-22 and are what reserve that space today by accident rather than design. Add `.minimumScaleFactor(0.85)` to the two readouts that cannot reflow (`SettingsPane.swift:69,97`).
    challenges: Facts check out: `rg -n "dynamicTypeSize|minimumScaleFactor|legibilityWeight" Sources/IslaKit` returns zero hits; NotchGeometry.swift:39 `tallBodyHeight = NotchMetrics.teleprompterBody.height` = 400 against `standardBodyHeight = 208` (NotchMetrics.swift:13), with the window pinned to `maximumWindow` 700×444 at NotchGeometry.swift:206-219 — so ~192pt of allocated transparent window really does sit unused, and the Theme.swift:61-65 'windows that cannot resize' rationale is not accurate. Size census confirmed: 13 sites at 9pt, 18 at 10pt. But the severity leans on a macOS setting that does not work the way stated — macOS 15 has no global Dynamic Type; Accessibility → Display text size applies only to a handful of Apple apps, and no menu-bar/Control-Center-class surface scales. So this is a legibility gap for a minority (real, since 9pt at 46% white is the app's workhorse label), not a broken system contract. | corrected: minor — no text-size lever and no graceful degradation for users who find 9–10pt hard to read; the 'cannot grow' rationale is demonstrably wrong, but macOS peers do not scale either and most users are unaffected.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/M) Fifteen distinct text sizes, four of them half-point — there is no type ramp, only per-site tuning
    today: Sibling labels in the same pane are set half a point apart with no rule behind it. In one Settings section the header is 9, the row title is 11.5, the segmented options are 10.5 and the note is 10.5 — four sizes inside a group of 26pt rows. The Shelf footer sets "Selected: 3" at 9 and "Deselect" at 10 on the same baseline.
    apple: AppKit offers three control sizes (13 / 11 / 9) and eight text styles, and a native pane picks two or three of them. Apple ships no half-point type — there is no 10.5 or 11.5 anywhere in macOS — because the ramp exists so a size *means* something (header, body, caption) instead of being tuned per screen.
    impact: Nothing is wrong on any single screen, but the panel reads as assembled rather than designed — the reason it sits a half-step off a native pane even when each individual choice is defensible.
    evidence: Literal sizes across `Sources/IslaKit/UI`: 7(×1), 8(×3), 9(×13), 10(×18), 10.5(×3), 11(×12), 11.5(×8), 12(×4), 13(×3), 15, 16, 20(×3), 21(×2), 22(×2), 26, 28 — plus computed 12.5 and 14 (`LockScreenCard.swift:158,162` ternaries), 13.5 (`LockScreenCard.swift:434`), 18 (`LockScreenCard.swift:158`), `side/3` (`LockScreenCard.swift:183`) and the ladder `[27, 20, 15, 11]` (`TranslatePane.swift:28`). Excluding SF Symbol glyph sizes that is 15 text sizes: 9, 10, 10.5, 11, 11.5, 12, 12.5, 13, 13.5, 14, 15, 16, 18, 20, 27. macOS's own ramp, read from `NSFont.preferredFont(forTextStyle:)`, is 10, 11, 12, 13, 15, 17, 22, 26. Six of Isla's sizes live within three points of each other doing the same job: `SettingsPane.swift:388` section header 9, `SettingsPane.swift:406` row title 11.5, `SettingsPane.swift:446` segmented option 10.5, `SettingsPane.swift:491` note 10.5, `SettingsPane.swift:69` readout 10, `ShelfPane.swift:103` footer button 10, `NotchContentView.swift:421` counter 10, `Confirmation.swift:74` confirm button 10, `ShelfPane.swift:96` "Selected: %d" 9.
    fix: Define semantic roles on `Theme` and use them everywhere: `.title` (16), `.subtitle` (12), `.body` (13), `.label` (11), `.caption` (10), `.sectionHeader` (10, uppercase, one shared tracking constant). Collapse 10.5 → 10 or 11, 11.5 → 11 or 12, 12.5 → 12, 13.5 → 13, and 9 → 10 (AppKit's caption floor — the same change that helps the accessibility finding). Six roles cover every site across the five panes; the two exceptions worth keeping are `TranslatePane`'s content-length ladder and `LyricRow`'s size parameter, both deliberate and documented.
    challenges: The census reproduces exactly — `rg -o "\.system\(size: [0-9.]+" Sources/IslaKit/UI` gives 7×1, 8×3, 9×13, 10×18, 10.5×3, 11×12, 11.5×8, 12×4, 13×3, 15, 16×2, 20×3, 21×2, 22×2, 26, 28, plus the computed 12.5/13.5/14/18 in LockScreenCard.swift:158,162,434 and the ladder in TranslatePane.swift:28; ShelfPane.swift:96 does set 9pt beside ShelfPane.swift:103's 10pt. But half-point deltas between sibling labels are below the threshold anyone perceives, let alone names: this is maintainability debt in the type system, not something a user reports. The rubric puts unnamed cosmetic parity at polish. | corrected: polish — design-system debt (no ramp, four half-point sizes); no user can name the difference between an 9pt and a 10pt sibling label.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) The entire type hierarchy is a white-opacity ramp, and Increase Contrast is wired only to glass borders
    today: A user who turns on Increase Contrast because labels are too faint gets a brighter *border* around the glass and identically faint 9pt text at 4.58:1 — a value chosen to sit exactly on the AA line and then frozen there.
    apple: macOS's Increase Contrast raises the alpha of the semantic label colours themselves — `secondaryLabelColor` and `tertiaryLabelColor` have high-contrast variants — so every native label in the system gets more contrast the moment the setting flips. The point of the setting is text, not chrome.
    impact: The one system control that exists to make faint text readable does nothing for the faintest text in the app. For a low-vision user that is the difference between a usable panel and a decorative one.
    evidence: The ramp is at `Sources/IslaKit/UI/Theme.swift:81-90`: `secondary = .white.opacity(0.55)`, `tertiary = .white.opacity(0.46)`, plus `LyricRow.swift:78` `.white.opacity(distance == 1 ? 0.34 : 0.18)` and `LockScreenCard.swift:163,284,369` at 0.62 / 0.45 / 0.45. `SystemAppearance` publishes `increaseContrast` (`Services/SystemAppearance.swift:21`) but every consumer is a border: `GlassSurface.swift:267-268` (`.white.opacity(appearance.increaseContrast ? 0.85 : 0.18)`, `lineWidth: … ? 1.5 : 1`) and `GlassSurface.swift:298`. `rg -n "increaseContrast" Sources/IslaKit` returns exactly those three lines — no text colour reads it anywhere. `Tests/IslaKitTests/AccessibilityDisplayTests.swift` covers Reduce Transparency and the read-through, never text. Computed contrast against the panel's black ground: 0.46 → 4.58:1 (matching the comment at Theme.swift:82-86, and only just clearing AA), 0.34 → 2.89:1, 0.18 → 1.54:1.
    fix: Make the ramp a function of the setting rather than a constant. Turn `Theme.secondary`/`Theme.tertiary` into computed values (or an `@Environment`-injected `TypeRamp`) reading `SystemAppearance.shared.increaseContrast`, lifting 0.46 → ~0.70 (8.5:1) and 0.55 → ~0.80 when on; do the same for `LyricRow.swift:78`'s 0.34/0.18 falloff (→ ~0.55/0.38) so lyric depth stays readable instead of vanishing. Extend `AccessibilityDisplayTests` with an assertion that the tertiary ramp differs between the two states, so this is protected the way the glass path already is.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) Shelf filenames tail-truncate so every screenshot reads the same, while clipboard prose middle-truncates — the two modes are swapped
    today: Two screenshots taken the same day carry identical labels in the Shelf, so the label conveys nothing and you must hover or open them to tell them apart. In the Clipboard, a copied sentence reads "The quick brown …zy dog again." instead of showing its beginning.
    apple: Finder's icon view truncates filenames in the *middle* precisely so the disambiguating tail — timestamp and extension — survives: "Screenshot 2026-…14.22.10.png". Conversely Spotlight, Notes and every macOS text preview truncate prose at the *tail*, because prose is read from the start.
    impact: The Shelf's labels stop identifying files at exactly the moment there is more than one screenshot on it — the Shelf's whole use case. The Clipboard makes you read around a hole in the middle of every entry.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift:140-146`: `Text(item.name).font(.system(size: 9)).lineLimit(2).multilineTextAlignment(.center).frame(height: 24, alignment: .top)` inside `.frame(width: 86, height: 92)` with `.padding(.horizontal, 6)` — 74pt per line, default tail truncation, and `item.name` is `url.lastPathComponent` (`Services/ShelfStore.swift:17`), the full filename with extension. Rendered through ImageRenderer at scale 4 at exactly those metrics, "Screenshot 2026-09-03 at 14.22.10.png" draws as **"Screenshot / 2026-09-03 at…"** — both the time and the `.png` are gone; the Russian "Снимок экрана 2026-09-03 в 14.22.10.png" draws as "Снимок экрана / 2026-09-03 в…". Measured, the string needs 177.4pt against ~148pt of two-line room (194.2pt in Russian). Meanwhile `Sources/IslaKit/UI/SpoilerField.swift:163-167` sets clipboard previews with `.lineLimit(1).truncationMode(.middle)` — arbitrary copied prose losing its middle.
    fix: Swap them: `ShelfPane.swift:144` gets `.truncationMode(.middle)`, `SpoilerField.swift:166` gets `.truncationMode(.tail)`. Both are one-line changes with no layout consequence. If the Shelf card should also assert file identity, split `item.name` into stem + extension and draw the extension unelided the way Finder's list view does.
- [CONFIRMED] (polish/S) Number readouts use C-locale formatting and untranslated unit suffixes the localization gate cannot see
    today: On a Russian system, Settings reads "0.35s" and "560 pt" and the lyric offset reads "+0.25s", beside rows whose titles are correctly translated. In the same pane `ByteCountFormatter` (`SettingsPane.swift:273`) correctly produces "1,2 ГБ" — so one pane shows a comma decimal and a period decimal simultaneously.
    apple: macOS in Russian writes 0,35 с and 560 пт. Apple's numeric UI goes through `NumberFormatter`, `MeasurementFormatter` or Swift's `.formatted()`, all of which take the separator and unit abbreviation from the user's locale — `ByteCountFormatter` in this very file already does it correctly.
    impact: Small, but it is precisely the tell that reads as "ported" rather than "localized" to a Russian user, and it sits in the one pane whose job is to look like System Settings.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift:68` — `Text(String(format: "%.2fs", hoverDelay))`; `SettingsPane.swift:96` — `Text("\(Int(bodyWidth)) pt")`; `Sources/IslaKit/UI/LyricsStage.swift:408` — `Text(String(format: "%+.2fs", lyrics.userOffset))`. `String(format:)` without a locale uses POSIX, so the decimal separator is always a period. `grep -n 'pt"' Resources/{en,ru}.lproj/Localizable.strings` finds nothing, so the interpolated key `"%lld pt"` misses in both tables and falls through to the literal. `Scripts/test-localizations.sh` only diffs en↔ru key sets (`assert english == russian`) — a string absent from *both* tables passes the gate, which is why these three survived it.
    fix: `hoverDelay.formatted(.number.precision(.fractionLength(2)))` plus a localized `"%@ s"` key, or `Measurement(value: hoverDelay, unit: UnitDuration.seconds).formatted()`; `Text(localized("%d pt", Int(bodyWidth)))` with the key added to both `.lproj` tables; the same for the signed lyric offset (`.number.sign(strategy: .always())`). Then close the hole in the gate: extend `Scripts/test-localizations.sh` to scan `Sources` for `Text("` / `String(format:` literals containing letters and fail on any whose key is absent from `en.lproj`, so the next one is caught rather than shipped.

## a11y
verdict: Split verdict, and the split is stark. The *display* half of accessibility is genuinely Apple-grade: Reduce Motion is honored in 12 files with real thought behind it (`EqualizerPresentation` at Skeleton.swift:41-49 structurally removes the animated equalizer rather than freezing it; SpoilerField.swift:44-55 draws one static frame; RefusalShake.swift:30 declines to fake a substitute), `SystemAppearance` watches Reduce Transparency / Increase Contrast / Reduce Motion live off `NSWorkspace` and is unit-tested (AccessibilityDisplayTests.swift), nothing strobes or autoplays video, and Differentiate Without Color is effectively satisfied without ever naming it — every stateful control also changes shape, weight or luminance (ModeToggle's dot, the heart's fill, ConfirmRow's title swap, the rail's fill), so I found no state carried by hue alone. Text contrast was deliberately fixed (Theme.swift:82-87 raises `tertiary` to 4.58:1 with the arithmetic in the comment). Roughly 50 controls across Music, Lyrics and the lock card carry real `accessibilityLabel`/`Value`/`AdjustableAction`, and the island scrubber (MediaPane.swift:284-300) is a textbook adjustable element. But all of that is stranded behind a front door that does not open: the app is `.accessory`, the panel is `canBecomeMain = false` and `canBecomeKey = false` except while Translate is up, it has no AX title, and there is not one `NSAccessibility` call in 14k lines. The codebase states the problem itself at GlobalHotKey.swift:14 — "assistive tech has no way in." So VoiceOver cannot reach the panel, Full Keyboard Access cannot operate a single tab, and Voice Control is reduced to its mouse-grid fallback. Where AX *could* reach, the newer surfaces regress: nine Settings switches have no name, every clipboard row announces the identical string, and shelf cards have no accessibility identity at all. Against the paid competition this is a wash (none of NotchNook/Alcove/MediaMate are accessible either); against the "Apple built it" bar this is the weakest dimension in the product, and the two blockers below are the difference between a beautiful utility and one a blind or keyboard-only user can actually buy.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/L) VoiceOver has no way to reach the panel — every label in the app is unreachable
    today: The app never activates, never owns a key or main window, exposes an untitled borderless panel, and posts no AX notifications when the panel appears. There is no code path that reacts to VoiceOver being on (no `NSWorkspace.shared.isVoiceOverEnabled`, no `voiceOverStatusDidChangeNotification` observer).
    apple: Apple ships plenty of non-activating, notch-adjacent UI — Control Center, the Now Playing menu extra, the volume/brightness HUD, Notification Center — and every one of them is a full accessibility citizen: the window carries an AX title and role, and the system posts `windowCreated`/`focusedWindowChanged` when it appears so VoiceOver's cursor lands there. The HIG's accessibility guidance treats reachability as the precondition for everything else.
    impact: A blind user installs Isla, hears nothing, and has no gesture that reaches it. The ~50 carefully written accessibility labels in MediaPane, LyricsStage and LockScreenCard are dead code. This is the single fact that makes the app unusable rather than merely awkward.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/App/IslaApplication.swift:12 `static let activationPolicy: NSApplication.ActivationPolicy = .accessory` (set once at :18, never changed). Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchPanel.swift:28-29 `override var canBecomeKey: Bool { acceptsKeyboard }` / `override var canBecomeMain: Bool { false }`, with `acceptsKeyboard` false except while Translate asks (NotchController.swift:874). The repo says it outright — Sources/IslaKit/App/GlobalHotKey.swift:14: "The panel is otherwise unreachable from the keyboard: it never activates, so nothing routes a key press to it and assistive tech has no way in." `rg 'NSAccessibility|isAccessibilityElement|accessibilityRole|voiceOver' Sources` returns nothing — zero AppKit accessibility calls in 14k lines. `rg '\.title\b' Sources/IslaKit/Notch/*.swift` returns nothing: the borderless panel is never given a title, so its `AXTitle` is empty.
    fix: In `NotchPanel.init` set `title = ProductIdentity.displayName` and `setAccessibilityTitle(ProductIdentity.displayName)` so the window is nameable in VoiceOver's window chooser. In `NotchController.setOpen(_:)` (NotchController.swift:900-915) post `NSAccessibility.post(element: panel, notification: .windowCreated)` on open and `.uiElementDestroyed` on collapse. Add a `voiceOverStatusDidChangeNotification` observer to `NotchController`: while `NSWorkspace.shared.isVoiceOverEnabled`, set `panel.acceptsKeyboard = true` for the life of the open panel (the mechanism already exists for Translate) and force `vm.isPinnedOpen = true`, so the panel becomes `AXFocusedWindow`, does not fold under a user with no pointer, and VoiceOver can drive it with VO+arrow like any other window.
    challenges: Every code fact checks out: Sources/IslaKit/App/IslaApplication.swift:12 `.accessory` set once at :18; NotchPanel.swift:28-29 `canBecomeKey { acceptsKeyboard }` / `canBecomeMain { false }` with acceptsKeyboard driven only by `Tab.needsKeyboard { self == .translate }` (NotchViewModel.swift:32, NotchController.swift:869-874); GlobalHotKey.swift:14 says it verbatim; `rg NSAccessibility Sources` exits 1 (zero AppKit AX calls in 66 files); no `title` is ever set on the panel. Two details are wrong. (1) `voiceOverStatusDidChangeNotification` is a UIKit/iOS name — `grep -rl voiceOverStatusDidChange` over the whole MacOSX.sdk Frameworks tree returns nothing. The macOS equivalents are KVO on `NSWorkspace.shared.isVoiceOverEnabled` (AppKit/Headers/NSAccessibility.h:113) and `NSWorkspaceAccessibilityDisplayOptionsDidChangeNotification` (NSAccessibility.h:122). (2) The stated mechanism is off: VoiceOver does not need a key or main window to navigate an app — it walks AXWindows and dispatches AXPress through the AX API, so `canBecomeKey == false` is not what blocks it. What actually blocks it is that an `.accessory` app that never activates never becomes AXFocusedApplication (no Dock icon, no menu-bar item, no Cmd-Tab entry, so no VoiceOver Application-Chooser route), and nothing posts `NSAccessibilityAnnouncementRequestedNotification`/`AXLayoutChanged` to move the VO cursor when the panel appears. | corrected: VoiceOver has no practical route to the panel and the app does nothing to create one — still a blocker. But say it correctly: the blocker is that a never-activating `.accessory` app is never the focused application and posts no AX notification when the panel appears, not that `canBecomeKey` is false (VoiceOver navigates non-key windows fine). And the API to observe is KVO on `NSWorkspace.shared.isVoiceOverEnabled` (NSAccessibility.h:113); `voiceOverStatusDidChangeNotification` is iOS-only and does not exist in the macOS SDK.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/L) Full Keyboard Access can open the panel and then do nothing with it
    today: ⌥⌘I opens the panel and pins it. Tab, Shift-Tab, arrows, Space and Return then reach nothing: with `canBecomeKey == false` the window is never key, so AppKit routes no key events to it, `NotchPanel.sendEvent` (including its Escape handler at :81-95) is never invoked, no control can take focus, and `NotchButtonStyle` draws no focus ring by design. Four of five tabs are pointer-only; Translate is the sole exception because it flips `acceptsKeyboard`.
    apple: Under Full Keyboard Access every macOS control accepts focus and draws the system focus ring; menu-bar extras open with a key equivalent and are then arrow-navigable and dismissible with Escape; Apple's own HUD panels hand focus to their content when opened deliberately.
    impact: A keyboard-only user (RSI, tremor, no trackpad) can summon the island but cannot play/pause, skip, switch tabs, copy a clipboard row, open a shelf file, or toggle a setting. Escape does not even close it — they must press ⌥⌘I a second time.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchPanel.swift:28 `canBecomeKey` is false unless the Translate tab asks. Sources/IslaKit/UI/Theme.swift:95-97: `/// Flat, focus-free button used for every control in the panel.` `struct NotchButtonStyle: ButtonStyle { var size: CGFloat = 26`. `rg 'focusable\(|@FocusState|\.focused\(|keyboardShortcut|onKeyPress' Sources` matches only TranslatePane.swift:17/98/104. NotchController.swift:530-543 `toggle()` opens on ⌥⌘I and sets `isPinnedOpen`, so the panel does stay put — there is simply nothing to press.
    fix: Set `panel.acceptsKeyboard = true` whenever the panel is opened deliberately (the ⌥⌘I/click paths in `NotchController.toggle()` and `islandClicked()`), not only for Translate — `.nonactivatingPanel` already means key status costs the user nothing. Add a `@FocusState` focus target per pane with a declared order (rail → pane content → footer), map ←/→ or ↑/↓ on the rail to `vm.select(_:)`, and restore the focus ring by dropping `NotchButtonStyle`'s implicit `.focusEffectDisabled` posture (give it a visible `RoundedRectangle` focus overlay keyed off `@Environment(\.isFocused)`). Escape already collapses via `NotchPanel.onEscape` once the window can be key.
    challenges: Mechanism confirmed: NotchPanel.swift:28 keeps `canBecomeKey` false, so no keyDown reaches sendEvent (NotchPanel.swift:80-101) and the Escape branch never fires; `rg 'focusable\(|@FocusState|\.focused\(|keyboardShortcut|onKeyPress' Sources` matches only TranslatePane.swift:17/98/104; NotchButtonStyle draws no focus ring (Theme.swift:95-112). But 'do nothing with it' is inflated: NotchController.swift:874 flips `acceptsKeyboard`, and Translate is fully typeable end-to-end (TranslatePane.swift:98 `.focused($focused)`, :104 `onKeyPress(.escape)`, plus NotchPanel.swift:52-58/120-124 hand-dispatching ⌘A/X/C/V/Z because an accessory app has no Edit menu) — the ⌥⌘T clipboard-translate flow works without a pointer. Also note the same root cause as the blocker above, so it should not be scored twice at top severity. | corrected: major — with the panel pinned open by ⌥⌘I, Music, Shelf, Clipboard and Settings are pointer-only (no focus ring, no key events, no keyboardShortcut anywhere); Translate is the one operable tab, and even there the result's CopyButton has no keyboard route.
- [DISPUTED] (major/S) All nine Settings switches are nameless to VoiceOver and Voice Control
    today: Each switch's accessibility label is the empty string. VoiceOver announces a run of ten identical "off, switch" elements; Voice Control has no name to match against.
    apple: Every switch in System Settings carries its row's label, so VoiceOver says "Launch at Login, off, switch" and Voice Control accepts "turn on Launch at Login". `labelsHidden()` is documented as hiding the label *visually* while keeping it for accessibility — passing `""` throws it away instead.
    impact: Even for a user who reaches the pane (via Voice Control's mouse grid today, or after the two blockers are fixed), Settings is a stack of anonymous switches. Toggling privacy or screen-recording exclusion by ear is guesswork, and these are exactly the switches where a wrong guess leaks clipboard contents into a recording.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift:413-416 inside `toggleRow`: `Toggle("", isOn: isOn).toggleStyle(.switch).controlSize(.mini).labelsHidden()`. The visible name is a *sibling* `Text(title)` at :409. The row has no `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` and no `.accessibilityLabel` (contrast SettingsPane.swift:75-76 and :103-104, where the two sliders *do* combine and label). `rg 'toggleRow\(' Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift` = 9 call sites: Launch at Login, Open on Hover, Save Clipboard Screenshots, Show Lyrics, Peek at New Tracks, Show on Lock Screen, Hide From Screen Recording, plus a `ForEach` over `PrivacyMode.Section.allCases` (clipboard, translate).
    fix: One-line change in `toggleRow`: `Toggle(title, isOn: isOn).labelsHidden()` — identical layout, correct AX name. Do the same for `choiceRow` (SettingsPane.swift:440-465), where the segmented options carry `.isSelected` but the group has no label; wrap the row in `.accessibilityElement(children: .contain).accessibilityLabel(title)`.
    challenges: The code is exactly as quoted — SettingsPane.swift:403-419 `toggleRow`, with `Toggle("", isOn: isOn).toggleStyle(.switch).controlSize(.mini).labelsHidden()` at 413-416 and the visible name as a sibling `Text(title)` at :409, no `.accessibilityElement`/`.accessibilityLabel` on the row, in contrast to the two sliders at :75-76 and :103-104 which do combine and label. Three details are wrong. (a) The count is nine, not ten: 7 static toggleRow calls (34, 39, 108, 133, 144, 155, 227) plus one inside `ForEach(PrivacyMode.Section.allCases)` at :221 rendering 2 (clipboard, translate); the `rg` cited returns 9 lines only because one is the declaration at :403. (b) VoiceOver does not hear "a run of identical off, switch elements": the sibling `Text(title)` at :409 is its own unignored AX element, so linear VO navigation alternates "Launch at Login" / "off, switch". (c) `.labelsHidden()` is not the cause — Apple documents that a hidden label is still used for accessibility; the cause is the empty string in `Toggle("")`. | corrected: Nine Settings switches carry no programmatic name, so Voice Control has nothing to match and the VoiceOver rotor lists nine anonymous switches; the visible title is announced as a separate adjacent static-text element rather than as the switch's name. The fix is `Toggle(title, isOn:).labelsHidden()` or `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` on the row (as the two sliders already do at :75-76 and :103-104), not removing `labelsHidden()`. ;; Code confirmed: SettingsPane.swift:413-416 is `Toggle("", isOn: isOn).toggleStyle(.switch).controlSize(.mini).labelsHidden()` with the visible name a sibling `Text(title)` at :409, and no `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` on the row — unlike the two sliders at :75-76 and :103-104 which do combine and label. Count is 9 instances (8 `toggleRow` call sites at :34/:39/:108/:133/:144/:155/:221/:227, one of them a ForEach over 2 PrivacyMode sections), not ten. Severity is inflated: because the row is *not* combined, the `Text` remains its own AX element immediately preceding the switch, so VoiceOver linear navigation still hears 'Launch at Login' then 'off, switch' — it is not 'a run of ten identical elements'. The real loss is Voice Control name matching and unambiguous announcement. | corrected: minor — the switches carry an empty AX label, so Voice Control cannot target them by name and VoiceOver must infer the name from the adjacent static text rather than from the control.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) Every clipboard row reads as "Copy Entry" — the history is unnavigable, and Delete is unreachable
    today: VoiceOver announces N consecutive rows all named "Copy Entry, button". There is no way to hear which entry is which, and no accessibility action for removing one — the ✕ only exists while the pointer is physically over the row.
    apple: macOS list rows announce their *content* and expose row actions by name — Mail reads the sender and subject then offers "Delete" as a custom action; a Finder item reads its filename. Apple never labels a list of distinct items with one shared verb.
    impact: The clipboard tab — one of five, and the one whose whole value is picking the right item out of a list — is unusable non-visually. Choosing an entry becomes a coin flip that overwrites the pasteboard.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/ClipboardPane.swift:100-106: `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` immediately followed by `.accessibilityLabel(localized("Copy Entry"))`, which replaces the merged content label with a constant. The row's content is `SpoilerText(text: item.preview, …)` at :60-64. The remove button (:70-82, correctly labeled "Remove Entry") is inside `if hovering {` at :66 and is additionally swallowed by the `.combine`.
    fix: Label the row with its content and move the verb to the action name: `.accessibilityLabel(hidden ? localized("Hidden") : item.preview)`, `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isButton)`, `.accessibilityAction(named: localized("Copy Entry")) { clipboard.copy(item); flash($justCopied) }`, plus `.accessibilityAction(named: localized("Remove Entry")) { clipboard.remove(item) }` so deleting no longer depends on hover. Keep "Hidden" for covered rows — SpoilerField.swift:160 already establishes that privacy rule.
- [DISPUTED] (major/M) Shelf cards cannot be read, selected, opened or removed without a pointer
    today: VoiceOver would encounter a bare `Image(nsImage:)` and a `Text(item.name)` with no grouping, no role and no actions; the ✕ announces as "xmark.circle.fill" on the rare occasions hover has revealed it.
    apple: Finder icon-view items are one AX element per file: name as label, `AXRole` button, `AXSelected` reflecting selection, and Open/Copy/Show in Finder exposed as named actions. Apple never hides a destructive action behind hover with no alternative.
    impact: The Shelf — the AirDrop-style feature that most justifies the app over a plain media widget — has no non-pointer operation at all: files cannot be identified, selected, opened, or removed.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift:131-197 — `ShelfCard.body` has no `accessibilityElement`, no label, no `.isButton`, no `.isSelected`, despite tracking `isSelected` (:129) and drawing a checkmark for it (:171-178). Click, double-click and drag all live in an AppKit shim: Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfDragSource.swift:32-95 (`DragView: NSView`) which publishes no AX role or action. The remove ✕ at ShelfPane.swift:181-188 has no `accessibilityLabel` and is gated on `if isHovered` (:180). Only the `.contextMenu` at :191-197 is reachable — and its items are raw literals (`Button("Copy")`, `Button("Open")`), so they are also unlocalized.
    fix: On the `ShelfCard` root add `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)`, `.accessibilityLabel(item.name)`, `.accessibilityAddTraits(isSelected ? [.isButton, .isSelected] : .isButton)`, a default `.accessibilityAction { shelf.open(item) }`, and named actions mirroring the context menu (`Copy`, `Show in Finder`, `Remove from Shelf`) so they no longer depend on hover. Add `.accessibilityLabel(localized("Remove from Shelf"))` to the ✕ at :181 and localize the four context-menu literals while you are there.
    challenges: The accessibility half is exactly right: `grep -n accessibility Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift` returns zero matches for the whole file. `ShelfCard.body` (:131-200) has no accessibilityElement, no label, no `.isButton`, no `.isSelected`, despite `isSelected` at :129 and the checkmark overlay at :170-178; click/double-click/drag all live in Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfDragSource.swift `DragView: NSView` (:32-108), which overrides only mouseDown/mouseDragged/mouseUp and publishes no AX role or action; the ✕ at :181-188 is bare and gated on `if isHovered` at :180 (and its `.padding(4)` at :187 is applied *outside* `.buttonStyle(.plain)`, so it does not even enlarge the hit region). The localization sub-claim is false, though. | corrected: Drop the trailing "so they are also unlocalized". The context-menu literals at ShelfPane.swift:191-197 are localized: "Copy", "Open", "Show in Finder" and "Remove from Shelf" are all keys in both Resources/en.lproj/Localizable.strings (:31-34) and Resources/ru.lproj/Localizable.strings (:30-33), and Sources/IslaKit/App/Strings.swift:5-7 records the reason — "SwiftUI's `Text` localises string literals by itself, so views mostly need nothing." Scripts/test-localizations.sh enforces en/ru key parity over those same tables. Same for "Deselect" at ShelfPane.swift:101 (en:29 / ru:28). The accurate claim is: shelf cards have no accessibility identity — no element, label, button/selected traits or actions — and both the ✕ and the only reachable affordance (the context menu) require a pointer. ;; The gaps are real — ShelfPane.swift:121-197 `ShelfCard.body` has no `accessibilityElement`, label, `.isButton` or `.isSelected` despite tracking selection (:127, :169-178); the ✕ at :179-187 has no label and is gated on `if isHovered`; click/double-click/drag live in ShelfDragSource.swift `DragView: NSView` which publishes no AX action. But the headline contradicts its own evidence: `Text(item.name)` at :137-142 is a plain SwiftUI Text and is read, and the `.contextMenu` at :190-196 exposes Copy/Open/Show in Finder/Remove (reachable via VO-Shift-M). The parenthetical about those literals being unlocalized is wrong — SwiftUI localizes string literals through Bundle.main (documented at Sources/IslaKit/App/Strings.swift:5-6) and all four keys exist in Resources/ru.lproj/Localizable.strings:28-33. What is genuinely unreachable is multi-select and the labeled remove ✕, on a surface whose primary interaction (drag files out) is pointer-bound by nature. | corrected: minor — the card name is read and Copy/Open/Reveal/Remove are reachable through the context menu; what is missing is the button role, the selected state, and a label on the hover-gated ✕.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) Track changes, the sneak peek, copies and completed translations are announced only in pixels
    today: State changes that the design deliberately surfaces visually — a new track peeking out of the notch, a copy landing, a translation returning — have no non-visual counterpart.
    apple: Music posts an accessibility announcement on track change; the system volume/output HUD announces itself; Apple's guidance is that any transient visual confirmation (a checkmark, a toast, a peek) must be paired with an `announcementRequested` notification, and asynchronous results should be announced when they arrive.
    impact: With VoiceOver on, the app is mute where it is most expressive. A user who asked for the translation hotkey gets no signal that the answer arrived; a listener gets no signal that the song changed even though the island visibly said so.
    evidence: `rg 'NSAccessibility' Sources` → no matches; there is no `.announcementRequested` post anywhere. The peek is purely visual: NotchController.swift:1018-1045 `sneakPeek()` raises `vm.isPeeking` for `NotchMetrics.sneakPeekDuration` and lowers it. `compactAccessibilityLabel` (NotchContentView.swift:386-391) builds a perfectly good "Playing: title, artist" string but nothing notifies AX when it changes. `flash($justCopied)` (Confirmation.swift:10-15) confirms a copy purely by turning a glyph green for 1.1 s. Translation results land silently into `Text(translator.output)` (TranslatePane.swift:150).
    fix: Add a small `Announce.say(_:priority:)` helper wrapping `NSAccessibility.post(element: NSApp, notification: .announcementRequested, userInfo: [.announcement: text, .priority: NSAccessibilityPriorityLevel.medium.rawValue])`. Call it from `sneakPeek()` with `compactAccessibilityLabel`, from `MediaController` when `track.key` or `isPlaying` changes, from `flash(_:)`'s call sites with localized "Copied", and from `Translator` when `output` is set. All strings need `en.lproj`/`ru.lproj` entries to satisfy `test-localizations.sh`.
    challenges: Factually right: `rg 'NSAccessibility'` over Sources returns nothing and there is no `.announcementRequested`/AXAnnouncement post. NotchController.swift:1004-1044 `sneakPeek()` only raises and lowers `vm.isPeeking`; NotchContentView.swift:386-391 builds `compactAccessibilityLabel` with nothing notifying on change; Confirmation.swift:9-14 `flash()` is a 1.1 s visual tick; TranslatePane.swift:148-155 drops the result into a Text with no live region. But severity is inflated — no state is lost, nothing misbehaves, and every one of these states is re-readable on demand once the element is reachable. The one with real weight is the async translation completing silently (a status-message defect); a track-change announcement on every song would arguably be wrong anyway. | corrected: minor — no live-region/status announcements, most consequentially when an async translation returns and when a copy is confirmed; all affected states remain re-readable.
- [DISPUTED] (major/S) Icon-only buttons as small as ~9–10pt, well under the 28pt floor the repo itself already applied once
    today: Six distinct controls ship at 9–24pt. The fix applied to the clipboard ✕ was never generalized to the identical buttons in Translate, Shelf, the lyric stage, or the reveal eye.
    apple: macOS's practical minimum click target is 28×28pt; Apple's own small glyph buttons (Safari's tab close, Finder's sidebar disclosure) reserve a padded hit rectangle far larger than the drawn glyph.
    impact: Anyone with a tremor, a trackpad, or an imprecise pointing device misses these. Missing the Shelf ✕ selects the card; missing the clipboard ✕ (the one that was fixed) used to overwrite the pasteboard — the same class of failure is still live in the other five.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/TranslatePane.swift:63-68 — clear-source ✕ is a bare `Image(systemName: "xmark").font(.system(size: 10, …))` with `.buttonStyle(.plain)` and no `.frame`, so the target is roughly the 10pt glyph. Sources/IslaKit/UI/SpoilerField.swift:177-183 `RevealEye` — 9pt glyph, no frame. Sources/IslaKit/UI/Confirmation.swift:29-36 `CopyButton` — 10pt glyph, no frame. Sources/IslaKit/UI/LyricsStage.swift:405 and :417 — `.buttonStyle(NotchButtonStyle(size: 20))` for the ±0.25 s lyric-offset buttons. Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift:181-187 — 13pt glyph + 4pt padding ≈ 21pt. Rail tabs are 30 × `railIconHeight`, and NotchGeometry.swift:68-74 computes `min(24, ceiling)` → 24pt tall. `NotchButtonStyle`'s default is 26 (Theme.swift:97). The project already knows the rule: ClipboardPane.swift:74-79 grew one ✕ to `.frame(width: 22, height: 22)` with the comment "A 9pt glyph is a ~10pt target … missing it by two points overwrote what the user had copied."
    fix: Introduce one modifier — `func hitTarget(_ side: CGFloat = 28) -> some View { frame(width: side, height: side).contentShape(Rectangle()) }` — and apply it to TranslatePane.swift:63, SpoilerField.swift:178, Confirmation.swift:33 and ShelfPane.swift:181. Raise `NotchButtonStyle.size`'s default from 26 to 28 (Theme.swift:97) and the lyric-offset buttons from 20 to 24 with 2pt of surrounding padding. The rail's 24pt ceiling is a genuine geometry constraint, but `railSpacing` (NotchGeometry.swift:16) can be spent on `contentShape` padding so the *target* is 28 even where the drawn icon stays 24.
    challenges: Every measurement checks out. TranslatePane.swift:63-68 — `Image(systemName: "xmark")` at 10pt semibold inside a `.buttonStyle(.plain)` Button with no `.frame` and no `.contentShape`. SpoilerField.swift:177-183 `RevealEye` — 9pt glyph, no frame. Confirmation.swift:29-36 `CopyButton` — 10pt glyph, no frame. LyricsStage.swift:405 and :417 — both `.buttonStyle(NotchButtonStyle(size: 20))` for the ±0.25 s offset buttons (the Back button just above at :393 gets 24). ShelfPane.swift:181-187 — 13pt glyph, and the `.padding(4)` at :187 sits after `.buttonStyle(.plain)` so it is outside the button and the real target is ~13-16pt, worse than the claim's 21pt estimate. NotchContentView.swift:551 `.frame(width: 30, height: vm.geometry.railIconHeight)` with NotchGeometry.swift:68-73 `min(24, ceiling).rounded(.down)`. Theme.swift:97 default 26. ClipboardPane.swift:74-79 carries the cited comment and `.frame(width: 22, height: 22)`. Only the framing is wrong. | corrected: Strike "the 28pt floor the repo itself already applied once". The repo applied 22×22 pt, not 28 — ClipboardPane.swift:78 is `.frame(width: 22, height: 22)`, and no 28pt target rule appears anywhere in Sources, docs or checklist.md. Accurate version: six distinct controls ship with hit targets of roughly 9-24 pt, below any reasonable pointer-target floor and below the 22 pt the project already decided one of them needed after a mis-click overwrote the pasteboard (ClipboardPane.swift:74-79). ;; Every measurement is correct: TranslatePane.swift:62-68 clear ✕ is a 10pt `Image(systemName: "xmark")` with `.buttonStyle(.plain)` and no `.frame`; SpoilerField.swift:173-185 `RevealEye` is 9pt with no frame; Confirmation.swift:30-38 `CopyButton` is 10pt with no frame; LyricsStage.swift:405 and :417 use `NotchButtonStyle(size: 20)`; ShelfPane.swift:181-187 is a 13pt glyph + 4pt padding; NotchGeometry.swift:68-74 yields `min(24, ceiling)` and NotchContentView.swift:549 frames rail icons at `width: 30, height: railIconHeight`; ClipboardPane.swift:78 is the 22×22 fix with the quoted comment. But the standard invoked is invented. The repo applied a 22pt floor, not 28. And macOS has no 28pt minimum: Apple's 44×44pt figure is the touch guidance for iOS/iPadOS, the macOS HIG states no pointer-target minimum, and Apple's own AppKit control heights are mini 16 / small 20 / regular ~22-24 (the window traffic lights are 12pt). That makes the 20pt lyric-offset buttons, the 26pt `NotchButtonStyle` default and the 30×24 rail tabs ordinary macOS metrics rather than violations. | corrected: Four frame-less glyph buttons ship with a hit target the size of their glyph — TranslatePane's clear ✕ (~10pt), `RevealEye` (~9pt), `CopyButton` (~10pt) — below even AppKit's 16pt mini control, and the shelf ✕ is ~21pt. The repo already set its own 22×22 floor once, on the clipboard ✕, for the reason stated in ClipboardPane.swift:74-79; generalize that 22pt floor to the other three. Drop the 20pt lyric buttons, the 26pt button default and the 30×24 rail tabs from the finding — those match Apple's own small/regular control metrics, and there is no 28pt macOS floor to cite. ;; Sizes verified: TranslatePane.swift:62-68 bare 10pt `xmark` with `.buttonStyle(.plain)` and no frame; SpoilerField.swift:176-183 `RevealEye` 9pt, no frame; Confirmation.swift:28-36 `CopyButton` 10pt, no frame; LyricsStage.swift:404/416 `NotchButtonStyle(size: 20)`; NotchContentView.swift:551 rail icons 30 × `railIconHeight` where NotchGeometry.swift:68-74 yields ≤24. Two things inflate it. First, there is no 28pt floor here — the repo's own precedent is 22×22 (ClipboardPane.swift:73-79), and 28/44pt is touch guidance, not macOS pointer guidance (macOS ships 12pt traffic lights). Second, the fix that was applied had a destructive miss ('overwrote what the user had copied'); the remaining five all fail safe — missing the Translate ✕ lands in the TextEditor, missing the Shelf ✕ selects the card. Note the Shelf ✕ is smaller than claimed: ShelfPane.swift:179-187 puts `.padding(4)` outside the Button, so it does not extend the hit region (~13-15pt, not 21pt). | corrected: minor — five icon-only controls sit at 9-13pt with no expanded hit frame, costing repeat clicks for imprecise pointing; none has a destructive miss, and the one that did was already grown to 22pt.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) Unlabeled glyph buttons announce as raw SF Symbol identifiers; `.help` is a hint, not a name
    today: VoiceOver falls back to the symbol identifier — "xmark", "xmark.circle.fill", "eye.slash", "doc.on.doc" — and the source field announces as an unnamed text area. `.help()` populates the tooltip and the AX *help/hint*, which VoiceOver reads only after a pause and Voice Control does not match on.
    apple: Every Apple glyph button carries an explicit accessibility label distinct from its tooltip; Voice Control's "click Clear" works because the label is a real name, and text fields always announce a role plus a name.
    impact: Voice Control users cannot address these controls by name at all (only via the numbered-overlay fallback), and VoiceOver users hear internal asset identifiers instead of English — the app sounds unfinished exactly where the visual design is most polished.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/TranslatePane.swift:63-69 — `Image(systemName: "xmark")` inside a Button with no `accessibilityLabel` and no `.help`. Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift:181-188 — `"xmark.circle.fill"`, likewise bare. Sources/IslaKit/UI/SpoilerField.swift:177-184 `RevealEye` and Sources/IslaKit/UI/Confirmation.swift:29-38 `CopyButton` supply only `.help(…)`. Sources/IslaKit/UI/TranslatePane.swift:77 — the `TextEditor` has no `accessibilityLabel`; :164 `ProgressView()` has none either. (For contrast, MediaPane.swift:327-363 and LockScreenCard.swift:551-599 label everything.)
    fix: Add `.accessibilityLabel(localized("Clear Text"))` (TranslatePane.swift:68), `.accessibilityLabel(localized("Remove from Shelf"))` (ShelfPane.swift:186), `.accessibilityLabel(hidden ? localized("Show") : localized("Hide"))` alongside the existing `.help` (SpoilerField.swift:184), `.accessibilityLabel(localized("Copy"))` (Confirmation.swift:38), `.accessibilityLabel(localized("Text to Translate"))` on the `TextEditor`, and `ProgressView(localized("Translating…")).labelsHidden()`. Keep `.help` for the tooltip — the two are different channels.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) The lock card's seek and volume rails are undeclared to accessibility, unlike the island's scrubber
    today: On the lock card, playback position and *system output volume* can be neither read nor changed by anything but a pointer drag. The volume rail additionally changes a system-wide setting with no accessible affordance.
    apple: The system's own lock-screen player and Control Center expose position and volume as `AXSlider`s with a spoken value and increment/decrement actions.
    impact: Where the card is reachable, two of its four interactive regions are invisible to AX — and one of them is the machine's volume, the control most likely to be needed without looking at the screen.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/LockScreenCard.swift:470-505 `seekBar` and :509-539 `volumeBar` are `Capsule()` shapes inside a `GeometryReader` with a bare `DragGesture` — no `accessibilityElement`, label, value or adjustable action. The identical problem was fixed on the island: MediaPane.swift:284-300 declares `.accessibilityElement()`, `.accessibilityLabel(localized("Playback Position"))`, `.accessibilityValue("%@ of %@")` and `.accessibilityAdjustableAction` seeking in 5% steps, with the comment "A capsule with a drag gesture is nothing at all to assistive tech."
    fix: Copy the MediaPane pattern verbatim onto both rails: for `seekBar`, label "Playback Position", value `formatTime`-based, `accessibilityAdjustableAction` calling `media.seek(to:)` in 5% steps; for `volumeBar`, label `localized("Volume")`, value as a percentage, adjustable action calling `SystemVolume.set(_:)` in 6.25% steps (matching the hardware key granularity). Sharing one `AdjustableRail` view between MediaPane and LockScreenCard would also stop the two surfaces drifting apart again, the way `LyricRow` already prevents for lyrics.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) Two-press destructive confirmations disarm after 3 s, and the armed state is never announced
    today: Press once to arm, press again within 3 seconds or the arming lapses silently. Nothing announces the transition to "Clear Everything?" or "Quit Isla?".
    apple: Apple confirms destructive actions with an alert that waits indefinitely; where a timed affordance is unavoidable, the HIG and WCAG 2.2.1 both require the limit be adjustable or extendable, and Apple's own timed UI extends when VoiceOver or Switch Control is active.
    impact: A VoiceOver user must navigate to the button, press, wait for the label change they were never told about, and press again — inside 3 seconds. In practice these five destructive actions, including Quit, are unreachable non-visually; the user presses repeatedly and either nothing happens or the wrong thing does.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/Confirmation.swift:66-70 (`ConfirmTextButton`) and :104-108 (`ConfirmRow`): `disarm = Task { try? await Task.sleep(for: .seconds(3)); … armed = false }`. The label change is exposed only passively via `.accessibilityLabel(armed ? armedTitle : title)` (:78, :128) — nothing posts a change notification, so a VoiceOver user is not told the button re-armed. Used by Clear Shelf (ShelfPane.swift:106-109), Clear Clipboard (ClipboardPane.swift:36-39), Clear Screenshots Folder, Clear Lyrics Cache and Quit (SettingsPane.swift:110-121, :185-193, :249-255).
    fix: Expose the confirmation as an explicit second action rather than a race: add `.accessibilityAction(named: armedTitle) { action() }` so assistive tech can commit directly, post an announcement when `armed` flips true, and multiply the 3-second window (or drop the timer entirely) when `NSWorkspace.shared.isVoiceOverEnabled` or `isSwitchControlEnabled` is set. Keep the visual two-press behavior unchanged for pointer users — the design rationale at Confirmation.swift:43-49 is sound.

## system-integration
verdict: The window-server plumbing here is better than almost anything in this category: `NotchController.install()` observes screen-parameter changes, active-space changes, display sleep/wake and lock/unlock and has a real repair path for each (NotchController.swift:47-136); `NotchGeometry.current()` survives the empty-`NSScreen.screens` window during display renegotiation and refuses to believe a momentarily-zero `safeAreaInsets` (NotchGeometry.swift:98-191); the panel excludes itself from screen capture (NotchPanel.swift:160-168), keeps a cursor tracking area alive on a never-key window (NotchRootView.swift:100-122), accepts incoming file *promises* and not just URLs (NotchRootView.swift:73-77, 200-241), and latches outgoing drags so the panel cannot fold out from under a drag session (ShelfDragSource.swift:105-134). Universal Clipboard arrivals are explicitly reasoned about (NotchStores.swift:41-47). That is genuinely Apple-grade window behavior, and I found nothing wrong with it. Where it is not yet a native citizen is at every seam the system offers *to* apps rather than the one it takes from them: there is not a single `AppIntent` in 14k lines, so it is invisible to Shortcuts, Spotlight and Focus Filters; the open panel accepts no keyboard events at all on four of its five tabs, so the hotkey opens a surface a keyboard user then cannot use; the two global shortcuts are hardcoded, undiscoverable inside the app, and fail silently on conflict; the island is permanently pinned to the built-in notched display with no setting; it has no notion of a fullscreen app or Mission Control; there is no update mechanism at all; and the shipped artifact is unsigned, with README instructions for a Gatekeeper bypass Apple removed in the app's own minimum OS. None of these are hard problems — they are the difference between "an extremely well-built window" and "a Mac app."
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) Zero App Intents: invisible to Shortcuts, Spotlight, Focus Filters and every automation surface Apple ships
    today: Nothing about Isla can be scripted, automated or triggered by anything except a pointer on the notch or the two fixed key combinations. There is no way to say "open the Shelf", "translate this", "add this file to the Shelf", "next track" or "what's playing" from Shortcuts, a Stream Deck, Raycast, Alfred, AppleScript, or a Focus Filter.
    apple: Since macOS 13 Apple's own accessory-class apps expose App Intents; App Shortcuts surface automatically in Spotlight and the Shortcuts app with no user setup, and `SetFocusFilterIntent` lets a Focus mode reconfigure an app. Apple's macOS HIG treats automation as a baseline expectation for utilities, not a pro feature.
    impact: A power user — exactly the person who buys a $25 notch app — discovers the app cannot participate in their existing automation at all. It also forfeits the cheapest answer to the discoverability and keyboard problems below: intents would give "Open Isla", "Translate Clipboard" and "Quit Isla" as Spotlight-searchable actions with user-assignable shortcuts, for free.
    evidence: `rg -n "AppIntent|import AppIntents|ShortcutsLink|NSUserActivity" Sources/` returns nothing across all 66 files. The app's entire external command surface is two hardcoded Carbon hot keys (AppDelegate.swift:34-55), one Services entry (`NSServices` → `translateSelection`, Scripts/bundle.sh:54-61 and AppDelegate.swift:74-81), and one OAuth URL scheme that only accepts Spotify callbacks: `for url in urls where url.scheme == "isla" { SpotifyAccount.shared.handleCallback(url) }` (AppDelegate.swift:11-15) — any other `isla://` URL is silently dropped.
    fix: Add `Sources/IslaKit/Intents/` with `@MainActor` AppIntents calling the verbs that already exist in the right shape: `OpenIslaIntent` → `NotchController.toggle()`, `TranslateIntent(text:)` → `NotchController.translate(_:)`, `AddToShelfIntent(files:)` → `ShelfStore.add(_:)`, `PlaybackIntent(action:)` → the existing `MediaController` transport, `NowPlayingIntent` returning title/artist as an `IntentResult` value. Register them in an `AppShortcutsProvider` so they appear in Spotlight with no setup. Add `IslaFocusFilter: SetFocusFilterIntent` toggling `sneakPeekEnabled`/`showOnLockScreenEnabled` per Focus mode. No new entitlement is needed and Scripts/bundle.sh needs no change — App Intents metadata is emitted into the binary by SwiftPM.
    challenges: Facts hold: `rg AppIntent|NSUserActivity Sources/` is empty; the whole external surface is two Carbon hot keys (Sources/IslaKit/App/AppDelegate.swift:34-55), one Services entry (Scripts/bundle.sh:54-61 + AppDelegate.swift:74-81) and a URL scheme that drops everything but the OAuth callback (AppDelegate.swift:11-15, guarded again at SpotifyAccount.swift:135). But severity is inflated: nothing is broken, no user is blocked, and the examples given are mostly served elsewhere — 'next track' / 'what's playing' already exist as system Music/Spotify intents, so the real gap is only Shelf/Clipboard/Translate automation and Focus Filters, which a small power-user slice would name as a missing feature, not a complaint about the app misbehaving. It is a worth-paying differentiator to build, not a defect users hit. | corrected: minor — no App Intents means no Shortcuts/Focus-Filter/Spotlight-action surface for Shelf, Clipboard and Translate; a differentiator opportunity, not a user-facing defect
- [DISPUTED] (major/M) ⌥⌘I opens a panel that accepts no keyboard input on four of its five tabs
    today: Press ⌥⌘I: the panel opens on Music (`vm.select(.media)`, NotchController.swift:511 and 747). From there Tab does nothing, arrow keys do nothing, Space does not play/pause, Escape does not close it, ⌘Q does not quit, ⌘, does nothing. Changing tab, hitting a transport button or reaching Settings → Quit all require the mouse. The only exit is pressing ⌥⌘I again.
    apple: Full Keyboard Access is a system-wide macOS setting Apple expects every app to honor; the HIG requires that anything reachable by pointer be reachable by keyboard, and every Apple accessory surface (Control Center, Notification Center, Spotlight, menu-bar extras) is navigable with Tab/arrows/Return and dismissible with Escape. Apple's own non-activating panels take key focus on demand precisely so this works.
    impact: A keyboard-driven or VoiceOver user can open the app and then do nothing with it. The `accessibilityLabel`s that already exist throughout MediaPane, LockScreenCard, LyricsStage and ClipboardPane are unreachable because focus never enters the window. GlobalHotKey.swift:12-14 states the intent — "anything that cannot be opened from the keyboard cannot be reached by assistive tech either" — but opening is where it stops.
    evidence: `NotchPanel.canBecomeKey` is `acceptsKeyboard` (NotchPanel.swift:28), which starts `false` (NotchPanel.swift:12) and is only set from `setKeyboard(_:)` (NotchController.swift:869-878) driven by `vm.$wantsKeyboard` (NotchController.swift:827-832), which is only raised for a tab where `needsKeyboard` is true — and `var needsKeyboard: Bool { self == .translate }` (NotchViewModel.swift:32). All key handling (Escape, ⌘A/X/C/V/Z) lives in `NotchPanel.sendEvent` (NotchPanel.swift:81-101), which a non-key window of an inactive app never receives. `rg -n "keyboardShortcut|focusable|FocusState|onKeyPress" Sources/IslaKit/UI` returns zero hits.
    fix: Make `wantsKeyboard` a property of how the panel was opened rather than of which tab is showing: in `NotchController.toggle()` (and `islandClicked()` if desired) set `vm.wantsKeyboard = true` when the open came from the hotkey, so `canBecomeKey` is true and `sendEvent` starts receiving keys. Then add `FocusState`-driven focus in `NotchContentView`: Tab cycles rail → pane controls, ←/→ change tab (the rail is already an ordered `Tab.leftRail`), Return activates, Escape routes to the existing `panel.onEscape`. Add ⌘Q and ⌘W to `NotchPanel.editingAction(for:)`'s switch (NotchPanel.swift:103-116) mapping to `NSApp.terminate` and close — the mechanism for menu-less key equivalents is already built and documented there.
    challenges: Core defect confirmed: NotchPanel.swift:28 `override var canBecomeKey: Bool { acceptsKeyboard }`, `acceptsKeyboard = false` at NotchPanel.swift:12, set only from `setKeyboard(_:)` (NotchController.swift:869-878) driven by `vm.$wantsKeyboard` (NotchController.swift:827-832), raised only via `select` when `needsKeyboard` — and `var needsKeyboard: Bool { self == .translate }` (NotchViewModel.swift:32, asserted by TabContractTests.swift:22). All key handling does live in `NotchPanel.sendEvent` (NotchPanel.swift:81-101), unreachable for a non-key window. But three supporting details are wrong. (1) The grep evidence is false: `rg -n "keyboardShortcut|focusable|FocusState|onKeyPress" Sources/IslaKit/UI` returns two hits — TranslatePane.swift:17 `@FocusState private var focused` and TranslatePane.swift:104 `.onKeyPress(.escape)`. Both are Translate-only, so the conclusion survives. (2) ⌥⌘I does not open on Music: the hotkey runs `toggle()` (NotchController.swift:534-545), which sets `isPinnedOpen` and `setOpen` and never calls `select` — `vm.select(.media)` at line 511 is the click path and at line 747 the hover path, so ⌥⌘I opens on whatever tab was last used. (3) ⌥⌘I is not the only exit: `updatePinnedClickMonitor` (NotchController.swift:550-571) installs a global click monitor that closes a pinned panel on any click outside, and hovering onto/off the panel drops the pin (line 736) and hands it back to the pointer rule. | corrected: ⌥⌘I opens the panel on whatever tab was last selected, and only the Translate tab ever accepts keystrokes — on the other four, Tab/arrows/Space/Escape/⌘Q/⌘, all go to the app underneath because `canBecomeKey` is false. Closing without the mouse still requires ⌥⌘I again (a click outside or a hover-and-leave also close it, but both are mouse gestures). Sources/IslaKit/UI does contain `@FocusState` and `.onKeyPress(.escape)`, but only inside TranslatePane. ;; The core is right: NotchPanel.canBecomeKey is `acceptsKeyboard` (Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchPanel.swift:28) starting false (NotchPanel.swift:12), set only from setKeyboard (NotchController.swift:869-878) driven by `vm.$wantsKeyboard` (NotchController.swift:827-832), raised only where `needsKeyboard` is true — and `var needsKeyboard: Bool { self == .translate }` (Sources/IslaKit/Model/NotchViewModel.swift:32). With no main menu on an .accessory app, ⌘Q/⌘, are dead, and Escape lives in NotchPanel.sendEvent (NotchPanel.swift:81-101), unreachable for a non-key window. Apple's own comparable surfaces (Control Center, Notification Center, Spotlight) are all fully keyboard-navigable, so the bar is real. But three details are wrong. (1) ⌥⌘I does NOT open on Music: `toggle()` (NotchController.swift:534-545) never calls `select(.media)`; line 511 is the click path `islandClicked()`, and line 747 sits behind `if inside, !NotchViewModel.opensOnHoverEnabled { return }` (NotchController.swift:733) and is only reached via a pointer sample — and `pointer.setInside` merely sets a flag, it does not fire `onChange` (Sources/IslaKit/Notch/PointerWatcher.swift:155-159). ⌥⌘I opens on whatever tab was last showing, which is arguably worse. (2) '⌥⌘I again' is not the only exit — a left or right click in any other app closes it via the global pinned-click monitor (NotchController.swift:550-571). (3) The cited `rg` on Sources/IslaKit/UI is false: TranslatePane.swift:17 has @FocusState and TranslatePane.swift:104 has .onKeyPress(.escape). | corrected: On the four non-Translate tabs the panel takes no keyboard input at all: Tab, arrows, Space, Escape, ⌘Q and ⌘, all do nothing, because canBecomeKey is false outside Translate (NotchPanel.swift:28, NotchViewModel.swift:32) and an .accessory app has no main menu to carry the standard equivalents. ⌥⌘I reopens the panel on whatever tab was last used — not on Music — and the only ways out are ⌥⌘I again or a click in another app (NotchController.swift:550-571); every tab change, transport button and Settings→Quit still requires the mouse.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The two global shortcuts are hardcoded, never shown in the UI, and fail silently when another app owns the combination
    today: If Xcode, a window manager or any other app already owns ⌥⌘I, registration fails, nothing is logged, and the hotkey never works for the life of the install. Even when it does work, a user who never read the GitHub README cannot learn the shortcuts exist: the app has no Dock icon, no menu-bar item, no Help menu and no onboarding.
    apple: Apple puts every user-facing shortcut in System Settings → Keyboard → Shortcuts, always visible and always rebindable, and shows the combination next to the command that invokes it. Every paid competitor (NotchNook, Alcove, MediaMate) ships a shortcut recorder in settings.
    impact: Two of the app's four entry points are invisible and one can be permanently dead with no diagnosis. Since Settings is only reachable by clicking the notch, the shortcut a user might rely on is one they must be told about out-of-band.
    evidence: `GlobalHotKey.defaultKeyCode = kVK_ANSI_I`, `defaultModifiers = optionKey|cmdKey`, `translateKeyCode = kVK_ANSI_T` are `static let` constants (GlobalHotKey.swift:106-113). `init?` returns nil when registration fails: `guard registered == noErr, reference != nil else { return nil }` (GlobalHotKey.swift:82). `AppDelegate` assigns the optional and never checks it (AppDelegate.swift:34-39 and 49-55). No shortcut appears anywhere in the UI — `grep -n -i "⌘\|shortcut\|hotkey" Resources/en.lproj/Localizable.strings` returns nothing, and the 524-line `SettingsPane` has no shortcut row. There is no defaults key for a shortcut in NotchViewModel.swift:231-367.
    fix: Add a Shortcuts section to `SettingsPane` with two recorder rows bound to new defaults keys (`openPanelShortcut`, `translateShortcut`) stored as keyCode+modifiers, re-registering through the existing `GlobalHotKey.unregister()` + re-init path (`AppDelegate.refreshPointerTuning` is the precedent for a settings-driven live refresh). When `GlobalHotKey(...)` returns nil, surface it in that row as "⌥⌘I is already in use" instead of dropping it — the row is the one place a user can act on it. Ship the current combinations as defaults so nothing changes for existing users.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/L) The island lives only on the notched built-in display and cannot be moved
    today: A MacBook Pro with an external monitor and the lid open always gets the island on the laptop panel, never on the external display, whichever one the user is working on. The code deliberately rejects `NSScreen.main` (correctly — the panel is never key), but the replacement is "the notched one, always" rather than "the one the user is on."
    apple: macOS puts the menu bar on every display and moves the active menu bar to whichever display holds the focused window. Control Center, Notification Center and Spotlight all appear on the display the user is working on, not a fixed one.
    impact: The most common pro setup — laptop open beneath a large external display — puts the island in peripheral vision on the smaller screen while the user works on the big one. Sneak peeks, the drop shelf and translate results all appear where they are not looking. Competitors ship a display picker or an all-displays mode, and a user who has one will not give it up.
    evidence: `NotchGeometry.current()` picks `NSScreen.screens.first(where: { $0.safeAreaInsets.top > 0 }) ?? NSScreen.screens.first(where: { remembered notch matches }) ?? NSScreen.screens.first` (NotchGeometry.swift:138-142). There is no user preference — the settings key list (NotchViewModel.swift:231-367) has no display key and `SettingsPane` has no display row. Nothing consults `NSEvent.mouseLocation`'s screen or `NSWorkspace.didActivateApplicationNotification` to follow the user.
    fix: Add a `targetDisplay` setting (built-in / follow the pointer / all displays) and make `NotchGeometry.current()` take the choice as a parameter instead of hardcoding the search order. "Follow the pointer" re-runs the existing `rebuild()` when `NSEvent.mouseLocation`'s screen changes — the pointer sampler already ticks at 8 Hz idle and knows the location (PointerWatcher.swift:99-113), and `rebuild()` already restores tab and open state (NotchController.swift:575-598), so this is one comparison per tick. "All displays" is the larger change (NotchController would own a keyed dictionary of panel+geometry rather than one), which is why this is L — ship the picker first.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The shipped build is ad-hoc signed and unnotarized, and the README's Gatekeeper workaround no longer exists on the app's own minimum macOS
    today: A downloaded Isla.dmg produces an app macOS refuses to open ("Apple could not verify Isla is free of malware"), and the documented recovery is a Terminal command that strips quarantine. The alternative the README offers — Control-click → Open — was removed by Apple in macOS 15 Sequoia, which is this app's minimum OS, so the fallback instruction is wrong for every supported user.
    apple: Apple's model is Developer ID signing plus notarization plus stapling, after which the app opens on first double-click with no dialog and no Terminal. On macOS 15 the only override is System Settings → Privacy & Security → Open Anyway after a first blocked launch.
    impact: The first thing a prospective user must do is paste a command that disables a macOS security check — for an app that loads a dylib into `/usr/bin/perl` and reads their clipboard. That is a conversion killer against a $25 competitor that just opens, and it is the hardest blocker to the worth-paying bar. It also silently degrades the app: `TokenStore` falls back from the keychain to an on-disk 0600 file precisely because the build is unsigned (README.md:80-86, Resources/Isla.entitlements:22-31).
    evidence: `SIGN_IDENTITY="${DEVELOPER_ID_APPLICATION:--}"` — ad-hoc (`-`) is the default (Scripts/bundle.sh:113 and 152-158). README.md:21-32 tells users to run `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/Isla.app` and adds "the command above, or right-click → Open, is the way in." `LSMinimumSystemVersion` is 15.0 (Scripts/bundle.sh:65, asserted by Scripts/test-identity.sh:13). The notarization machinery exists and is correct — `xcrun notarytool submit` plus `stapler staple`/`validate` for both app and DMG (Scripts/release.sh:62-94) — it simply requires a Developer ID that has not been used.
    fix: Enroll in the Apple Developer Program and run the existing `Scripts/release.sh` path with `DEVELOPER_ID_APPLICATION` and notarytool credentials set — the script, the timestamping, the inside-out nested signing of `libislamedia.dylib` and the stapling are already written and correct. Until then, fix README.md:31-32 to drop the Control-click advice and give the macOS 15 route (open once, then System Settings → Privacy & Security → Open Anyway), and promote the Developer ID gate from a manual checklist line to a hard blocker in checklist.md's release verdict.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) No update mechanism of any kind — a user who installs today stays on that build forever
    today: There is no version check, no "Check for Updates" action, and no notification when a new build exists. The user must remember the repo, check it, download, strip quarantine and drag again.
    apple: Apple's answer is the App Store's automatic updates; for direct-download Mac apps the settled convention is Sparkle with a signed appcast, which every paid competitor here ships. Apple's HIG makes keeping software current the app's responsibility, not the user's.
    impact: Security fixes, media-helper repairs after a macOS point release (the MediaRemote path is explicitly fragile — it broke for everyone in 15.4), and the SkyLight lock-screen SPI breaking on a macOS update all become silent, permanent regressions for anyone who installed once. Every bug report you receive will be against a build you shipped months ago.
    evidence: `rg -n -i "Sparkle|appcast|updateCheck|SUFeed" .` (excluding build dirs) returns nothing. The only network clients are the lyrics providers (LyricsStore.swift), the Spotify PKCE flow (SpotifyAccount.swift) and one artwork fetch (PlayerBridge.swift:247). Distribution is a hand-built DMG (Scripts/dmg.sh) uploaded to GitHub releases (README.md:18-19). `SettingsPane`'s Application section offers only About and Quit (SettingsPane.swift:243-254).
    fix: Add Sparkle 2 as a SwiftPM dependency, an `SUFeedURL`/`SUPublicEDKey` pair to the Info.plist heredoc (Scripts/bundle.sh:36-74), a "Check for Updates…" `actionRow` in `SettingsPane`'s Application section beside About, and appcast generation plus signing in `Scripts/release.sh` after stapling. Sparkle needs a signed, notarized app to be useful, so this lands with the Gatekeeper fix. Also make `CFBundleVersion` a monotonically increasing build number distinct from `CFBundleShortVersionString` — they are the same `$VERSION` today (Scripts/bundle.sh:63-64) and Sparkle compares `CFBundleVersion`.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The island never gets out of the way: no notion of a fullscreen app, Mission Control, or a hidden menu bar
    today: With a fullscreen app frontmost the island keeps drawing at the top of the screen, and a track change fires a sneak peek that expands over it. On a synthetic notch (any external display) `collapsedDepth` is 8 (NotchGeometry.swift:283) and `applyActiveRect` still claims a `bodySize.width × 8` click region at the very top of the screen (NotchController.swift:1080 and 1101-1103), so a click aimed at a fullscreen video player's top edge opens the panel instead. During a Mission Control gesture the panel is not withdrawn.
    apple: macOS hides the menu bar entirely in fullscreen until the pointer is thrown at the top edge, and hides it during Mission Control. Apple's model is that chrome yields to full-screen content; nothing but the system's own HUD stays over a fullscreen app uninvited.
    impact: Watching a fullscreen video on an external display, the pill sits on top of the picture and a track change animates over it while the top strip of the video eats clicks.
