But 'forever, even on a Mac nobody has touched for hours' is false: pointer.stop() is called on screensDidSleepNotification (NotchController.swift:77) and on both branches of screenLocked (:179 and :197), so an untouched Mac whose display has slept, or one that is locked, is sampling nothing - the claim's own claim-1 evidence says so. The idle timer also sets tolerance = interval/2 (PointerWatcher.swift:110), so macOS coalesces those fires, which is Apple's prescribed mitigation. | corrected: While the display is awake and the Mac unlocked, the pointer sampler never drops below 8 Hz no matter how long the cursor sits still, because tick() is also what toggles panel.ignoresMouseEvents. It does stop entirely on display sleep and on lock, so the 'idle all night' case is already covered; the residue is a screen-awake-but-idle Mac (video playback, display sleep set to Never), and the fires carry a 62 ms tolerance so the system can coalesce them. ;; The mechanism and the severity are right, the scenario is not. NotchMetrics.swift:32-34 (fastPointerInterval 1/60, idlePointerInterval 1/8, restThreshold 3.0) and PointerWatcher.updateRate/schedule (PointerWatcher.swift:99-113, 127-144) confirm the sampler floors at 8 Hz and never stops on its own, and the reason it cannot stop is right too — tick() is what drives ignoresMouseEvents via onInteractiveChange (PointerWatcher.swift:183 → NotchController.swift:759). But "forever, on a machine whose cursor has not moved since last night" is refuted: that machine's display is asleep, and the screensDidSleepNotification handler calls pointer.stop() outright (NotchController.swift:70-78). The 8 Hz floor runs only while the screen is awake, and its timer carries tolerance = interval / 2 (PointerWatcher.swift:110) so the wake-ups coalesce. Each tick is one NSEvent.mouseLocation read plus two rect containment tests. | corrected: polish — the pointer sampler floors at 8 Hz and never stops on its own because tick() is also the only thing that flips ignoresMouseEvents (PointerWatcher.swift:183, NotchController.swift:759); it does stop entirely on display sleep (NotchController.swift:70-78), so this is 8 coalescable wake-ups a second only while the screen is on.

## interaction
verdict: The plumbing under input is genuinely excellent — `PointerWatcher` is a sampled, hysteresis-aware, rate-adaptive pointer model that solves problems most Mac apps never notice (non-activating panels get no hover events, tracking areas die on rebuild, drags out of the shelf must hold the panel open), and `NotchRootView` handles file promises, drag re-entry, and cursor claiming correctly. But the input vocabulary sitting on top of it is one gesture wide. The island is click-only: no scroll, no swipe, no pinch, no two-finger, no long-press, no right-click. Apple's Dynamic Island is gesture-first (drag down to expand, swipe up to dismiss), and every paid competitor ships swipe-to-skip and scroll-to-volume — the repo's own battle plan lists it as a table stake with the physics already specified, and it was never built. Below that headline gap, four correctness issues bite: Escape does not reach the panel on four of five tabs (the panel is never key), the clickable region is decided by a polling timer so a fast flick-and-click can be delivered to the app underneath, two-thirds of the drawn island is dead on any display without a physical notch, and the drop target is the island strip only with no invitation when a drag starts. Direct manipulation is also thin where it exists: the scrubber is a commit-on-release drag with no live seek, no precision falloff, no rubber-band, and no detent. This dimension is the single largest gap between "beautifully engineered hover machinery" and "feels like Apple built it."
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/M) The island is click-only: no scroll, swipe, pinch, or two-finger gesture anywhere
    today: Every interaction with the compact island is a click, and the click has exactly one meaning: open the panel. `NotchContentView.swift:214-225` documents the deliberate removal of the last non-click affordance ("The compact island has no controls at all"). Scrolling over the island does nothing. Swiping horizontally does nothing. There is no way to skip a track, change volume, or dismiss the island without opening the panel first and aiming at a 30pt button.
    apple: Apple's Dynamic Island is gesture-first: drag down on the compact island to expand it, swipe up on the expanded island to dismiss, and the expansion tracks the finger one-to-one with rubber-banding at the limits rather than snapping on release. macOS's own notch-adjacent surfaces (Control Center sliders, the volume/brightness HUD) all accept scroll-wheel and two-finger swipe on the affordance itself. Every paid app in this category ships the same vocabulary — Alcove swipe-to-skip since 1.2.2, NotchNook track swiping, MediaMate and DynamicLake scroll-over-notch for volume.
    impact: Someone coming from a $25 NotchNook or a $15 Alcove loses the gesture they use dozens of times a day: two fingers across the notch to skip a track, or up/down to change volume, without moving their hand or opening anything. Isla forces click → wait for the open spring → aim at a 30pt transport button → move the pointer away → wait 320ms for the collapse. That is four interactions where the competition has one flick, and it is the first thing a reviewer comparing the two will notice.
    evidence: The entire gesture surface of the collapsed island is `NotchContentView.swift:114-129` — a single `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 0)` used only to emulate press-highlight-and-commit (`onEnded` → `vm.onIslandClick?()`), plus `NotchContentView.swift:42` `.onTapGesture { vm.onIslandClick?() }` on the locked pill. A full-tree grep for gesture types returns only `DragGesture` (3 scrubber/volume sites), `onTapGesture` (4 sites), and one `contextMenu` — no `MagnifyGesture`, `RotationGesture`, `LongPressGesture`, `SpatialTapGesture`, `simultaneousGesture`, or `highPriorityGesture` anywhere in `Sources/`. Scroll-wheel/two-finger input is already wired and proven to work on this non-activating panel (`UI/ScrollWheelWatch.swift:28` `NSEvent.addLocalMonitorForEvents(matching: .scrollWheel)`, which distinguishes real trackpad phases at `:33`) but is consumed at exactly one call site: `LyricsStage.swift:269`. The project's own research doc names the gap: `docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:34` — "Swipe gestures on the notch (skip, dismiss) | Alcove (swipe-to-skip v1.2.2) + NotchNook (track swiping) | **M**" — and `:85` already specifies the physics ("~10pt hysteresis before committing, one-to-one tracking during the drag, momentum projection to pick the endpoint, rubber-banding at the pill's edges").
    fix: Add a `GestureLayer` on the collapsed shell in `NotchContentView.shell`, built on the two mechanisms already present. (1) Reuse `onScrollWheel` (extend `ScrollWheelWatch` to pass the `NSEvent` through instead of `()`): accumulate `scrollingDeltaX` with ~10pt hysteresis and `momentumPhase == .none` gating → `media.next()`/`media.previous()`, one commit per gesture with a re-arm on `phase == .ended`; accumulate `scrollingDeltaY` → `SystemVolume.set` with a transient level readout in the pill (Settings toggle, default on for skip, off for volume). (2) Add a second `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 10)` to the existing gesture at `NotchContentView.swift:114` as an `.exclusively(before:)` pair, so a horizontal drag past 10pt becomes a track change (with the pill translating one-to-one and rubber-banding via `min(abs(dx), 24) * sign` past the commit threshold) while a vertical drag downward past 10pt opens the panel with the drag's velocity projected into `Theme.open`'s spring initial velocity — which is precisely the case `docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:84` reserves the 0.82 damping for. Fire `Haptics.levelChange()` on `.drawCompleted` at each commit, matching the existing `Services/Haptics.swift` doctrine.
    challenges: Facts hold: the only gesture on the collapsed island is `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 0)` used as press/commit at Sources/IslaKit/UI/NotchContentView.swift:114-129 (plus the locked pill's `.onTapGesture` at :42); a whole-tree grep returns only DragGesture (NotchContentView:115, MediaPane:260, LockScreenCard:481, :522), 4 `onTapGesture` and 1 `contextMenu` — no Magnify/Rotation/LongPress/simultaneous/highPriority gesture anywhere in Sources/. Scroll-wheel plumbing exists and works on the non-activating panel (UI/ScrollWheelWatch.swift:28) but has exactly one call site, LyricsStage.swift:269. The wing's play/pause tap was deliberately removed with a documented rationale (NotchContentView.swift:214-225). But severity is inflated: nothing here stops a user from using or trusting the app — every function is reachable via click-to-open, and the compact island is honest about having no controls (it draws none). This is a competitive feature gap (Alcove swipe-to-skip, NotchNook track swiping — named in the project's own docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:34,:85), i.e. a worth-paying-bar item, not a blocker. | corrected: major — the island has no gesture layer at all, which is a real gap against paid competitors that ship swipe-to-skip/dismiss, but the app is fully usable without it
- [DISPUTED] (major/M) The scrubber never seeks until you let go, and has no precision, rubber-band, or detent
    today: Pressing and dragging on the progress bar moves a white capsule and re-labels the elapsed time, but the audio keeps playing from where it was; the seek fires once, on mouse-up. Precision is fixed at `duration / barWidth` — on a 4-minute track in a 560pt panel the bar is roughly 420pt, so one pixel is 0.57s and there is no way to land on a specific lyric line. Dragging past either end simply clamps with no compression or resistance. Nothing is felt at 0:00, at the end, or when crossing the current position.
    apple: macOS Music, QuickTime Player, and Podcasts all scrub live: the audio follows the playhead continuously during the drag. iOS Music adds variable-speed scrubbing — pull away from the bar and the same horizontal travel maps to a fraction of the timeline, with a haptic and a visible precision label at each step — and rubber-bands with compression at both ends. Apple's haptic guidance reserves `.alignment` for "an object finding its place," which the app's own `Services/Haptics.swift:31-33` quotes; a scrub snapping to 0:00 or to the live position is exactly that case.
    impact: Scrubbing is guesswork: you drag, hear nothing change, release, and only then find out whether you landed on the chorus. On a long podcast or a 10-minute track it is unusable for anything finer than "roughly here" — which is the one thing the app's whole precision-position engine (`MediaController.seek`, `pendingSeek`, the Spotify clock correction) exists to make possible. Next to Alcove, which has iterated on seeker accuracy across three releases and rubber-bands its HUD, this reads as the cheaper control.
    evidence: `UI/MediaPane.swift:259-277`: `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 0)` whose `onChanged` only writes local state (`scrubbing = min(max(value.location.x / width, 0), 1)`) and whose `onEnded` is the sole caller of `media.seek(to: media.duration * target)`. The lock card is identical — `UI/LockScreenCard.swift:480-500`. `value.translation`, `value.velocity`, and `value.startLocation` are never read, so the gesture cannot know how far the pointer moved off-axis or how fast it is travelling. `UI/ScrubPreview.swift:10-21` is a pure `x/width` clamp with no precision term. There is no `Haptics` call on either bar — the only three sites in the app are `LyricsStage.swift:369`, `LockScreenCard.swift:315`, and `LockScreenCard.swift:417`. The volume bar at `LockScreenCard.swift:521-532` does write live (`SystemVolume.set(next)` inside `onChanged`), so the two bars in the same card behave differently.
    fix: In `MediaPane.scrubber` and `LockScreenCard.seekBar`: (1) seek live — throttle `onChanged` to ~10Hz and call `media.seek(to:)` there; `MediaController.seek` already anchors optimistically (`Services/MediaController.swift:503-505` `setAnchor(clamped)` + `pendingSeek`), so the bar will not yank back. (2) Add precision falloff: compute `offAxis = abs(value.location.y - value.startLocation.y)` and scale the horizontal delta by `1.0 / max(1, offAxis / 20)` clamped to a 1/8 floor, showing the factor beside the elapsed label. (3) Rubber-band past the ends with ~0.45 compression, snapping back on release. (4) Fire `Haptics.alignment()` when the drag crosses `media.position` or either endpoint and `Haptics.levelChange()` on commit — both already `.drawCompleted`-timed. Give `ScrubPreview` a `fraction(x:width:duration:precision:)` overload so the new math stays covered by `ScrubPreviewTests`.
    challenges: The code half is exact. MediaPane.swift:259-277 — onChanged writes only `scrubbing = min(max(value.location.x / width, 0), 1)`, onEnded is the sole caller of `media.seek(to: media.duration * target)`. LockScreenCard.swift:480-490 is identical, while the volume bar 25 lines below it at :521-532 writes live (`SystemVolume.set(next)` inside onChanged) — two bars in one card behaving differently is real. value.translation/.velocity/.startLocation are never read; ScrubPreview.swift:10-21 is a bare x/width clamp; Haptics exists at exactly three sites (LyricsStage.swift:369, LockScreenCard.swift:315, :417), none of them a bar. But the Apple half overreaches on three of the four named gaps, and one sub-claim is contradicted by this repo's own code. | corrected: Confirmed and genuinely un-native: the scrubber commits only on mouse-up, where Apple's own macOS players (Music, Podcasts, TV, QuickTime Player) seek live during the drag — and the app contradicts itself, since LockScreenCard.swift:521-532's volume bar does write live inside onChanged. Not supported: (a) 'precision' scrubbing is an iOS UISlider affordance (drag away from the slider to slow the scrub rate); macOS ships no equivalent in AppKit, AVKit or any Apple app, so its absence is not a native-bar miss. (b) Rubber-banding past 0:00/end is an NSScrollView elasticity behavior; Apple does not rubber-band media scrubbers on macOS or iOS — they clamp, exactly as this code does. (c) Haptic detents on a scrubber are not an Apple macOS pattern, and NSHapticFeedbackManager produces nothing at all on a Magic Mouse or any non-Force-Touch pointing device, so a detent would be felt by only some users. (d) 'no way to land on a specific lyric line' is false: LyricsStage.swift:355-375 makes every lyric row a seek target (Haptics.alignment() then media.seek(to: Self.clickTarget(...))), which is the precise affordance the claim says is missing. ;; Mechanics confirmed: MediaPane.swift:259-277 `onChanged` writes only local `scrubbing`, and `media.seek(to:)` is called solely from `onEnded`; LockScreenCard.swift:480-500 is identical; UI/ScrubPreview.swift is a plain x/width clamp; the lock card's volume bar does write live (`SystemVolume.set(next)` in `onChanged`, LockScreenCard.swift:521-532); no Haptics call on either bar. But the user-visible consequence is small and partly wrong. Commit-on-release is the behavior of Apple's own Music player scrubber, so it is not a native-bar failure. The precision argument is self-defeating: the claim's own math gives 0.57 s per pixel on a 4-minute track, which lands well inside any lyric line — 'no way to land on a specific lyric line' does not follow. Rubber-banding, detents and variable-speed scrubbing are refinements no user would name as a defect; the scrub/volume asymmetry is expected (volume is a live system setting, seek is a commit). | corrected: polish — the scrubber is functionally correct and matches Apple's own commit-on-release model; missing precision drag, rubber-band and detent haptics are refinements
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) Whether the island takes a click is decided by a polling timer, so a fast flick-and-click lands on the app underneath
    today: If the pointer has been parked for more than 3 seconds (the sampler is at 8Hz), a user who flicks the pointer to the notch and clicks within the same idle window clicks a window that is still `ignoresMouseEvents = true`. The click is delivered to whatever is beneath — the menu bar, a menu title, a status item, or a document — and the island does not open. `NotchRootView.acceptsFirstMouse` (`:84`) cannot help, because the window never sees the event at all.
    apple: AppKit hit regions are event-synchronous: a window either owns a point or it does not, decided at dispatch time, never on a timer. Apple's own overlay surfaces that must be selectively click-through (menu-bar extras, Spotlight, the Notification Center shade) use window shapes or masks rather than a polled `ignoresMouseEvents` flip. The platform contract is that the first click on a visible control always counts.
    impact: Roughly once a session — more if you are a fast mouse user — you click the island and instead the Window menu drops down, a menu-bar extra pops open, or the click lands in the document behind. Because it is intermittent and depends on how long your hand was still, it reads as "this app is flaky," which is the worst possible impression for a surface whose entire job is one click.
    evidence: The panel's click-through state is written only from the sampler: `Notch/NotchController.swift:759` `self?.panel?.ignoresMouseEvents = !interactive`, fed by `Notch/PointerWatcher.swift:183` `let interactive = isDragging() || interactiveRect.contains(point)` inside `tick()`. `tick()` runs on a `Timer` whose interval is chosen by `PointerWatcher.updateRate` (`:127-144`): full rate only when `near && moving`, where `moving` requires movement within `restThreshold` (`NotchMetrics.swift:34` = 3.0s) and the idle rate is `NotchMetrics.swift:33` `idlePointerInterval = 1.0 / 8.0` — 125ms — with `timer.tolerance = interval / 2` on top (`PointerWatcher.swift:110`), so an idle tick can be up to ~187ms late. `PointerWatcher.swift:9-10` states the design constraint plainly: "Sampling `NSEvent.mouseLocation` is independent of event routing." And `NotchController.swift:757-758` notes there is no fallback: "a `nil` from hitTest only discards the event, it does not forward it."
    fix: Close the timing hole rather than change the mechanism. In `PointerWatcher.updateRate` (`:127-144`), drop the `moving` requirement for a narrow inner band — add a `hotZone` (the collapsed hover rect grown ~60pt downward) that forces `warm = true` on position alone, keeping the `moving` test only for the wide 260pt `warmZone` it was introduced for (a pointer resting in the menu bar). Worst-case latency then falls from ~187ms to one 16ms frame. Belt and braces: in `NotchController` at `:759`, when `interactive` flips true, re-read `NSEvent.mouseLocation` on the next runloop pass and treat a `leftMouseDown` arriving within one frame as landing on the island. `Tests/IslaKitTests/PointerWatcherTests.swift` already covers the rebuild-latency case and is the right home for a hot-zone rate regression test.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) Escape does not close the panel on four of the five tabs — it goes to the app underneath
    today: Open the panel with ⌥⌘I on the Music tab and press Escape: the panel stays open and the Escape is delivered to the frontmost application — cancelling a Finder rename, exiting full screen, dismissing a sheet, or aborting an IME composition in whatever you were doing. Beyond Escape, nothing inside the open panel is reachable from the keyboard: no Tab traversal (`canBecomeKey` is false, so there is no key loop), no `keyboardShortcut` anywhere in `Sources/`, no space-to-play-pause, no arrow-key seek. The only keyboard verbs in the app are the two global hotkeys.
    apple: Escape dismisses every transient surface macOS puts on screen — popovers, Spotlight, Control Center, Notification Center, menus — and those surfaces either take key status while up or install a monitor for the one key they answer. Apple's Full Keyboard Access baseline further requires that anything reachable by pointer be reachable by keyboard; a five-tab panel with a media transport and a clipboard list is squarely in scope.
    impact: The documented way to dismiss a pinned panel silently does something destructive in another app instead. And a user who cannot or does not want to use the trackpad can open the island and then do nothing with it — no tab switch, no play/pause, no copy from the clipboard list.
    evidence: `Notch/NotchPanel.swift:28` `override var canBecomeKey: Bool { acceptsKeyboard }`, and `acceptsKeyboard` is written in exactly one place: `NotchController.setKeyboard` (`:874` `panel?.acceptsKeyboard = wants`), driven by `vm.$wantsKeyboard` (`:827`). `wantsKeyboard` is only ever raised by `NotchViewModel.select` when `tab.needsKeyboard` (`Model/NotchViewModel.swift:379`, and `needsKeyboard` is `self == .translate` at `:31`) or by a click landing in the field (`NotchController.swift:686`). `NotchController.toggle()` (`:544-552`, the ⌥⌘I path) never touches it. Escape is handled in `NotchPanel.sendEvent` (`:82-95`), which only receives key events while the window is key. So on Music, Shelf, Clipboard and Settings the panel is not key and never sees the keystroke. `docs/runbook.md:112` asserts the opposite: "Press ⌥⌘I, move the pointer well away, and confirm the panel stays open until Escape, ⌥⌘I again, or a click in another application closes it."
    fix: Two parts. (1) Escape: install an `NSEvent.addGlobalMonitorForEvents(matching: .keyDown)` alongside the existing `pinnedClickMonitor` (`NotchController.swift:558-575`) — armed only while `viewModel?.holdsOpen == true`, and only for keyCode 53 — routing to the same `setOpen(false)` / `pointer.setInside(false)` pair. A global monitor is passive and needs no permission for a bare keycode check, which preserves the app's permission-free stance. (2) Keyboard operability: let the panel take key status when it was opened by ⌥⌘I (a deliberate keyboard open implies the user is at the keyboard), then add `.keyboardShortcut` to the transport (`space` for `togglePlayPause`, `.leftArrow`/`.rightArrow` for ±5s via `media.seek`) and `⌘1`…`⌘5` to `Rail.icon(for:)`. Update `docs/runbook.md:112` in the same change so the manual pass tests what the code actually does.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) On any display without a physical notch, only the top ~8pt of the ~24pt drawn island is clickable
    today: On an external monitor, a Mac mini/Studio, or any pre-2021 MacBook, the app draws a black synthetic pill 24–38pt tall across the middle of the menu bar, but only its top ~8pt (10 with the `includingTopEdge` +2 at `NotchGeometry.swift:240-243`) answers the pointer. Clicking the lower two thirds — including the artwork thumbnail and the equalizer, which are vertically centred in the drawn pill — passes straight through to the menu bar and does nothing.
    apple: macOS never draws a control larger than its hit region. Menu-bar extras claim their full drawn height; so does the menu bar itself. The HIG's pointer-target guidance is that a target should be at least as large as the visual affordance, never smaller, and macOS's own click-through overlays size their input region to their content.
    impact: Anyone on a desktop Mac or working docked to an external display sees a visible island and clicks it repeatedly with nothing happening, then concludes the app is broken. The dead zone is invisible — nothing distinguishes the 8pt that works from the 16pt that does not — and it directly contradicts the app's own shipped fix `d160909` ("every point of the compact island opens it").
    evidence: `Notch/NotchGeometry.swift:283` `var collapsedDepth: CGFloat { isPhysical ? notchSize.height : 8 }`. The clickable rect is cut from that: `NotchController.swift:1080` `size = CGSize(width: vm.bodySize.width, height: vm.geometry.collapsedDepth)` → `:1103` `rootView.activeRect = rect` and `:1111` `pointer.interactiveRect`. But the island is drawn at the full notch height: `Model/CompactMediaActivity.swift:40-43` returns `height: notchSize.height`, which on a synthetic notch is `NotchGeometry.swift:187` `max(menuBarHeight, NSStatusBar.system.thickness, 24)` — 24 to 38pt in practice. `pointer.hoverRect` uses the same `collapsedDepth` (`NotchGeometry.swift:299-306`), so the hover lift also only lights in the top strip. `Tests/IslaKitTests/CompactHitAreaTests.swift:12-26` asserts the clickable rect matches the drawn *width* at every panel width but never checks the height, so the "every point of the compact island opens it" invariant is only half-tested.
    fix: Make `collapsedDepth` depend on what is actually drawn rather than on whether the notch is physical: keep the 8pt strip only for the bare, contentless synthetic notch (where the status-item concern at `NotchGeometry.swift:273-282` genuinely applies) and claim the full drawn height whenever the compact media pill is on screen and already occluding those pixels. This is consistent with what the horizontal axis already concedes: the pill claims `notchSize.width + NotchMetrics.compactMediaExtension` (104pt, 52 per side) straight across the menu bar, so the status-item argument is only being enforced vertically. Extend `CompactHitAreaTests` with a height assertion mirroring `testTheClickableRectCoversTheWholeDrawnShape`.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) Two hardcoded global hotkeys take system-standard shortcuts away from every app, with no way to rebind
    today: ⌥⌘T is macOS's standard Show/Hide Toolbar equivalent (Finder, Mail, Safari, TextEdit, Preview, and any app using the standard View menu) and ⌥⌘I is Web Inspector in Safari and DevTools in Chrome/Edge/Brave. Once Isla launches, both are silently taken system-wide: ⌥⌘I in Safari opens Isla's panel instead of the inspector, and ⌥⌘T in Finder translates the clipboard instead of toggling the toolbar. The only remedy is to quit the app.
    apple: Apple reserves ⌥⌘T for Show/Hide Toolbar in the standard menu set, and expects third-party global shortcuts to be user-assignable — every utility that registers one (Alfred, Raycast, CleanShot, Bartender, Rectangle) ships a recorder field, and macOS puts its own shortcuts in System Settings → Keyboard → Keyboard Shortcuts, editable. Taking a documented app-level equivalent globally and permanently is what the HIG's shortcut guidance exists to prevent.
    impact: A web developer loses ⌥⌘I in the browser and cannot get it back except by quitting Isla; a Finder user loses toolbar toggling. Because `RegisterEventHotKey` claims the key before the frontmost app sees it, there is no clue what took it — the shortcut just stops working, in a different app, days after install.
    evidence: `App/GlobalHotKey.swift:107-111`: `static let defaultKeyCode = UInt32(kVK_ANSI_I)`, `defaultModifiers = UInt32(optionKey | cmdKey)`, `translateKeyCode = UInt32(kVK_ANSI_T)` — registered at `App/AppDelegate.swift:34-55` via `RegisterEventHotKey`, which reserves the combination with the window server and therefore wins over every application's own menu equivalent. The comment at `:106` asserts "⌥⌘I — free in macOS's own shortcut set," which holds for the Apple menu but not for the apps people run. Nothing in `Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift` exposes a rebinding control; the only input settings there are `opensOnHover` and the hover-delay `Slider` (`SettingsPane.swift:15-17`, `:43-65`).
    fix: Add a shortcut recorder to `SettingsPane` (an `NSViewRepresentable` capturing `keyCode` + `modifierFlags` from a local monitor is ~60 lines, no dependency), persist beside `hoverDelayKey`/`opensOnHoverKey`, and re-register through the existing teardown path — `GlobalHotKey.unregister()` (`:93-98`) exists precisely for this and is currently only exercised at quit (`AppDelegate.swift:91-92`), so the rebinding path is already built and unused. Change the shipped defaults to combinations Apple does not define (⌃⌥⌘I and ⌃⌥⌘T), and surface `RegisterEventHotKey`'s failure return (`GlobalHotKey.swift:82`) as a visible "that shortcut is taken" state in Settings instead of the current silent `nil`.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) Dragging a file only registers when the pointer is already on the island, and the 24pt forgiveness margin is unreachable
    today: With the compact island on a physical notch the effective drop target is roughly `notchWidth + 104` wide by `notchSize.height` (~32-38pt) tall; with nothing playing it shrinks to the bare notch. Nothing on screen changes when a file drag begins — the island gives no sign it can accept anything until the pointer is on top of it, at which point the panel snaps open on the Shelf tab. Aim 20pt low and the drag sails past.
    apple: macOS drop targets announce themselves while the drag is in flight, not on contact: the Dock magnifies and highlights applications that can open the dragged type, Finder sidebar rows light up, Stage Manager's strip expands, and Mission Control's spaces bar drops down when a drag approaches the top of the screen. Apple's drag-and-drop guidance is explicit that a destination should show it will accept the drop before the pointer arrives, and that targets should be forgiving because a dragged object is aimed by hand with an offset grab point.
    impact: The shelf — the feature that most justifies the app against NotchDrop and NotchNook — is hard to hit and gives no invitation. Users try once, miss, and stop using it. Every competitor expands the notch region the moment a file drag starts anywhere on screen, which is what makes the gesture teachable in the first place.
    evidence: `NotchController.swift:1108` grows the drop target: `rootView.dropRect = rect.insetBy(dx: -24, dy: -24)`. But whether the window receives any mouse or drag input is decided by `pointer.interactiveRect` (`:1111`), which is the ungrown rect (`contentScreenRect(for: size).insetBy(dx: -slack, dy: 0)`, slack = `Theme.collapsedTopRadius` = 6), fed to `panel.ignoresMouseEvents` at `:759`. A window that ignores mouse events is not considered as a dragging destination, so the 24pt band `dropRect` describes is never reached — `NotchRootView.draggingEntered` (`:148`) is not called there. The only drag affordance is `NotchController.swift:631-636` `root.onDragEntered` → `vm.tab = .shelf; vm.isDropTargeted = true; setOpen(true)`, which fires only once the pointer is inside the island itself. Nothing in `Sources/` observes a drag session that started elsewhere on screen.
    fix: (1) Make the grown `dropRect` actually reachable: add a `dropInteractiveRect` to `PointerWatcher` (the grown rect) and OR it into the `interactive` test at `PointerWatcher.swift:183` while a drag is detected, so `ignoresMouseEvents` is false across the whole forgiving band. (2) Detect drags in flight without new permissions: `NSEvent.addGlobalMonitorForEvents(matching: [.leftMouseDragged])` armed while the pointer is inside `geometry.warmZone`, disarmed on `leftMouseUp`; on arm, run the existing peek animation (`NotchController.sneakPeek`, `:1013`) with a tray glyph so the island visibly grows and says "drop here." The `isDropTargeted` state and its `ShelfPane.dropHint` treatment (`ShelfPane.swift:73-90`) already exist — this only needs an earlier trigger.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) Right-clicking the island is swallowed and does nothing; Control-click on a shelf card selects instead of opening its menu
    today: Right-clicking the compact island or the open panel produces nothing at all — not a menu, not a pass-through to whatever is beneath. Control-clicking a shelf card selects or deselects it rather than showing the Copy / Open / Show in Finder / Remove menu that a two-button right-click shows.
    apple: macOS defines Control-click as the secondary click and requires it to be indistinguishable from a right-click; trackpad users with secondary click disabled rely on it. And an on-screen element with no Dock icon, no menu-bar item, and no window is exactly where Mac users expect a right-click to reveal Settings / About / Quit — every menu-bar-class utility offers one.
    impact: Shelf users who Control-click get a selection toggle instead of the menu, with no route to Copy or Show in Finder short of discovering that a real right-click is required. And a first-run user who right-clicks the unfamiliar black pill to find out what it is gets nothing — the discovery gesture every Mac user tries first is a dead end.
    evidence: There is exactly one `contextMenu` in the app — `UI/ShelfPane.swift:191-197` — and none on the island itself. `NotchRootView` overrides `hitTest`, `mouseEntered`, `mouseExited`, and `cursorUpdate` but never `rightMouseDown`, so a right-click inside `activeRect` is claimed by the panel (the window is not ignoring events there) and dies in the responder chain without falling through to the menu bar. On the shelf, `UI/ShelfDragSource.swift:42-86` installs a `DragView` overlay above the card that overrides `mouseDown`/`mouseDragged`/`mouseUp` but not `rightMouseDown`, and its `mouseUp` routes Control-click straight into selection: `:84` `onClick(event.modifierFlags.intersection(.deviceIndependentFlagsMask))` → `ShelfPane.swift:166` `shelf.select(item, modifiers:)` → `Services/ShelfStore.swift:192-205`, which tests only `.shift` and `.command` and treats `.control` as a plain click.
    fix: In `ShelfDragSource.DragView`, override `rightMouseDown(with:)` to call `super` so the SwiftUI `.contextMenu` is found, and in `mouseUp` (`:80-85`) branch on `event.modifierFlags.contains(.control)` to present that same menu instead of calling `onClick`. On the island, attach a `.contextMenu` to the shell at `NotchContentView.swift:109` offering Open Panel / Open on Hover / Settings / About / Quit, reusing the actions `SettingsPane` already calls (`orderFrontStandardAboutPanel`, `terminate`) so the panel is not the only route to them.

## onboarding
verdict: The craft *inside* the panel is genuinely Apple-grade — `ConfirmRow`'s two-press arming instead of an impossible sheet (Confirmation.swift:43-83), the Music pane's empty state that names the condition and then offers "Open Apple Music" (MediaPane.swift:447-477), the Translator's four named failure causes with a deep link into System Settings (Translator.swift:36-57, 233-237), the artwork skeleton that gives up after 2.5 s instead of shimmering forever (MediaPane.swift:186-191). That is a designer's work. The problem is everything *before* the panel. This is a `.accessory` binary with no Dock icon, no menu-bar item, no window, no notification — and a collapsed island that, when nothing is playing, is drawn at exactly `notchSize` in pure black with zero shadow. On the MacBook this ships for, double-clicking Isla.app produces literally zero visible change on screen. There is no first-run branch anywhere in the codebase (`rg firstLaunch|hasSeen|didLaunch` returns nothing), the two hotkeys appear in no user-facing string, and Quit is the last row of the sixth section of an unindicated ~4-screen scroll view inside the panel the user cannot find. The team knows: docs/runbook.md:82-84 says "This is the only quit route; if it fails, the app can only be stopped from Activity Monitor." Against NotchNook / Alcove / Boring Notch — all of which ship a first-run window and a permissions walkthrough — this is the single thing that loses the sale in the first ten seconds, before any of the good work is ever seen. Secondary but real: every privacy-consequential switch (lyrics → three third-party network services; clipboard screenshots → disk) ships with no explanatory caption, even though the `noteRow` helper to write one exists and is used exactly once.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/M) First launch produces zero visible change on a notched Mac — and no way to learn the app exists or to quit it
    today: User drags Isla to /Applications, clears quarantine, double-clicks. Nothing appears: no Dock icon, no menu-bar item, no window, no notification, no panel, no pill. A black shape the exact size and colour of the notch is drawn over the notch. If no music is playing there is no signal whatsoever that the process is alive. The gestures that would reveal it — click the notch, or ⌥⌘I — appear in no UI, no About panel, no bundled resource; only in README.md on GitHub and in source comments. LSUIElement apps are also absent from ⌥⌘Esc Force Quit, so a user who cannot open the panel cannot quit it either.
    apple: Apple never ships a launch that produces no observable change. Menu-bar-only apps get a menu-bar item on first launch (Time Machine, Bluetooth, Now Playing). Apps with no UI announce themselves — registering a login item posts a system notification; Shortcuts, Sidecar and Continuity Camera all leave a persistent affordance. The HIG's Onboarding guidance is explicit that first run should teach the primary gesture in context and that people must never have to hunt for the app's entry point. Every paid competitor here (NotchNook, Alcove, Boring Notch, DynamicLake Pro) opens a first-run window.
    impact: A first-time user cannot tell whether the app launched, crashed, or was blocked by Gatekeeper. They cannot discover the click-the-notch gesture, cannot learn ⌥⌘I, and cannot quit. This is the highest-probability reason a downloaded copy gets deleted in under a minute — before any of the app's real quality is ever seen.
    evidence: `IslaApplication.swift:12` `static let activationPolicy: … = .accessory`; `Scripts/bundle.sh` Info.plist `<key>LSUIElement</key><true/>`. `AppDelegate.applicationDidFinishLaunching` (AppDelegate.swift:17-70) installs the controller and two hotkeys and returns — no first-run branch. `rg -ni "onboard|welcome|firstLaunch|firstRun|hasLaunched|hasSeen|didLaunch" Sources/` returns nothing. With no track: `CompactMediaActivity.swift:36` `guard isVisible else { return notchSize }`, so `NotchViewModel.bodySize` == the notch's exact size; `NotchContentView.swift:78` sets shadow opacity to `0` in that state; `NotchContentView.swift:153-155` renders the header as `Color.clear`. Hover lights only two `Theme.collapsedTopRadius` = 6 pt slivers, because `hoverLift` skips the cutout on physical notches (`NotchContentView.swift:264-266`, `Theme.swift:76`). `docs/runbook.md:82-84`: "This is the only quit route; if it fails, the app can only be stopped from Activity Monitor."
    fix: Add `NotchViewModel.hasCompletedFirstRunKey = "hasCompletedFirstRun"` alongside the other defaults (NotchViewModel.swift:231-360). In `AppDelegate.applicationDidFinishLaunching`, when the key is unset, call `controller?.toggle()` after a ~0.8 s settle and set the key. Route `NotchContentView.pane` (NotchContentView.swift:459-478) to a new `WelcomePane` for that one open: app name, one line per tab, the two shortcuts rendered as ⌥⌘I / ⌥⌘T, "Click the island to open it", and a single "Get Started" button that dismisses to the Music tab. Keep it inside the existing 560×208 body — no new window, no policy change, so the design doc's "no window" amendment holds. Localize both keys in `Resources/{en,ru}.lproj`. Add a `WelcomeContractTests` case asserting the default is unset on a fresh domain and set after one run, so the flow cannot silently regress.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) Privacy-consequential switches carry no explanatory caption, though the helper to write one already exists and is used once
    today: A user flips "Show Lyrics" and, with no statement anywhere in the app, begins sending their listening history to lrclib.net, raw.githubusercontent.com and lyrics.kugou.com. A user flips "Save Clipboard Screenshots" and begins writing every pasteboard image to ~/Pictures/Isla. Both consequences are documented richly — in source comments and in README.md — and nowhere the user will read them.
    apple: macOS System Settings puts a caption under any switch whose consequence is not self-evident: Privacy & Security → Analytics has a paragraph plus "About Analytics & Privacy"; Siri, Location Services and Apple Intelligence all state what leaves the device before the switch. The HIG's Privacy guidance requires explaining what data is used and why *at the point of the choice*, not in documentation.
    impact: Someone who cares about their listening history leaving the machine cannot learn from the app that it does. The project's own hard constraint ("anything new that leaves the machine… must default to off") is honoured mechanically but not honestly: off-by-default protects the user who never touches it, and tells the user who does nothing at all.
    evidence: `SettingsPane.swift:489-497` defines `noteRow(_:)` — "A line of explanation under a section's controls… it states something the user would otherwise have to guess." It is called exactly once, at `SettingsPane.swift:215`, for Spotify token storage. Meanwhile `SettingsPane.swift:133-143` renders "Show Lyrics" as a bare toggle, while `NotchViewModel.swift:243-252` documents what it does: "This is the app's only network use: turning it on sends what is currently playing — title, artist, album, and for Spotify the track id — to three third-party services. That is listening history leaving the machine." Same for `SettingsPane.swift:108-112` "Save Clipboard Screenshots" against `NotchViewModel.swift:231-241` ("writes a copy of every image that touches the pasteboard to ~/Pictures/Isla… a screenshot of a recovery-code sheet"), and `SettingsPane.swift:227-240` "Hide From Screen Recording" (defaults on, per NotchViewModel.swift:360-370).
    fix: Call the existing `noteRow` under each: after the Show Lyrics toggle — "Sends the current title, artist and album to lrclib.net, GitHub and lyrics.kugou.com to look words up."; after Save Clipboard Screenshots — "Writes every image that reaches the clipboard to ~/Pictures/Isla. Keeps the most recent 200."; after Hide From Screen Recording — "The panel is omitted from screenshots and screen sharing."; after Show on Lock Screen — "Shows what is playing over the lock screen. The card answers clicks; the island does not." Six new keys in `Resources/{en,ru}.lproj`; `test-localizations.sh` already gates parity.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) Apple Events consent fires unannounced on first panel open, and refusing it degrades the app permanently with no path back
    today: A user clicks the notch for the first time with Music open. The panel unfolds and macOS immediately throws a security dialog: "Isla wants access to control Music.app." Nothing in the app led up to it. If they press Don't Allow (the safe-looking answer to an unexplained prompt), shuffle and repeat silently never appear again (`refreshPlaybackModes` sets both to nil), Spotify precision sync silently degrades to ~1 s stale positions, the Liked-Songs heart never resolves, and the scripting fallback for Music/Spotify is dead. Nothing in Settings mentions Automation; nothing offers System Settings → Privacy & Security → Automation.
    apple: Apple's rule is explain-then-ask: put your own explanation on screen, then trigger the system prompt from a deliberate user action. And every Apple app that depends on a revocable permission surfaces the revoked state with a route back — Photos, Screen Recording in QuickTime, Contacts in Mail all say "…doesn't have permission" and offer Open System Settings.
    impact: An unexplained security dialog at the exact moment of first delight is the classic trust-destroying moment, and it is the one prompt this app raises. A user who declines gets a permanently half-working Music tab and no way to discover why or how to fix it — a silent, undiagnosable degradation.
    evidence: `MediaPane.swift:67-68` calls `media.refreshPlaybackModes()` in `onAppear`; `MediaController.swift:293-305` routes it to `PlayerBridge.playbackModes`, which runs `NSAppleScript` against Music or Spotify. `MediaController.swift:600` re-fires it on every track change while the panel is open. `PlayerBridge.swift:388-392` is the entire failure handling: `if let error, let code = … { NSLog("Isla: AppleScript error \(code): …") }` — `errAEEventNotPermitted` (-1743) is never distinguished, never published, never surfaced. `rg "errAEEventNotPermitted|-1743" Sources/` returns nothing. `Scripts/bundle.sh` does set `NSAppleEventsUsageDescription`, so the system dialog has a string; nothing in the app precedes it. The recovery pattern exists and is used elsewhere: `Translator.swift:233-237` `openLanguageSettings()` opens `x-apple.systempreferences:…`, surfaced by `TranslatePane.swift:138-140`.
    fix: Two parts. (1) Gate the first script: on the Music tab, when `displayedPlayerApp != nil` and `AEDeterminePermissionToAutomateTarget(…, askUserIfNeeded: false)` returns `errAEEventWouldRequireUserConsent`, render an inline row in `MediaPane` — "Let Isla read shuffle and repeat from Music" with an Allow button — and only call `refreshPlaybackModes()` from that button. (2) Publish the refusal: add `@Published private(set) var automationDenied = false` on `MediaController`, set from a new `PlayerBridge` completion that passes the error code through and matches `-1743`; add a `noteRow` + `actionRow` pair to the Music section of `SettingsPane` — "Isla cannot control Music or Spotify" / "Open Automation Settings…" opening `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.security?Privacy_Automation`, mirroring `Translator.openLanguageSettings`.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The app's only Quit is the last row of the sixth section of a ~4-screen scroll view with no scroll indicator
    today: A user who wants to quit must: find the island, click it, hover the settings icon at the foot of the rail, then scroll four screens through a viewport that gives no sign there is anything below the fold, then press Quit twice. Someone who reaches Settings and sees General/Screenshots has no visual reason to believe Privacy, Spotify or Quit exist at all.
    apple: System Settings has a sidebar, a search field and visible scroll position. macOS scroll views show an overlay indicator on any scroll gesture by default; suppressing it is reserved for content whose extent is obvious. Apple's own accessory apps always keep Quit one level from the surface (menu-bar item → Quit).
    impact: Two failures compound: settings the user came for are undiscoverable below the fold, and the single most important escape hatch in an app with no Dock icon and no Force Quit entry is four hidden screens deep.
    evidence: `SettingsPane.swift:31` `ScrollView(showsIndicators: false)`. Six sections in order: General, Screenshots, Music, Spotify, Privacy, Application (`SettingsPane.swift:33, 107, 132, 195, 219, 243`); Quit is the last row of the last one (`SettingsPane.swift:247-253`). The viewport is `NotchGeometry.standardContentHeight` = `expandedSize.height − notchSize.height − bodyBottomPadding` (NotchGeometry.swift:56-58) = 208 (`NotchMetrics.swift:13`) − ~37 − 14 ≈ **157 pt**. The content is ~17 rows at 26 pt (`toggleRow`/`actionRow`/`confirmRow` all `.frame(height: 26)`) plus six section headers and padding — roughly 640 pt, about four screens. No search field, no section index, no edge fade, no indicator. `docs/runbook.md:82-84` confirms this is the only quit route.
    fix: Three changes in `SettingsPane`. (1) Drop `showsIndicators: false` on line 31 — the panel is 157 pt tall and the indicator is the only signal the content continues. (2) Pin the Application section outside the `ScrollView`: wrap the body in a `VStack` with the scroll view on top and About/Quit as a fixed footer row, so Quit is always one click from opening Settings. (3) Add a `.mask(LinearGradient(…))` bottom fade over the scroll view so cut-off content reads as cut off. All three are contained to SettingsPane.swift:30-33 and 243-258.
    challenges: Every mechanical detail checks out: SettingsPane.swift:31 is ScrollView(showsIndicators: false); the six sections are at SettingsPane.swift:33, 107, 132, 195, 219, 243 with Quit as the final confirmRow at 247-253; NotchGeometry.swift:56-58 gives standardContentHeight = 208 (NotchMetrics.swift:13) − notch − 14 (NotchGeometry.swift:18) ≈ 157 pt, against ~17 rows at 26 pt (SettingsPane.swift:419, 466, 517) plus six headers ≈ 610 pt. But there is no fade/edge mask anywhere in the scroll body (grep for mask/LinearGradient in NotchContentView finds only the hover-wing falloff at 263/271), so the Screenshots section box is visibly sliced at the fold — that is the standard "there is more below" signal. Users do not name suppressed overlay scrollers; the real cost is discoverability of one rarely-used action. | corrected: minor — Quit is genuinely 4 screens down with scroll indicators suppressed, but the pane hard-clips mid-section at the fold, so "no visual reason to believe more exists" is wrong and scrolling is the ordinary macOS affordance
- [DISPUTED] (major/S) Connecting Spotify has no in-flight state and no failure state — a refused or failed authorization is completely silent
    today: User presses Connect. Safari opens Spotify's consent page. They press Cancel there, or their network drops, or the exchange 400s. They come back to the panel and the row still reads "Connect Spotify Account…" — identical to before. Nothing says the attempt happened, failed, or why. The only feedback loop available is pressing it again and hoping.
    apple: Every Apple browser-round-trip sign-in (Internet Accounts, App Store, iCloud) shows an in-progress state while the browser is out and an explicit failure with Try Again on return. The HIG's feedback principle: a user action must always produce an observable result.
    impact: The one account-linking flow in the app is a black box. A user whose exchange failed will conclude the feature is broken, which is indistinguishable from it actually being broken — and the app's only account-backed feature (Liked Songs) never appears.
    evidence: `SpotifyAccount.swift:111-131` `beginAuthorization()` opens the browser and returns, publishing nothing; its first line is `guard let clientID else { return }` — a silent no-op if a user has overridden the id with an empty string. `SpotifyAccount.swift:134-151` `handleCallback` has four `guard … else { return }` clauses (wrong host, no code, no verifier, state mismatch) — all silent. `SpotifyAccount.swift:162-176` `exchange` ends `guard let tokens = await tokenRequest(body: body) else { return }`; `SpotifyAccount.swift:191-199` `tokenRequest` returns nil on any non-200 or transport error, with no logging at all. The pane (`SettingsPane.swift:195-217`) renders "Connect Spotify Account…" purely from `spotify.isConnected`; there is no `isAuthorizing` and no error published.
    fix: Add `@Published private(set) var authorizationState: AuthState` to `SpotifyAccount` with `.idle | .waitingForBrowser | .exchanging | .failed(String)`. Set `.waitingForBrowser` in `beginAuthorization` before `NSWorkspace.shared.open`, with a 120 s timeout back to `.failed`; set `.exchanging` in `handleCallback`; have `tokenRequest` return a `Result` so `exchange` can set `.failed`. In `SettingsPane` (195-217), swap the action row for a `ProgressView` + "Waiting for Spotify…" while non-idle, and add a `noteRow` carrying the failure text plus a Try Again `actionRow`. Also replace the silent `guard let clientID else { return }` with a `.failed` state.
    challenges: The headline is fully confirmed: SpotifyAccount.swift:111-131 `beginAuthorization()` opens the browser via `NSWorkspace.shared.open` and publishes nothing; `handleCallback` (134-151) returns silently on a wrong host/path, a missing `code` (which is exactly what Spotify sends back on Cancel — `error=access_denied&state=…`), a missing verifier, or a state mismatch; `exchange` (162-176) ends `guard let tokens = await tokenRequest(body: body) else { return }`; `tokenRequest` (191-199) returns nil on any transport error or non-200 with no logging at all; and SettingsPane.swift:195-217 renders the row purely from `spotify.isConnected`. The class publishes `isConnected`, `apiBlocked`, `saved`, `tokenUnavailable`, `canImportLegacyAccount`, `storage` (SpotifyAccount.swift:25-67) — no `isAuthorizing`, no `lastError`. But one cited mechanism is wrong. | corrected: Everything in the claim holds except the empty-client-id case. `clientID` (SpotifyAccount.swift:103-107) trims the `spotifyClientID` default and returns `(value?.isEmpty ?? true) ? Self.builtInClientID : value`, and `builtInClientID` (SpotifyAccount.swift:20) is a non-optional `String` literal — so an empty override falls back to the built-in id and `guard let clientID else { return }` in `beginAuthorization()` can never fail. The silent no-op the claim describes is unreachable; the real silence is the callback and token-exchange guards. ;; Confirmed: SpotifyAccount.swift:111-131 beginAuthorization publishes nothing; handleCallback (134-151) has four silent guards; exchange (162-176) ends in guard let tokens ... else { return }; tokenRequest (191-199) returns nil on any non-200 or transport error with zero logging; the @Published set (SpotifyAccount.swift:25-68) has no isAuthorizing and no error, and SettingsPane.swift:195-217 renders the row purely from spotify.isConnected. But the claimed silent no-op is wrong: clientID (SpotifyAccount.swift:103-107) falls back to the non-optional builtInClientID (line 21) when the override is empty, so guard let clientID never fails. Success does change the row, so only cancel/network/400 lands in the silent state, on a feature that exists to resolve the Liked-Songs heart. | corrected: minor — the silence is real and confirmed, but it affects only the failure path of one optional secondary feature, and the sub-claim about an empty client-id override is false
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) Shelf, Clipboard and Translate empty states are a bare glyph or literally blank, while the Music pane shows how it should be done
    today: A new user opens the Shelf and sees a dashed rectangle with a tray icon — no statement that files can be dragged onto the notch. Opens Clipboard and sees one grey glyph — no statement that history will accumulate as they copy. Opens Translate and sees two empty rectangles with 9 pt "ENGLISH" / "RUSSIAN" headers and no placeholder text or caret hint.
    apple: macOS empty states are always worded: Finder "This folder is empty", Notes "No Notes", Mail "No Messages", Photos "No Photos or Videos". Apple's own Translate app shows an "Enter text" placeholder. A glyph alone names the feature; it does not tell the user what to do or that nothing is wrong.
    impact: Three of five tabs read as broken or unfinished on the first visit. The Shelf's core gesture — drag a file to the notch — is never stated anywhere in the app, so the feature most likely to sell the product against NotchDrop goes undiscovered.
    evidence: `ShelfPane.swift:73-90` `dropHint` is a dashed `RoundedRectangle` with a `tray.and.arrow.down.fill` glyph — no `Text` at all. `ClipboardPane.swift:9-13` renders a lone `Image(systemName: "list.clipboard")` — no `Text`. `TranslatePane.swift:171-173` the result column's empty branch is `Color.clear`; `TranslatePane.swift:77` is a bare `TextEditor` with no placeholder, relying on the comment at TranslatePane.swift:4-6 ("The left one sits on a surface — that is the whole signal that it can be typed into"). Contrast `MediaPane.swift:447-477`, which shows a glyph, the localized line "Nothing is playing", *and* one "Open Apple Music"/"Open Spotify" button per installed player — with the comment "a dead end teaches people not to open the tab."
    fix: Add a `Text` under each glyph, matching the Music pane's 12 pt `.medium` / `Theme.secondary` treatment: Shelf — "Drag files here to hold them" (and swap to "Drop to add" while `isTargeted`, which the view already tracks); Clipboard — "Nothing copied yet". For Translate, add an `.overlay(alignment: .topLeading)` placeholder "Type or paste to translate" on the `TextEditor` when `translator.input.isEmpty`, gated off while `covered`. Four keys in `Resources/{en,ru}.lproj`.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) The two global hotkeys appear nowhere in the app, and About is the bare system panel with nothing in it
    today: About shows "Isla / Version 0.1.0 / MIT License" — no website, no shortcut list, no support route. The shortcuts are documented only in README.md on GitHub. The hotkeys are also fixed constants with no rebinding UI, so a user whose ⌥⌘I is taken by another app has no shortcut and no way to know it collided.
    apple: Apple ships a Help menu in every app and a Credits panel with a link. Menu-bar utilities put their shortcut next to the command that uses it. macOS itself lists every shortcut in System Settings → Keyboard → Keyboard Shortcuts, and every serious utility (Alfred, Raycast, and all the paid notch apps) exposes an editable hotkey field.
    impact: The two keyboard routes into the app — including the *only* route that works when the panel cannot be found — are invisible to anyone who did not read GitHub. A collision with an existing shortcut is undiagnosable.
    evidence: `GlobalHotKey.swift:107-111` defines ⌥⌘I and ⌥⌘T; `rg "⌥⌘" Sources/` finds them only in comments (`AppDelegate.swift:109`, `NotchViewModel.swift:67,72`, `NotchMetrics.swift:47`). `Resources/en.lproj/Localizable.strings` (147 lines, 109 keys) contains no shortcut text and no help string. `SettingsPane.swift:244-246` calls `NSApp.orderFrontStandardAboutPanel(nil)` with no options dictionary, and `Scripts/bundle.sh` copies no `Credits.rtf` — only `AppIcon.icns`, the two `.lproj` folders and `Licenses/`. `NSHumanReadableCopyright` is the string `MIT License`. There is no `NSApp.mainMenu`, so ⌘? Help does not exist.
    fix: Add a `section(localized("Shortcuts"))` to `SettingsPane` above Application, with two rows showing "Open Isla — ⌥⌘I" and "Translate Clipboard — ⌥⌘T" (a `KeyboardShortcutRow` mirroring `toggleRow`'s layout with a monospaced glyph capsule on the trailing edge). Pass an options dictionary to `orderFrontStandardAboutPanel` with `.credits: NSAttributedString` naming islamac.app and the GitHub repo. Rebindable hotkeys are a larger follow-up — `GlobalHotKey.unregister()` (GlobalHotKey.swift:86) already exists for it.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) An offline or refused lyric lookup reports "No lyrics for this track", and the retry button is unreachable from that state
    today: On a plane or a captive-portal Wi-Fi, every track reports "No lyrics for this track" — factually wrong, since the words exist and only the lookup failed — and the one control that would retry is not on screen. The user must change tracks and back to force another attempt.
    apple: Apple separates "not found" from "couldn't reach the service": App Store shows "Cannot connect to App Store" with Retry; Music shows "Not Available Offline"; Weather shows "Weather Unavailable — check your connection". Never a content-absent message for a transport failure, and a recoverable failure always carries its recovery.
    impact: The flagship differentiating feature (lyrics with word-level sweep) looks broken and empty rather than temporarily unreachable, and offers no way to try again.
    evidence: `LyricsStore.swift:485-492`: `} catch { … settle([]) ; return }` — a network failure produces the same `.none` state as a genuine catalogue miss (`LyricsStore.swift:47-53`). `LyricsStage.swift:76-79` branches on `.synced(let lines), !lines.isEmpty` else `unavailable`; `LyricsStage.swift:446-472` `unavailable` renders the spinner or "No lyrics for this track" plus only a back chevron. The "Search Lyrics Again" button (`LyricsStage.swift:424-437`) lives in `controls`, which is only composed inside `stage(lines:)` — so it does not exist in the failure state. Notably the lock card *does* distinguish the causes: `LockScreenCard.swift:292-295` returns "Lyrics are switched off in Settings." / "Looking for the words…" / "No words for this track."
    fix: Add `case unreachable` to `LyricsStore.State` (LyricsStore.swift:47-53) and set it in the `catch` at :485 when the error is a `URLError` with a transport code, keeping `.none` for a service that answered with nothing. In `LyricsStage.unavailable` (446-472), branch on it for "Lyrics could not be looked up" and, in both `.none` and `.unreachable`, hoist the existing `arrow.clockwise` research button (424-437) into the view so retry is always available. Mirror the copy in `LockScreenCard.swift:292-295`, which already has the right shape.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/M) There is no single place to clear everything Isla has stored, and dragging the app to the Trash leaves data behind
    today: A user who tries Isla and removes it leaves behind `~/Library/Application Support/Isla` (lyrics cache up to 500 tracks, dropped-file copies, `spotify-credentials.json` on unsigned builds), `~/Pictures/Isla` if screenshots were ever on, a keychain item on signed builds, and the whole `com.ctimothe.isla` defaults domain. There is no in-app statement of what is stored or where, and no way to clear it all.
    apple: Apple apps expose their storage and let you reclaim it: Settings → General → Storage itemizes per-app data with a Delete; Safari has "Remove All Website Data"; Music has "Delete All Downloads". The HIG expects an app that keeps user data to make removing it as easy as creating it.
    impact: An evaluator cannot cleanly undo a trial, and a user who has second thoughts about the clipboard-screenshot or lyrics features cannot verify the data is gone. For a direct-download, unnotarized app asking for trust, leaving residue is exactly the wrong signal.
    evidence: Clearing is scattered and partial: screenshots (`SettingsPane.swift:116-129`), lyrics cache (`SettingsPane.swift:185-192`), Spotify disconnect (`SettingsPane.swift:197-199`), shelf and clipboard `Clear` buttons inside their own panes (`ShelfPane.swift:106-109`, `ClipboardPane.swift:36-39`). Nothing clears: the ten `UserDefaults` keys (`NotchViewModel.swift:231-360`), or `~/Library/Application Support/Isla/Dropped`, which only self-prunes after seven days (`AppPaths.swift:60-73`) and is untouched by `ShelfStore.clear()` (ShelfStore.swift:182-186, which only empties the item list and re-persists). `rg "removePersistentDomain|removeObject\(forKey" Sources/` returns nothing.
    fix: Add a final `confirmRow` to the Application section of `SettingsPane` (243-254): "Reset Isla and Quit" / armed "Delete Everything and Quit?". Have it call a new `AppPaths.removeAllStoredData()` that trashes `supportDirectory` and, if the toggle was ever on, `screenshotDirectory`; then `SpotifyAccount.shared.disconnect()`; then `UserDefaults.standard.removePersistentDomain(forName: ProductIdentity.bundleIdentifier)`; then `NSApp.terminate(nil)`. Add a `noteRow` above it listing exactly the two folders and the keychain item, so what will be deleted is stated before the second press. Cover `removeAllStoredData` in `Tests/IslaKitTests` against a temp `AppPaths`, the way `ScreenshotVaultTests` already does.
- [CONFIRMED] (polish/M) A dead media path is indistinguishable from nothing playing — the pane says "Nothing is playing" while audio is audibly playing
    today: macOS closes the MediaRemote route (as 15.4 already did once), or perl fails to load the helper, while a YouTube tab is playing in Safari. The failure policy backs off to `.fallback`; `PlayerBridge` only knows Music and Spotify, so it reports nothing. The island collapses and the Music tab says "Nothing is playing" over audible audio, offering "Open Apple Music" / "Open Spotify" as the fix.
    apple: Apple distinguishes "empty" from "unavailable" everywhere — Now Playing in Control Centre simply omits itself rather than claiming silence; Photos says "Unable to Load", not "No Photos". Stating a false fact about the system is worse than stating nothing.
    impact: The user is told something they can hear is false, and is offered an action that cannot help. On the release where Apple closes the private route again — the failure the whole helper architecture exists to survive — every user sees a confidently wrong message instead of an honest one.
    evidence: `MediaController.swift:17-45` publishes `track`, `artwork`, `isPlaying`, `duration`, `position`, `sourceName`, `canSkip`, `shuffleEnabled`, `repeatMode` — and no flag for "the helper is gone" or "running on the AppleScript fallback". `NowPlayingFeed.swift:267` consumes `NowPlayingFailurePolicy.Action.fallback` (NowPlayingFailurePolicy.swift:44-46) without publishing it. `MediaPane.swift:96-103` therefore renders `emptyState` for both cases, and its own comment at `MediaPane.swift:452-453` claims otherwise: "Status, not instruction: an empty pane on its own would not say whether nothing is playing or nothing could be read." The copy it chose — "Nothing is playing" (MediaPane.swift:454) — asserts one of the two.
    fix: Publish the state that already exists internally: add `@Published private(set) var sourceAvailability: Availability` (`.live | .scriptingFallback | .unavailable`) to `MediaController`, driven from `NowPlayingFeed`'s `.fallback` branch (NowPlayingFeed.swift:267) and from `PlayerBridge.currentState` returning nil with no player running. In `MediaPane.emptyState`, when it is `.unavailable`, swap the line to "Isla cannot read what is playing" and drop the Open-player buttons (they do not address it); keep today's copy and buttons for `.live`. Two keys, and a unit test on the policy→availability mapping beside the existing `NowPlayingFailurePolicy` tests.

## distribution
verdict: The engineering inside the release machinery is unusually good — `Scripts/release.sh` already does the right things in the right order (notarize the app, staple it, *then* build the DMG around the stapled app, tag only after notarization succeeds, roll the tag back on failure, publish a SHA-256), and `bundle.sh` signs inside-out with the hardened runtime and no `--deep`. But none of it has ever run: there is no local tag, no `docs/releases/` directory (so `release.sh:19` fails on its second check), and the live v0.1.0 release was published by hand with an ad-hoc-signed, `spctl`-rejected, arm64-only app inside a completely unsigned DMG. That gap is not cosmetic. I verified empirically that a quarantined, non-notarized dylib is refused by system policy inside `/usr/bin/perl`, and that the helper dylib copied out of the shipped DMG carries `com.apple.quarantine` with flags `0281` — no user-approved bit — and is BLOCKED. So on the Apple-sanctioned install path the app's single most important feature silently never works, and nothing in the app notices or says so. On top of that there is no updater of any kind (no Sparkle, no appcast, no version check), no crash reporting for an app that has no Dock icon or menu-bar item to reveal that it died, and no uninstall path, so trashing the app leaves a live Spotify refresh token in plaintext JSON plus a dangling login-item registration. Against NotchNook/Alcove/DynamicLake — all of which are Developer ID-signed, notarized, and Sparkle-updating — this is not yet a product a stranger can install, trust, or keep current. The distance is real but bounded: it is one Apple Developer account, one Sparkle integration, one `--arch` flag, and one uninstall sheet.
- [DISPUTED] (blocker/M) On a downloaded install the Now Playing helper is blocked by Gatekeeper, permanently and silently
    today: A user downloads the DMG, drags Isla to Applications, and approves it through System Settings → Privacy & Security. The app launches and looks fine. But the nested helper dylib still carries an unapproved quarantine flag, so `/usr/bin/perl` refuses to load it, `NowPlayingFailurePolicy` burns its restart budget, and Isla drops to the AppleScript fallback — which only drives Music and Spotify, cannot read any other player, has no artwork or supported-command data, and additionally raises an Apple Events consent prompt the user never expected. Nothing in the UI says the primary media route is dead; the only trace is an NSLog nobody will read. The README's `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` (README:26) is the *only* instruction that actually fixes it, which is precisely why it is there.
    apple: Apple's contract is that a notarized, stapled app just runs: the ticket is checked offline, quarantine is satisfied without user action, and nested code is covered by the same submission. Where a first-party feature genuinely cannot start, Apple surfaces it in place — Screen Time, Focus, and Continuity Camera all state what is blocked and offer the one control that unblocks it, rather than degrading to a lesser mode in silence.
    impact: The feature the whole app exists for — reading Now Playing from any app — never works for anyone who installs it the documented, Apple-sanctioned way. They see a music panel that only responds to Music and Spotify, blame the app for being broken, and uninstall. This alone makes it not worth using against a $25 competitor whose media panel works on first launch.
    evidence: Verified against the live artifact. Downloaded https://github.com/ctimothe/isla-mac/releases/download/v0.1.0/Isla-0.1.0.dmg, applied a Safari-shaped quarantine xattr to the image, mounted it, copied Isla.app out: `xattr -l Isla.app/Contents/Resources/libislamedia.dylib` → `com.apple.quarantine: 0281;...` (bit 0x40, QTN_FLAG_USER_APPROVED, is NOT set). Loading it the way the app does — `Sources/IslaKit/Services/NowPlayingFeed.swift:191-196`, `task.executableURL = URL(fileURLWithPath: "/usr/bin/perl")` with `DynaLoader::dl_load_file($ARGV[0], 0x01)` — fails: `dlopen(...): code signature ... not valid for use in process: library load disallowed by system policy`. Controlled test on five freshly-built, ad-hoc + hardened-runtime dylibs with distinct cdhashes isolates the rule exactly: flags `0081` → BLOCKED, `0283` → BLOCKED, `00c1`/`0043`/`00e3` (user-approved bit set) → LOADED. The app's only reaction is `NowPlayingFeed.swift:185-188` `onUnavailable?()` → `MediaController.swift:177` → `switchToScriptingFallback()` at `MediaController.swift:852-873`, whose entire user-facing output is `NSLog("Isla: Now Playing helper unavailable, falling back to Music/Spotify scripting")` (line 859). `rg -in 'quarantine|xattr' Sources/` returns nothing — the app never checks and never tells anyone.
    fix: Two layers. (1) The real fix: ship a Developer ID-signed, notarized build — `Scripts/release.sh:62-77` already notarizes and staples the app before packaging, so this is credentials, not code. Notarized nested code is exempt from this policy entirely. (2) A cheap, honest safety net that should exist regardless: in `NowPlayingFeed.launch()` (`NowPlayingFeed.swift:184`), before spawning perl, read `com.apple.quarantine` off `helperPath` via `getxattr` and check for the 0x40 bit; if it is missing, call a new `onBlockedByGatekeeper` instead of `onUnavailable`, and have `MediaPane`/`SettingsPane` render one inline row — "macOS is blocking Isla's Now Playing helper" with a button that runs `removexattr(XATTR_NAME, ...)` recursively over `Bundle.main.bundleURL` and restarts the feed. Add a `Scripts/test-package.sh` assertion that the signed dylib's cdhash is stapled once notarization is live.
    challenges: Mechanism reproduced locally: copying build/Isla.app/Contents/Resources/libislamedia.dylib to /tmp, setting com.apple.quarantine '0081;...;Safari;<uuid>' and running the app's exact loader (`perl -e 'use DynaLoader; DynaLoader::dl_load_file($ARGV[0],0x01)'`, Sources/IslaKit/Services/NowPlayingFeed.swift:191-196) fails with "code signature ... not valid for use in process: library load disallowed by system policy", while the clean copy and a copy flagged 00c1 (user-approved bit) load. The blocked helper then sits silent, NowPlayingFeed.swift:164-167 watchdog (silenceTimeout=12s, :83) restarts it, NowPlayingFailurePolicy.swift:44-46 returns .fallback after 3 consecutive failures, MediaController.swift:177 -> switchToScriptingFallback() at :852-873 whose only output is NSLog at :859. `grep -rin 'quarantine|xattr' Sources/` is empty and Resources/en.lproj/Localizable.strings has no helper/unavailable string, so nothing is shown to the user. README.md:26 carries the `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` fix. Two exaggerations: (a) the scripting fallback DOES fetch artwork — MediaController.swift:897-903 calls PlayerBridge.artwork(for:) (PlayerBridge.swift:237); only supported-command data is lost (MediaController.swift:856-858 hardcodes canSkip = true); (b) the cited evidence measured quarantine flags on a copy that was never approved, so "still unapproved after System Settings approval" is inferred, not demonstrated. | corrected: On a downloaded install the quarantined nested dylib cannot be loaded into /usr/bin/perl (verified: 'library load disallowed by system policy'), so after ~3 silent restarts Isla falls back to AppleScript for Music/Spotify only — losing every non-scriptable player and all supported-command data (artwork is still fetched via PlayerBridge.artwork), raising an Apple Events prompt, and saying nothing in the UI. Whether System Settings 'Open Anyway' leaves the nested dylib unapproved was not measured; only `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` (README:26) is proven to fix it. ;; Mechanism independently reproduced: I copied libislamedia.dylib out of the shipped v0.1.0 DMG, set com.apple.quarantine flags 0281, and ran the exact loader from Sources/IslaKit/Services/NowPlayingFeed.swift:191-196 — 'code signature ... not valid for use in process: library load disallowed by system policy'; the same dylib with the user-approved bit (00c1) or no xattr loads. A sweep of /Applications confirms nested files keep unapproved flags (0183/0381) after launch, so app approval does not blanket-propagate. No quarantine handling exists (grep 'quarantine|xattr' Sources/ → nothing), feedAvailable is never surfaced in the UI (only MediaController.swift:48-854), and the only trace is NSLog at MediaController.swift:859. Two things inflate it: PlayerBridge.swift:237-255 DOES fetch artwork for both Music and Spotify, so the fallback is not artwork-less; and README:26 plus the live release body both lead with `xattr -dr`, which fixes it — only users who instead take System Settings → Open Anyway are hit. On that path the app still launches and shows now-playing with artwork and transport for the two most common players. | corrected: major — on the System Settings 'Open Anyway' install path the primary media route dies silently (~40s of dead media per launch: silenceTimeout=12s × 3 failures with 2s/4s backoff), leaving Music/Spotify-only coverage plus an unexpected Apple Events prompt and no supported-command data. Artwork is NOT lost (PlayerBridge.swift:237), and the documented install step (xattr -dr, README:26 and the release body) avoids it entirely — so it degrades the app rather than stopping use or trust.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/M) Ad-hoc signature, unsigned DMG, and install copy that contradicts the project's own script
    today: On macOS 15 and 26 a double-click yields "Apple could not verify \"Isla\" is free of malware…" with only Move to Trash / Done. Control-click → Open, which the README recommends, was removed in Sequoia for exactly this case — so the user follows the instructions, they do not work, and they are stuck. The remaining path is System Settings → Privacy & Security → scroll → "Open Anyway" → admin authentication → a second confirmation sheet. The published alternative is `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/Isla.app`, i.e. asking a stranger to paste a Gatekeeper-disarming Terminal command into their Mac.
    apple: Apple's documented distribution path for non-App-Store Mac apps is Developer ID + notarization + stapling; the user drags and double-clicks and nothing appears at all. Apple explicitly removed the Control-click bypass in macOS 15 to push developers onto that path, and never asks users to run `xattr`.
    impact: Most people stop at the malware warning. The ones who continue are taught to strip quarantine by hand — a habit that harms them elsewhere. Compounding it: because the build has no team identity, `TokenStore` (Sources/IslaKit/Services/TokenStore.swift:14-31, 99-106) cannot use the data-protection keychain, so every shipped user's Spotify refresh token lands in a plaintext `spotify-credentials.json` instead. A paid competitor is one double-click.
    evidence: `codesign -dvvv` on the shipped app: `flags=0x10002(adhoc,runtime)`, `Signature=adhoc`, `TeamIdentifier=not set`; `spctl -a -vv` → `rejected`. `codesign -dvvv Isla-0.1.0.dmg` → `code object is not signed at all`. `Scripts/bundle.sh:113` `SIGN_IDENTITY="${DEVELOPER_ID_APPLICATION:--}"` defaults to ad-hoc and that is what shipped. `Scripts/dmg.sh:64-74` prints a warning that itself states "В macOS 15 открытие через Control-клик для такого случая больше не работает" — yet README:31-32 tells users "the command above, or right-click → Open, is the way in", and the live GitHub release body repeats it. `checklist.md:127` still has `- [ ] Developer ID signing, notarization, stapling, and Gatekeeper validation pass.` unchecked.
    fix: Buy the $99 Apple Developer membership, install a Developer ID Application certificate, create `docs/releases/0.1.1.md`, export `DEVELOPER_ID_APPLICATION`/`APPLE_ID`/`APPLE_TEAM_ID`/`APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD`, and run `bash Scripts/release.sh` — the script already does the whole sequence correctly. Then rewrite README:21-34 and the release body to "download, drag, open" and delete the `xattr` and right-click instructions entirely. Add a gate `Scripts/test-gatekeeper.sh` running `spctl -a -vv --type exec` plus `xcrun stapler validate` on both app and DMG, and wire it into `release.sh` after line 94.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/L) There is no updater, no version check, and no way for a shipped bug to ever reach a user
    today: Once someone installs 0.1.0 they are on 0.1.0 forever. There is no background check, no notification, no "Check for Updates…" row, and no in-app hint that a newer build exists. The only discovery channel is the user independently revisiting a GitHub releases page for an app that deliberately has no Dock icon and no menu-bar item to remind them it exists.
    apple: Apple ships Software Update for its own software and, for direct-download developers, expects an in-app update path; every named competitor (NotchNook, Alcove, DynamicLake Pro, MediaMate) integrates Sparkle and updates in one click. Apple's own HIG position is that keeping software current must not be the user's chore.
    impact: Every bug you fix is invisible to everyone who already installed. Worse, in the current unsigned world an "update" means re-downloading a DMG and repeating the whole Gatekeeper/xattr ritual — so even a motivated user will not bother. This is the difference between an app someone keeps and a one-time curiosity.
    evidence: `rg -in 'sparkle|appcast|updater|checkForUpdate|SUFeed' .` over the whole tree returns only an unrelated `sparkles` SF Symbol name in `Tests/IslaKitTests/SettingsIconTests.swift:40,44` and `Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsIcon.swift:12`. `Package.swift` has no dependencies. `Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift` sections are General, Screenshots, Music, Spotify, Privacy, Application — the Application section (line 243-252) contains only About and Quit. The only read of the version anywhere is `Sources/IslaKit/App/AppDelegate.swift:120-124` (`Bundle.shortVersion`) and `Sources/IslaKit/Services/LyricsStore.swift:419` (User-Agent string).
