    fix: Add Sparkle 2 as a SwiftPM dependency in `Package.swift`, embed `Sparkle.framework` in `Scripts/bundle.sh` (sign it nested, before the bundle, in the same inside-out order as the helper dylib at bundle.sh:188-201), add `SUFeedURL` and `SUPublicEDKey` to the Info.plist heredoc at bundle.sh:36-75, and expose a single "Check for Updates…" `actionRow` in `SettingsPane`'s Application section next to About. Generate the appcast in `Scripts/release.sh` after the GitHub release is created (line 148) and publish it to the same repo's Pages. Sparkle requires monotonic `CFBundleVersion` — see the bundle-metadata finding. Add `Scripts/test-appcast.sh` asserting the feed parses and its newest item matches `Scripts/version`.
    challenges: Mechanism confirmed: grep for sparkle|appcast|updater|checkForUpdate|SUFeed across the tree hits only the 'sparkles' SF Symbol in SettingsIcon.swift:12 and SettingsIconTests.swift:40,44; Package.swift declares no external dependencies; SettingsPane.swift:243-254 'Application' section contains only About and Quit. So there is genuinely no update path and no in-app version surface. But nothing here stops a user from using or trusting 0.1.0 today — the app is fully functional; the cost is that a shipped bug can never reach an installed user, which is a servicing gap rather than a user-facing failure. | corrected: major
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) The shipped binary is arm64-only while the README and release notes promise Intel support
    today: An Intel Mac running Sequoia — still a large, supported population — downloads the DMG on the strength of the stated compatibility, drags the app across, and gets "You can't open the application \"Isla\" because it is not supported on this type of Mac." There is no fallback and no explanatory text.
    apple: Apple ships universal binaries for every app it distributes outside the App Store and shipped Rosetta specifically so the transition would never be a user's problem. Xcode's default release configuration for a Mac app is `arm64 x86_64`.
    impact: Every Intel Mac user who follows the README hits a hard wall after a download and a Gatekeeper fight. It also makes the published compatibility claim false, which is the exact kind of thing that costs trust in a first release.
    evidence: `lipo -info` on the app inside the downloaded v0.1.0 DMG: `Non-fat file: .../MacOS/Isla is architecture: arm64`; same for `Contents/Resources/libislamedia.dylib`. `Scripts/bundle.sh:28-29` runs plain `swift build -c release` with no `--arch arm64 --arch x86_64`, and `clang` at bundle.sh:101-105 passes no `-arch` flags either. README:10 says "**macOS 15+** (Sequoia), Apple Silicon or Intel." and the live release body repeats "macOS 15+ (Sequoia), Apple Silicon or Intel." No gate checks architecture — `Scripts/test-identity.sh` and `Scripts/test-package.sh` verify plist keys and file presence only.
    fix: In `Scripts/bundle.sh:28-29` build universal: `swift build -c "$CONFIG" --arch arm64 --arch x86_64 --package-path "$ROOT"` (the `--show-bin-path` call needs the same flags). Add `-arch arm64 -arch x86_64` to the `clang -dynamiclib` invocation at bundle.sh:101-105. Then add to `Scripts/test-package.sh` a hard assertion: `lipo -archs "$APP/Contents/MacOS/Isla" | grep -q arm64 && ... | grep -q x86_64`, and the same for the dylib. If you would rather ship arm64-only, fix README:10 and the release notes to say "Apple Silicon only" — but the plist has no `LSRequiresNativeExecution`/`LSArchitecturePriority` hint either, so the failure stays ungraceful.
- [DISPUTED] (major/M) A UI-less app with no crash reporting, no relaunch, and no way to tell it died
    today: If Isla crashes, the island simply vanishes from the notch. There is no Dock icon that stops bouncing, no menu-bar item that disappears, no window that closes — the user's only signal is that the notch stopped responding, which is indistinguishable from "I clicked slightly wrong". Recovery requires opening Applications and launching it again, if they work out that it quit at all. On your side, you learn nothing: no crash report reaches you, and the six NSLog sites that explain *why* media broke are buried in the unified log with no way for a user to hand them over.
    apple: Apple's own agent-style processes are relaunched by launchd and report through CrashReporter, and Apple asks once for permission to share diagnostics. Where a background feature degrades, first-party UI says so in place (Wi-Fi menu, Bluetooth menu, Focus). Third-party menu-bar utilities near-universally ship a "Copy diagnostics" or "Report an issue" affordance.
    impact: Silent death of an app that has no visible presence reads as "unreliable" and cannot be diagnosed by either side. Bug reports arrive as "it stopped working sometimes" with nothing attached, so they never get fixed — which is also why the missing updater hurts twice.
    evidence: `rg -in 'NSSetUncaughtExceptionHandler|crashlytics|sentry|CrashReport|telemetry|analytics' Sources/` returns nothing. The app is `LSUIElement` (`Scripts/bundle.sh:66`) with, per CLAUDE.md and confirmed by `SettingsPane.swift:243-252`, no Dock icon, no menu-bar item and no window. `NSSupportsAutomaticTermination`/`NSSupportsSuddenTermination` are both `false` (bundle.sh:68-69) but nothing restarts the process. Every failure path in the media stack ends at `NSLog`: `NowPlayingFeed.swift:167` ("helper went silent; restarting"), `:228` ("helper failed to launch"), `MediaController.swift:859`, `AppPaths.swift:34`, `AppPaths.swift:54`, `TokenStore.swift:164`. There is no log-export or "Report a Problem" affordance anywhere in `SettingsPane`.
    fix: Three small pieces, none of which need a third-party SDK or network. (1) Route the existing NSLog sites through `os.Logger(subsystem: ProductIdentity.bundleIdentifier, category:)` so they land in a filterable subsystem. (2) Add a "Copy Diagnostics" `actionRow` to `SettingsPane`'s Application section that writes app version, macOS version, architecture, notch/display geometry, `TokenStore.backing`, `MediaController.feedAvailable`, the helper's quarantine state and the last N `OSLogStore` entries for the subsystem onto the pasteboard. (3) Register a login-independent relaunch: either `SMAppService.agent` with a `KeepAlive` plist, or at minimum an `NSSetUncaughtExceptionHandler` that logs the stack before dying. Explicitly do *not* add analytics — `docs/plans/2026-08-18-dynamic-island-parity-design.md:331` lists it as a non-goal and that restraint is correct.
    challenges: The code half is exactly right: `rg -in 'NSSetUncaughtExceptionHandler|crashlytics|sentry|CrashReport|telemetry|analytics' Sources/` returns nothing, every media failure ends at NSLog (NowPlayingFeed.swift:167,227,321; MediaController.swift:859; AppPaths.swift:34,54; TokenStore.swift:164), and SettingsPane has no log-export or report affordance. But the Apple half overstates: macOS's own ReportCrash shows a "quit unexpectedly" alert with Reopen for application crashes (Apple QA1288 distinguishes applications from background processes; LSUIElement apps are applications, not LSBackgroundOnly daemons) and writes an .ips into ~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports that the user can hand over. | corrected: Isla ships no crash reporting, no telemetry, no uncaught-exception handler, and no way to export the six NSLog explanations of why media broke — and Apple delivers no crash reports to a Developer ID direct-download developer even after notarization. But on an actual crash macOS itself posts an "Isla quit unexpectedly" alert with a Reopen button and files a local .ips report, so the user is not entirely without signal or a one-click relaunch; the true gaps are silent non-crash degradation (helper dead, fallback active) and having no channel to receive diagnostics. NSSupportsAutomaticTermination/NSSupportsSuddenTermination (bundle.sh:68-69) are unrelated to restart-after-crash and should not be cited as evidence for it. ;; Facts hold: grep for NSSetUncaughtExceptionHandler|crashlytics|sentry|CrashReport|telemetry|analytics over Sources/ returns nothing, the app is LSUIElement (bundle.sh:66) with only About and Quit in Settings (SettingsPane.swift:243-254), and all 12 NSLog sites are the sole record of media failures with no export affordance. But the severity rests on a crash that is not demonstrated to occur — this is diagnosability, not a defect users hit; SMAppService 'Launch at Login' (SettingsPane.swift:283-289) restores the app at next login; and macOS's own CrashReporter, not the app, owns the crash notification. No user notices or complains about the absence of telemetry. | corrected: minor
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) Trashing the app leaves a live Spotify refresh token in plaintext and a dangling login item
    today: Drag Isla to the Trash and a valid, non-expiring Spotify refresh token stays on disk in readable JSON indefinitely, with no revocation and no prompt. `~/Pictures/Isla` — which can hold 200 screenshots of whatever passed through the clipboard — stays in the user's own Pictures folder. And because `SMAppService.mainApp` was registered against a bundle that no longer exists, System Settings → General → Login Items retains an orphaned entry the user cannot easily explain or clean up.
    apple: Apple's guidance for direct-download Mac apps is that removal is dragging to the Trash, and apps that hold credentials or user media are expected to offer a way out. Apple's own apps revoke tokens on sign-out, and macOS deliberately makes login-item registrations user-visible so an app leaving a stale one is a defect, not a footnote.
    impact: A privacy-forward app — the README devotes a whole section to what leaves the machine — leaves a live account credential behind when you remove it. That is exactly the fact a security-conscious reviewer will find and lead with, and it undoes the credibility the rest of the privacy story earns.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/TokenStore.swift:42` writes `spotify-credentials.json` into `AppPaths.supportDirectory` = `~/Library/Application Support/Isla` (`AppPaths.swift:9-10`); `TokenStore.writeFileValues` (`:152-166`) writes a `JSONEncoder`-encoded `[String: String]` — plaintext — then chmods `0o600`. Because the shipped build is ad-hoc (`TeamIdentifier=not set`), `TokenStore.backing` (`:99-106`) resolves to `.file` for every real user, so this is the shipping path, not a dev path. Also left behind: `~/Pictures/Isla` (`AppPaths.swift:16-20`, up to 200 PNGs, `ScreenshotVault.retentionLimit = 200`), `~/Library/Application Support/Isla/Dropped/*` and the lyrics cache (`LyricsStore.cacheLimit = 500`, `LyricsStore.swift:576`), `~/Library/Preferences/com.ctimothe.isla.plist` (~15 keys across `NotchViewModel.swift:234-368`, `ShelfStore.swift`, `PrivacyMode.swift`), keychain items, and an `SMAppService.mainApp` registration (`SettingsPane.swift:283-289`). `rg -in 'uninstall|resetAll|Clear All Data' Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift` → nothing; Settings offers only "Clear Screenshots Folder", "Clear Lyrics Cache" and "Disconnect Spotify Account".
    fix: Add an "Uninstall Isla…" `confirmingRow` to `SettingsPane`'s Application section (beside About/Quit at `SettingsPane.swift:243-252`) that, on confirm: calls `SpotifyAccount.disconnect()` (which already hits `TokenStore.delete`, `TokenStore.swift:90-96`) so the token is revoked rather than orphaned; `SMAppService.mainApp.unregister()`; trashes `AppPaths.live.supportDirectory` and `AppPaths.live.screenshotDirectory` via `FileManager.trashItem` (reversible, matching the existing screenshot-clearing behavior at README:64); `UserDefaults.standard.removePersistentDomain(forName: ProductIdentity.bundleIdentifier)`; then `NSWorkspace.shared.recycle` on `Bundle.main.bundleURL` and terminate. Document the leftovers in README's privacy section either way. Longer term, encrypting `spotify-credentials.json` is moot — Developer ID signing moves it into the data-protection keychain, so finding #2 fixes this at the root.
    challenges: Mechanism confirmed: TokenStore.swift:42 writes spotify-credentials.json into AppPaths.supportDirectory (AppPaths.swift:9-10); writeFileValues (TokenStore.swift:152-166) JSON-encodes plaintext then chmods 0o600; TokenStore.backing (:99-106) probes the data-protection keychain, which cannot succeed on the shipped ad-hoc build (TeamIdentifier=not set, verified on the released app), so .file is the real shipping path — the code comment at TokenStore.swift:31 claiming it 'never applies to a build anybody ships' is wrong, and I found no 'backing' string in any UI file, so it is not surfaced either. Leftovers (ScreenshotVault.retentionLimit=200, LyricsStore.cacheLimit=500, prefs, SMAppService registration) and the absence of any reset/uninstall row in SettingsPane are real. But this only bites users who connected Spotify and then uninstalled; leftover Application Support and prefs are ordinary Mac behavior, and no user names a residual refresh token. | corrected: minor
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) The good release script has never run; v0.1.0 was published by hand and CI ships nothing
    today: The publishing path that encodes all the hard-won ordering knowledge — notarize before packaging, tag after notarization, roll the tag back on failure, publish a checksum — is dead code. What actually ships is whatever was in `build/` at the time, uploaded manually, with no checksum in the notes and no proof the gates ran against that exact binary. CI catches regressions but produces nothing anyone can install or verify.
    apple: Not a HIG matter, but it is the baseline every paid competitor meets: a reproducible signed pipeline, a published checksum, and a downloadable artifact tied to a specific commit.
    impact: Users have no way to verify the DMG they downloaded is the one you built (GitHub's asset digest is the only check, and the notes never mention it), and you have no way to prove the shipped binary passed the gates. On the next release, the manual path will drift again.
    evidence: `git tag -l` in the worktree is empty, yet `gh release view v0.1.0 --repo ctimothe/isla-mac` shows a release published 2026-08-28 with `Isla-0.1.0.dmg` attached — so the tag and upload did not come from `Scripts/release.sh:102-147`. `ls docs/` shows no `releases/` directory, so `release.sh:19` (`[ -s "$NOTES" ] || fail "release notes are required at docs/releases/$VERSION.md"`) would abort the script on its second check. The release body contains no SHA-256, which `release.sh:139-141` would have appended. `.github/workflows/build.yml:13-23` runs every gate and builds a DMG but has no `actions/upload-artifact` step and no signing, so CI output is discarded. `docs/runbook.md:238,247,251` still instructs `Create docs/releases/0.6.5.md` and describes tagging `v0.6.5` — stale against `Scripts/version` = `0.1.0`. `checklist.md:132-137` lists "Release notes exist at docs/releases/<version>.md" and "Scripts/release.sh completes without bypassing a gate" as unchecked.
    fix: Create `docs/releases/` and write `docs/releases/0.1.1.md` so `release.sh:19` can pass. Update `docs/runbook.md:238-251` to name the real version instead of 0.6.5. Add `actions/upload-artifact@v4` to `.github/workflows/build.yml` after line 23 uploading `build/Isla-*.dmg` plus a `shasum -a 256` sidecar, so every push produces an inspectable build. Then add a tag-triggered release job that runs `Scripts/release.sh` with `DEVELOPER_ID_APPLICATION`/`APPLE_ID`/`APPLE_TEAM_ID`/`APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD` from repository secrets and an imported P12, so the manual path stops being reachable.
    challenges: Confirmed and strengthened: `git tag -l` in the worktree is empty while gh shows v0.1.0 published 2026-08-28, docs/releases does not exist (so Scripts/release.sh:19 would abort), the release body carries no SHA-256 that release.sh:139-141 would have appended, and .github/workflows/build.yml has no upload-artifact or signing step. Decisive new evidence: shasum -a 256 of the downloaded asset (289b9583…7911) is byte-identical to the local build/Isla-0.1.0.dmg — release.sh would have codesigned, notarized and stapled the image, producing different bytes, so the published DMG is a hand-uploaded local dmg.sh output. Still, the only user-facing consequence is that an unsigned download ships without a published checksum; the rest is process risk. | corrected: minor
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The whole media path hangs on /usr/bin/perl, a runtime Apple has been removing, with no health surface
    today: The app's differentiating capability — Now Playing for *any* app, not just Music and Spotify — rests on one undocumented, deprecated host binary at a fixed path. If a macOS release removes `/usr/bin/perl`, gives it library validation, or changes what `mediaremoted` trusts, Isla degrades to two apps with no explanation to the user and no signal to you (see the crash-reporting finding: the only trace is `NSLog` at `MediaController.swift:859`). `Scripts/test-helper.sh` verifies the helper answers `get` on the build machine's current OS; nothing verifies it after an OS update on a user's Mac.
    apple: Apple does not build features on deprecated runtimes at fixed paths, and where a capability may be unavailable it states so — the media controls in Control Center simply do not appear rather than silently controlling a different app than the one shown.
    impact: Not broken today, but a single macOS point release can take the app's headline feature away from every installed copy at once — and with no updater, no crash reporting, and no in-app status, you would find out from a review rather than from telemetry, and could not ship the fix to anyone.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/NowPlayingFeed.swift:185` guards only `FileManager.default.isExecutableFile(atPath: "/usr/bin/perl")`; `:191` hard-codes `URL(fileURLWithPath: "/usr/bin/perl")`; `:192-197` passes `use DynaLoader; DynaLoader::dl_load_file($ARGV[0], 0x01)`. `Sources/IslaMediaHelper/helper.m:9-13` documents why: perl is a platform binary (`codesign -dvvv /usr/bin/perl` → `Platform identifier=26`, `Identifier=com.apple.perl`) signed without library validation, and `mediaremoted` trusts it. On this machine that is perl 5.34.1 under macOS 26.5.2 — still present, but Apple has flagged the bundled scripting runtimes for removal since Catalina and already removed Python 2 in 12.3. When it goes, `:186-188` calls `onUnavailable?()` → the AppleScript fallback for Music and Spotify only.
    fix: Do not try to remove the dependency — the design doc's reasoning is sound and there is no supported alternative. Instead make the failure legible and recoverable: (1) surface it, one line in `MediaPane` and a status row in `SettingsPane` reading which route is live ("Now Playing helper" vs "Music & Spotify only"), driven by the existing `MediaController.feedAvailable` flag (`MediaController.swift:853-854`); (2) generalize the host — replace the hard-coded string at `NowPlayingFeed.swift:185,191` with an ordered candidate list (`/usr/bin/perl`, `/usr/bin/ruby` if present, and any future platform binary without library validation), trying each before declaring unavailability; (3) add a `Scripts/test-helper.sh` assertion that `codesign -dvvv` on the chosen host still reports a platform identifier, so a CI run on a new macOS beta fails loudly instead of a user discovering it.
    challenges: Design fact confirmed: NowPlayingFeed.swift:185 guards only isExecutableFile('/usr/bin/perl'), :191 hard-codes that path, :192-197 passes the DynaLoader one-liner, and helper.m:9-15 documents the platform-binary rationale. The 'no health surface' half is real today — feedAvailable never reaches the UI and MediaController.swift:859 is the whole user-facing output — but that is the same defect already charged in the quarantine finding. The removal risk itself is speculative: perl 5.34 is present and loads the shipped dylib on macOS 26.5 here, and Apple has not removed it. | corrected: minor (a forward risk plus a health-surface gap already counted under the quarantine finding, not a defect users hit today)
- [DISPUTED] (minor/S) CFBundleVersion duplicates the marketing version, and the About panel's copyright line reads "MIT License"
    today: Two different builds of "0.1.0" are indistinguishable to LaunchServices and to any updater, and Sparkle compares `CFBundleVersion` — so adding an updater later will require this fixed first. The standard About panel, the app's only formal identity surface, prints "MIT License" where macOS expects a copyright line, so it reads as "Isla / Version 0.1.0 (0.1.0) / MIT License". `LSApplicationCategoryType` absent means Finder and Launchpad have no category for it. (A privacy manifest is genuinely *not* required for direct download — only App Store submissions enforce it — so this is worth adding for hygiene, not compliance.)
    apple: Apple's own apps carry a monotonically increasing `CFBundleVersion` distinct from the marketing string, a real `NSHumanReadableCopyright` ("Copyright © 2026 …"), and a category. The About panel is a first-party surface with a defined shape, and Apple fills every field it defines.
    impact: Small but visible: the About sheet — the one place a curious or cautious user looks to confirm what they installed — has a malformed line. The version duplication is invisible today and becomes a blocker the moment you add Sparkle.
    evidence: `Scripts/bundle.sh:63-64` writes `CFBundleShortVersionString` and `CFBundleVersion` from the same `$VERSION`, both `0.1.0` in the shipped plist. `bundle.sh:72` sets `NSHumanReadableCopyright` to `MIT License`. There is no `LSApplicationCategoryType` and no `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` (`find . -name 'PrivacyInfo*'` → nothing). `Scripts/test-identity.sh:10-13` and `Scripts/test-package.sh:17-20` check name/id/executable/`LSMinimumSystemVersion` but never version monotonicity or the copyright string. The About sheet is `NSApp.orderFrontStandardAboutPanel(nil)` (`SettingsPane.swift:245`), which reads exactly these keys.
    fix: In `Scripts/bundle.sh`, read a second `BUILD=` line from `Scripts/version` (or use `git rev-list --count HEAD`) and emit it as `CFBundleVersion` at line 64, keeping `CFBundleShortVersionString` as the marketing string. Change line 72 to `<key>NSHumanReadableCopyright</key><string>Copyright © 2026 Isla. MIT-licensed.</string>` — both English and Russian users see this, so add it to the `.lproj` tables via `InfoPlist.strings` if you want it localized. Add `<key>LSApplicationCategoryType</key><string>public.app-category.utilities</string>`. Extend `Scripts/test-identity.sh` with an assertion that `CFBundleVersion` is strictly greater than the previous tag's, so a release cannot ship a build number that already exists. Optionally add `Resources/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` declaring `NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryUserDefaults` (CA92.1) and `…FileTimestamp` (C617.1, used by `AppPaths.pruneDropInbox` and `ScreenshotVault.trimToLimit`) and copy it into `Contents/Resources`.
    challenges: The substantive halves check out: bundle.sh:63-64 writes CFBundleShortVersionString and CFBundleVersion from the same $VERSION (shipped plist has both = 0.1.0, so LaunchServices and any Sparkle-style CFBundleVersion comparison cannot order two builds), bundle.sh:72 sets NSHumanReadableCopyright to "MIT License" which `orderFrontStandardAboutPanel` (SettingsPane.swift:~245) renders in the copyright position, `find . -name 'PrivacyInfo*'` is empty, `rg LSApplicationCategoryType` is empty, and neither test-identity.sh nor test-package.sh checks version monotonicity or the copyright string. The privacy-manifest caveat is correct (enforced for App Store submission, not direct download). The LSApplicationCategoryType consequence is wrong about Apple's software. | corrected: LSApplicationCategoryType is optional outside the Mac App Store, and Finder does not group or label apps by category at all — nor does Launchpad on macOS 15. Its absence has no visible effect for a direct-download app today; it matters for App Store submission and for the App Store / newer system app-browsing surfaces that read the key. Keep the version-key and copyright-string findings, drop the Finder/Launchpad rationale. ;; All three facts verified in the plist of the shipped app: CFBundleShortVersionString and CFBundleVersion are both 0.1.0 (bundle.sh:63-64), NSHumanReadableCopyright = 'MIT License' (bundle.sh:72), and there is no LSApplicationCategoryType and no PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy. But the version duplication has zero effect until an updater exists (and there is none), the missing category is invisible for an LSUIElement direct-download app, and the About-panel copyright line is exactly the kind of Apple-parity detail no user would ever name — the claim itself concedes the privacy manifest is not required. | corrected: polish
- [CONFIRMED] (polish/M) The disk image is an unstyled Finder list — the first ten seconds of the product
    today: Opening the DMG gives a default Finder window — plain white, icons wherever Finder puts them, a generic disk icon in the sidebar, and no visual instruction that the gesture is "drag left onto right". The version in the volume name means the mounted volume is a different name every release.
    apple: Apple does not ship DMGs anymore, but the convention it established and that every notable Mac app follows — including all seven named competitors — is a fixed-size window with a background that draws the app icon, an arrow, and the Applications alias in fixed positions, plus a custom volume icon. It is the first thing a paying user sees and it sets the expectation for everything after.
    impact: The install window is the product's first impression and currently looks like an unfinished build artifact. Against an app someone already paid $25 for, this is where they decide whether the free one is a real alternative or a hobby project.
    evidence: `Scripts/dmg.sh:41-55` stages the app plus `ln -s /Applications` and calls `hdiutil create -volname "Isla $VERSION" -srcfolder "$STAGE" -fs HFS+ -format UDZO`. There is no `.DS_Store`, no window bounds or icon positions, no background image, no `.VolumeIcon.icns`, and no `SetFile -a C`. The image is signed only on the `release.sh` path (`release.sh:87`); `dmg.sh` on its own produces an unsigned image, which is what shipped (`codesign -dvvv Isla-0.1.0.dmg` → `code object is not signed at all`). The volume mounts as "Isla 0.1.0", version-stamped rather than stable.
    fix: Extend `Scripts/dmg.sh`: create the image read-write (`hdiutil create -format UDRW`), attach it, drop `.background/background.png` (a 2x asset in `Resources/`) and `.VolumeIcon.icns`, apply window bounds, icon size, background and item positions with an `osascript` Finder block, `SetFile -a C` the volume root, detach, then `hdiutil convert -format UDZO` to the final image. Use a stable `-volname "Isla"` so the mounted volume is not version-stamped. Sign the DMG unconditionally when `DEVELOPER_ID_APPLICATION` is set rather than only in `release.sh:87`. Add the background PNG to `Scripts/test-package.sh`'s asset list so it cannot silently go missing.

## i18n
verdict: The plumbing is better than most indie Mac apps: every in-app label goes through `localized(_:)` or a SwiftUI `LocalizedStringKey` (I cross-checked all 66 source files — zero hardcoded user-facing `Text`/`.help`/`.accessibilityLabel` literals), hot keys are registered by virtual keycode so a Cyrillic layout still fires ⌥⌘I (`GlobalHotKey.swift:31-35`), no view uses `.leading`/`.trailing` incorrectly or hardcodes `.left`/`.right` (zero matches), the screenshot filename stamp is deliberately pinned to `en_US_POSIX` with a comment explaining why (`ScreenshotVault.swift:51-64`), `ByteCountFormatter` is used for the folder size, and the Russian table is a real translation by someone who speaks Russian, not machine output. But the app is not internationalized — it is bilingualized. Two things break the illusion badly: the Translate tab is hard-wired to English↔Russian and routes *every* non-Cyrillic language into Russian, so a German, French, Spanish or Japanese user selecting text and hitting the Services item gets Russian back; and nothing outside `Localizable.strings` is localized at all — the Services menu title and the app's single permission prompt are baked English in `bundle.sh`, with no `ServicesMenu.strings` and no `InfoPlist.strings` in either `.lproj`, which is exactly the pair of files Apple's own TextEdit ships. Underneath that, `test-localizations.sh` only compares en to ru and never looks at the source, and the table has already visibly drifted: 12 dead keys including two from features removed in August, and a duplicate `"Done"` key that `plutil -lint` silently collapses. Against the paid competitors this is roughly parity on the shell and a liability on Translate — NotchNook and Alcove ship English-only and are honest about it; Isla ships a Translate tab that quietly assumes you speak Russian.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/M) Translate routes every non-Cyrillic language into Russian, and mislabels the source as "English"
    today: Any input without a Cyrillic character is declared to be English and translated into Russian. A German user pasting "Wo ist der Bahnhof?" gets Russian; the left column is headed "ENGLISH" over German text; the model is instructed "You translate English into Russian" while being handed German. There is no picker, no preference, no override — the only way to get a different pair is to be Russian.
    apple: macOS 26's system-wide Translate (Services > Translate, and the Translate app) detects the source language and translates into a target the user chose, defaulting to the user's own primary language from Language & Region; the target is a visible, persistent control, and the source shows "Detect Language" rather than asserting a language it did not detect. Apple's `Translation` framework's `TranslationSession.Configuration` takes an explicit target and never silently assumes one.
    impact: For everyone outside the ru/en pair — which is the whole addressable market for a paid $25-class notch app — the headline Translate tab, the ⌥⌘T hot key and the "Translate in Isla" Services item all produce Russian. It is not a degraded result, it is the wrong answer, delivered confidently with a header that names the wrong source language.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/Translator.swift:82-86`: `let cyrillic = text.unicodeScalars.contains { (0x0400...0x04FF).contains($0.value) }` / `return cyrillic ? Route(source: russian, target: english) : Route(source: english, target: russian)`. The two poles are the only ones that exist: `Translator.swift:19-20` `static let russian = Locale.Language(identifier: "ru")` / `static let english = ...`. The route feeds the model prompt verbatim at `Translator.swift:148-168` (`You translate \(Self.name(route.source)) into \(target)`), and the pane's column headers at `TranslatePane.swift:61` and `:119` are `Translator.name(translator.route.source/target)`. `TranslatorTests.swift:6-11` locks the behaviour in. Nothing anywhere reads `Locale.current` or `Locale.preferredLanguages` to pick a target.
    fix: Give `Translator` a stored target: `@AppStorage("translateTargetLanguage")` defaulting to `Locale.current.language.languageCode?.identifier ?? "en"`. Rewrite `Translator.route(for:)` to (a) take the stored target, (b) detect the source with `NLLanguageRecognizer` (or keep the Cyrillic shortcut only as a tiebreak for very short strings), and (c) invert to English when the detected source already equals the target — that is the "press it again to go the other way" rule the current Cyrillic test is really trying to express. Surface the target in `TranslatePane.column(_:)`'s header as a `Menu` over `Locale.Language` values the foundation model handles, and mark the source header "Detect Language" when confidence is low rather than printing `Translator.name(route.source)`. Update `TranslatorTests.testRouteUsesCyrillicToChooseDirection` to assert against a target parameter instead of the hardcoded pair.
    challenges: Facts fully confirmed: Translator.swift:83-86 routes on a Cyrillic-scalar test alone, with only two poles (Translator.swift:19-20); the route text feeds the model instruction verbatim (Translator.swift:148-155, 'You translate \(Self.name(route.source)) into \(target)') and the column headers via Translator.name at TranslatePane.swift:61/119, uppercased at TranslatePane.swift:219 — so German input really is labelled 'ENGLISH' and sent to Russian. TranslatorTests.swift:6-11 locks it in; no picker, no preference, nothing reads Locale.preferredLanguages. But severity is inflated: this is one of five tabs, gated behind macOS 26 + Apple Intelligence (Translator.swift:106-125 returns .needsNewerSystem/.deviceNotEligible otherwise), and Music, Shelf, Clipboard and Settings are unaffected. It breaks one feature for everyone outside the ru/en pair; it does not stop a user running or trusting the app. | corrected: major (rank it first among majors) — Translate is unusable and actively mislabelled for any pair other than ru<->en, and it is promoted app-wide via the ⌥⌘T hotkey and the system Services entry, but it is one optional tab behind an Apple Intelligence gate, not an app-level blocker.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) The Services menu item and the Apple Events consent prompt are baked English — no ServicesMenu.strings, no InfoPlist.strings
    today: On a Russian Mac the Services menu of every app shows "Translate in Isla" in English, sitting among Apple's translated entries. And when the AppleScript fallback first drives Music or Spotify, macOS puts up its consent alert with the app's purpose string verbatim — in English — even though the surrounding alert chrome is Russian.
    apple: Apple localizes the Services menu title via a per-localization `ServicesMenu.strings` keyed by the `NSMenuItem`/`default` value (TextEdit and Preview both ship one), and localizes Info.plist usage descriptions and display names via a per-localization `InfoPlist.strings`. The purpose string is the one sentence the user reads before granting or refusing automation access; Apple's own apps never present it in a foreign language.
    impact: A Russian user meets the app in English at two of its three system-integration points — the Services entry, which is one of only four ways into the app, and the single permission prompt it will ever raise. The permission case is the worse one: an English sentence in a Russian security alert is exactly what a cautious user denies.
    evidence: `Scripts/bundle.sh:54-61` writes `NSServices` with `<key>NSMenuItem</key><dict><key>default</key><string>Translate in Isla</string></dict>`, and `:70-71` writes `NSAppleEventsUsageDescription` as a fixed English sentence. `Scripts/bundle.sh:92-96` copies only `Resources/*.lproj`, and both `.lproj` folders contain exactly one file: `ls Resources/en.lproj Resources/ru.lproj` → `Localizable.strings` only. The built bundle matches: `build/Isla.app/Contents/Resources/{en,ru}.lproj/Localizable.strings` and nothing else. For contrast, on this machine `/System/Applications/TextEdit.app/Contents/Resources/ru.lproj/ServicesMenu.strings` exists and contains `"New TextEdit Window Containing Selection" => "Открыть выбранное в новом окне TextEdit"`; Preview.app ships one too.
    fix: Add `Resources/en.lproj/ServicesMenu.strings` and `Resources/ru.lproj/ServicesMenu.strings` with `"Translate in Isla" = "Перевести в Isla";` and add `InfoPlist.strings` to both with `NSAppleEventsUsageDescription` (and `CFBundleDisplayName` if it is ever anything but a proper noun). `Scripts/bundle.sh:92-96` already copies whole `.lproj` directories, so both files ship with no script change. Extend `Scripts/test-localizations.sh` to `plutil -lint` every `.strings` in every `.lproj` and to require the same file set in each locale, and extend `Scripts/test-package.sh`'s bundle contract to assert both files are present in the built app.
    challenges: Facts confirmed: bundle.sh:57 hardcodes the NSMenuItem default as 'Translate in Isla' and bundle.sh:70-71 hardcodes an English NSAppleEventsUsageDescription; bundle.sh:92-96 copies only Resources/*.lproj, and both source .lproj folders and the built bundle (build/Isla.app/Contents/Resources/{en,ru}.lproj) contain exactly one file, Localizable.strings — no InfoPlist.strings, no ServicesMenu.strings. Severity is inflated: the Services submenu is rarely opened, and the Apple Events alert fires once and only on the AppleScript fallback path (helper unavailable). A Russian user sees English text in two rarely-visited system surfaces; nobody abandons or distrusts the app over it, and nobody files it as a complaint. | corrected: minor — real Apple-native parity gap (TextEdit/Preview ship both files), cheap to fix, but confined to a rarely-seen menu entry and a one-time consent alert.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) test-localizations.sh only compares en to ru — it never checks the code, duplicates, or format specifiers
    today: The full release gate passes on a table that carries a duplicate key and 12 dead entries, two of them for features deleted a fortnight ago. Rename or typo a key in Swift and both tables still match each other, so every gate stays green while the Russian build silently falls back to printing the raw English key.
    apple: Xcode's string catalogs flag unused and missing keys at build time and surface a stale/needs-review state per string; `genstrings`/`xcstringstool` derive the table from the source rather than letting the two drift apart. Apple's localization tooling treats "the code says one thing and the table says another" as a build-visible error, not something you find by reading.
    impact: Not visible yet — but it is the failure mode this key scheme makes cheapest. The first key reworded in Swift and not in `ru.lproj` ships a Russian build with an English sentence in it, and every check in `.github/workflows/build.yml` passes. A `%d`/`%@` swap in a future Russian translation is a `String(format:)` crash (`Strings.swift:18`) with the same green gate.
    evidence: `Scripts/test-localizations.sh` does exactly two things: `plutil -lint` each file, then `assert english == russian` on two Python `set`s of keys. Consequences I verified: (1) 12 keys exist in both tables that no source file references, including `"notes.json cannot be read, so nothing is being saved."` (`en.lproj:76`) and `"Scroll Speed"` (`en.lproj:87`) — Notes and the Teleprompter were removed on 2026-08-22 — plus `"+%d more in Sound Settings"` (`:145`), `"Playing on this Mac"` (`:134`), `"Continue"`, `"Cancel"`, `"Connect Spotify"` and the Spotify Client-ID paste prompt (`:100`), whose dialog was replaced by `connectSpotify()` at `SettingsPane.swift:363-371`. (2) `"Done"` is defined twice in both files (`en.lproj:48` and `:64`, `ru.lproj:48` and `:64`); a `set` cannot see that, and I confirmed `plutil -lint` on a two-`"Done"` file returns 0 and `plutil -p` shows the last value winning. (3) No check that a `localized("…")` key in Sources exists in the table, and no check that `%` specifiers agree between en and ru (they all do today — I diffed all six format keys — so this one is latent, not live).
    fix: Extend the Python block in `Scripts/test-localizations.sh` to: (a) parse keys as a list, not a set, and fail on duplicates within a file; (b) scrape `localized("…")` and `Text("…")`/`Button("…")` literals out of `Sources/**/*.swift` and assert `used ⊆ table` (fail) and report `table − used` (fail, so removals are cleaned up with the feature — the two stale section comments already retitled on 2026-08-21 show this is a known drift); (c) compare the ordered list of `%`-specifiers between the key and each locale's value and fail on a mismatch. Then delete the 12 dead keys and collapse the duplicate `"Done"`. This is the gate that would have caught every other finding in this list.
    challenges: The gate's shape is confirmed — Scripts/test-localizations.sh only plutil -lints the two files and asserts set(en keys) == set(ru keys); it never reads Sources, never sees duplicates, never compares format specifiers. Sub-facts partly off: I count 10 dead keys, not 12 (the 8 named in the claim plus 'Delete'; 'Cancel'/'Connect Spotify' are indeed dead once you exclude substring matches like Task.isCancelled), and 'Done' is duplicated in both files (en.lproj:48/64, ru.lproj:48/64) but with identical values on both sides, so the last-wins behaviour is a no-op. Crucially I ran the check the gate is missing: every localized("…") key in Sources exists in the table today (zero misses), and the claim itself concedes the format-specifier risk is latent. So the live user-visible damage is nil; what remains is a real tooling gap that would let a future rename ship as a raw English key in the Russian build. | corrected: minor — a latent release-gate weakness plus dead-key/duplicate cruft, with zero user-visible defect today; worth fixing as a gate hardening item, not as a user-facing bug.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) Slider readouts use a C decimal point and untranslated unit abbreviations
    today: In the Russian build the Settings sliders read "0.35s" and "560 pt" — an English decimal point where Russian writes a comma, and two English unit abbreviations next to Cyrillic row titles ("Задержка открытия … 0.35s", "Ширина панели … 560 pt").
    apple: Every numeric readout in System Settings goes through a locale-aware formatter: a Russian Mac shows `0,35 с` with a comma and a non-breaking space before a translated unit. `Measurement<UnitDuration>(value:unit:).formatted(.measurement(width: .abbreviated))` produces exactly that, and `Duration`/`FormatStyle` handle the separator without the caller thinking about it.
    impact: Two rows in the one tab a Russian user opens to configure the app read as untranslated leftovers. It is small, but it lands on the settings screen — the surface where an app either looks finished or looks ported.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift:68`: `Text(String(format: "%.2fs", hoverDelay))` — `String(format:)` with no locale always emits `.` as the decimal separator. `SettingsPane.swift:96`: `Text("\(Int(bodyWidth)) pt")` — `pt` is a bare literal and appears in neither `Localizable.strings`; my key cross-check lists `'\(Int(bodyWidth)) pt'` as used-in-code-but-absent-from-the-table. Both sit in fixed 38pt trailing-aligned frames (`:70` and `:98`). Elsewhere the app does this right — `SettingsPane.swift:273` uses `ByteCountFormatter`, so the folder size is correctly localized.
    fix: Replace `SettingsPane.swift:68` with `Measurement(value: hoverDelay, unit: UnitDuration.seconds).formatted(.measurement(width: .abbreviated, usage: .asProvided, numberFormatStyle: .number.precision(.fractionLength(2))))`. For points there is no Foundation unit, so add a key `"%@ pt" = "%@ пт";` to both tables and call `localized("%@ pt", Int(bodyWidth).formatted())` so the number goes through `IntegerFormatStyle` and the unit through the table. Widen the two 38pt frames slightly, since "0,35 с" is wider than "0.35s".
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) Shelf tiles show the raw filename instead of the Finder display name
    today: The tile always prints the on-disk path component, extension included. On a Russian Mac, dragging the home Documents folder onto the Shelf shows "Documents" while Finder, the Dock and the sidebar all say "Документы"; dragging an app shows "TextEdit.app" rather than its localized display name; and with "Show all filename extensions" off, Isla shows ".pdf"/".png" where the rest of the system does not.
    apple: Finder, AirDrop, Stage Manager and every Apple drop target read `try url.resourceValues(forKeys: [.localizedNameKey]).localizedName`, which resolves `.localized` folder names, application display names from the app's own `InfoPlist.strings`, and the user's extension-visibility preference. The whole point of the key is that a shelf and the Finder never disagree about what a file is called.
    impact: A drop target that names files differently from the Finder they were dragged out of. On a localized Mac the two labels are in different languages for the standard folders users drag most.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/ShelfStore.swift:17`: `var name: String { url.lastPathComponent }`, rendered at `Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift:141` as `Text(item.name)` with `.lineLimit(2)`. Nothing in the file reads `URLResourceKey.localizedNameKey` — `grep -rn 'localizedName' Sources` returns nothing.
    fix: Change `ShelfStore.swift:17` to `var name: String { (try? url.resourceValues(forKeys: [.localizedNameKey]).localizedName) ?? url.lastPathComponent }`. Since `ShelfItem` is decoded from disk and the URL may be stale, keep the `lastPathComponent` fallback (it already covers the missing-file case the store handles at `:67-77`), and cache the resolved name alongside the icon rather than hitting the filesystem inside `body`. Add a case to `ShelfStoreTests` asserting the fallback survives a URL that no longer resolves.
- [DISPUTED] (minor/S) No .stringsdict anywhere; the copy is written around plurals rather than handling them
    today: English reads "Selected: 3" and the tab badge is a naked digit. Nothing is grammatically wrong in either language, but the only tool available for a count is `%d` glued to a colon.
    apple: Apple ships `.stringsdict`/string-catalog plural variants as a matter of course — `/System/Applications/Reminders.app/Contents/Resources/ru.lproj/` carries `PluralLocalizable.strings` and `TTRAccessibilityLocalizable.stringsdict` — and writes the copy as a sentence: Finder says "3 items selected" / "Выбрано объектов: 3", not "Selected: 3". Russian needs three forms (one/few/many) and `NSStringLocalizedFormatKey` is the only correct way to get them.
    impact: No user is hurt today. But the English copy is visibly not Apple's voice on the Shelf header, and the moment anyone writes "%d files" or "%d элемента" it is an outright grammar bug in Russian that `test-localizations.sh` will happily pass.
    evidence: `find . -name '*.stringsdict' -not -path './.build/*'` returns nothing. The three count-bearing keys deliberately place the number where no noun has to agree with it: `"Selected: %d" = "Выбрано: %d"` (`en.lproj:28` / `ru.lproj:27`, used at `ShelfPane.swift:95`), `"+%d more in Sound Settings" = "Ещё %d в настройках звука"` (`:145`, currently unused), and the bare-number badge at `NotchContentView.swift:420` `Text("\(value)")`. I checked all six `%`-format keys: none puts a countable noun after the number, so there is **no live Russian plural bug** — the tables dodge the problem rather than solving it.
    fix: Add `Resources/{en,ru}.lproj/Localizable.stringsdict` with a `%d items selected` entry (`NSStringLocalizedFormatKey` = `%#@count@`, en: one/other, ru: one/few/many/other), switch `ShelfPane.swift:95` to it, and have `Scripts/bundle.sh` keep copying the whole `.lproj` (it already does). Extend `Scripts/test-localizations.sh` to require that any key containing `%d` or `%lld` next to a word has a `.stringsdict` counterpart in every locale, so the infrastructure is a gate rather than a convention.
    challenges: The absence is real — find for *.stringsdict returns nothing — but the claim's own evidence establishes there is no consequence. I re-derived it independently: the six %-format keys are 'Clear Screenshots Folder (%@)' (en.lproj:22), 'About %@' (:25), 'Selected: %d' (:28), '%@ of %@' (:86), 'Open %@' (:131) and the unused '+%d more in Sound Settings' (:145); none places a countable noun after the number, and the tab badge at NotchContentView.swift:420 is a bare `Text("\(value)")`. Nothing on screen is grammatically wrong in either language today, so per the rubric this has no user-visible consequence. | corrected: not a finding — a latent infrastructure note for whenever a count-plus-noun string is first written; carry it as a backlog line, not a defect.
- [CONFIRMED] (polish/S) Adjacent Russian rows call the same folder two different things, and neither matches Apple's term
    today: One Settings section, read top to bottom: «Скриншоты» → «Сохранять скриншоты из буфера» → «Показать папку скриншотов» → «Очистить папку снимков». Two different nouns for the same folder in two adjacent rows.
    apple: Apple's Russian glossary is fixed and enforced: Screenshot is «Снимок экрана» throughout (the app is «Снимок экрана», the default filename is «Снимок экрана 2026-09-03 в 12.00.00»). «Скриншот» is colloquial and does not appear in Apple's UI. Apple's localization process runs against a term base precisely so two rows in one pane cannot disagree.
    impact: A Russian user reads the section and, for a beat, wonders whether "папку скриншотов" and "папку снимков" are two different folders. It is the specific tell of a translation done string-by-string without a glossary — which is what "keys are the English text, comment is empty" (`Strings.swift:13`) gives a translator.
    evidence: `Resources/ru.lproj/Localizable.strings:18-21`: `"Save Clipboard Screenshots" = "Сохранять скриншоты из буфера";` / `"Show Screenshots Folder" = "Показать папку скриншотов";` / `"Clear Screenshots Folder" = "Очистить папку снимков";` / `"Clear Screenshots Folder (%@)" = "Очистить папку снимков (%@)";`. All four render as consecutive rows inside one section — `SettingsPane.swift:107-131` builds `section(localized("Screenshots"))` containing exactly these rows in this order. The section header itself is `"Screenshots" = "Скриншоты"` (`ru.lproj:17`).
    fix: Settle on Apple's term and use it in all four values plus the section header: «Снимки экрана» / «Сохранять снимки экрана из буфера» / «Показать папку снимков экрана» / «Очистить папку снимков экрана». While there, note the same class of drift elsewhere and fix it in one pass: `"Clipboard" = "Буфер"` (`ru.lproj:7`) where Apple says «Буфер обмена» — the rail is narrow, so keep «Буфер» only if it is verified not to clip. Longer term, the empty `comment:` in `Strings.swift:13` is what makes this inevitable; if the tables ever move to a string catalog, carry a real comment per key.

## lockscreen-privacy
verdict: The privacy plumbing is genuinely better than most of the paid field and in two places better than it needed to be: `ClipboardStore` honours all three `org.nspasteboard` opt-out types before it reads a single byte (Services/ClipboardStore.swift:84-88, 148) and keeps history in memory only, so it is not storing passwords on disk; `Translator` runs on Apple's on-device FoundationModels with no network at all (Services/Translator.swift:1-16); lyrics default to off and use an ephemeral `URLSession` with no cookie jar (Model/NotchViewModel.swift:250-251, Services/LyricsStore.swift:82-88); there is zero telemetry, no analytics SDK, no updater phone-home; `NSWindow.sharingType = .none` defaults to on and, because `LockCardWindow` builds a `NotchPanel`, it correctly covers the lock card too (Notch/NotchPanel.swift:157-168, Notch/LockCardWindow.swift:73); global hotkeys use `RegisterEventHotKey` so the app never asks for Accessibility or Input Monitoring (App/GlobalHotKey.swift:5-11); the keychain path uses `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` (Services/TokenStore.swift:134); and PKCE is done properly, with `state`, constant-time comparison, single-use verifier and a serialized refresh (Services/SpotifyAccount.swift:113-160, 234-266). The team also already closed the obvious lock-screen hole — a click on the card can no longer raise the keyboard over the password field (Notch/NotchController.swift:678-686). Where it falls short of Apple is the *lock screen as a trust boundary*. Apple's model is that every capability exposed above the shield is individually gated and nothing that mutates an account or a system-wide setting is reachable without auth. Isla's lock card is default-on and offers, to anyone standing at a locked Mac: a write to the owner's Spotify library, a permanent change to the system default audio output device, and full track metadata plus lyrics — with exactly one all-or-nothing switch covering all of it. There is also a concrete SkyLight teardown-ordering defect that leaves the notch panel assigned to a private space that has just been destroyed under it. Fix those and the security posture is genuinely Apple-shaped; the remaining gap is distribution (unsigned, un-notarized, README teaches users to strip quarantine), which is the one thing every $25 competitor has already cleared.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/S) Anyone at your locked Mac can add or remove songs from your Spotify library
    today: The lock card is presented by default on every lock (Sources/IslaKit/Model/NotchViewModel.swift:296-299 defaults `showOnLockScreen` to on). If the owner has connected Spotify, the heart is live above the login shield: a person with physical access and no password can like or un-like the playing track as many times as they want, and each press can trigger a refresh-token rotation against the owner's account.
    apple: On iOS the lock-screen Now Playing widget offers transport and volume only — no library mutation, no account writes. Anything that touches an account requires Face ID/passcode, and each lock-screen capability is individually gated under Settings → Face ID & Passcode → Allow Access When Locked. Apple's rule is that the lock screen is a read-and-transport surface, never a write-to-my-account surface.
    impact: A colleague, a hotel cleaner or anyone who walks past your desk can silently edit your Spotify Liked Songs. It is low-damage but it is an unauthenticated write to a remote account from a locked machine, and it is the kind of thing that ends a security review of a paid app.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/LockScreenCard.swift:191 `.overlay(alignment: .topTrailing) { heart.padding(3) }` puts the heart on the lock card's artwork; :647 `Button { spotify.toggleSaved(trackID: id) }`; :660 `.task(id: id) { spotify.refreshSavedState(trackID: id) }`. `SpotifyAccount.toggleSaved` (Sources/IslaKit/Services/SpotifyAccount.swift:326-352) issues `PUT`/`DELETE https://api.spotify.com/v1/me/tracks?ids=…` with a Bearer token, and `freshAccessToken()` (:236-266) will spend and rotate the stored refresh token to get one. Nothing on either path consults `isLockedPresentation` or any lock state — `LockScreenCard` is not even given one (its only init parameter is `initialPane`, :38-42). The card's window deliberately answers clicks (Sources/IslaKit/Notch/LockCardWindow.swift:75-77).
    fix: Give `LockScreenCard` an explicit `isLocked: Bool` (default true, since the only production caller is `LockCardWindow.present`) and return `EmptyView()` from `heart` when it is set — the `@ViewBuilder` at :637 already branches, so add `!isLocked` to the existing `if spotify.isConnected, !spotify.apiBlocked, !spotify.tokenUnavailable` condition. Pass `isLocked: false` from the `DI_LOCK_PREVIEW` and render-test call sites so the existing `LockCardRenderTests`/`LockScreenCardRenderTests` still photograph it. Add a test in `Tests/IslaKitTests/LockedIslandIsInertTests.swift` asserting no account-mutating control exists on the locked card, in the same spirit as `TabContractTests`.
    challenges: Mechanism confirmed: LockScreenCard.swift:191 puts `heart` on the locked card's artwork, :647 calls `spotify.toggleSaved(trackID:)`, SpotifyAccount.swift:326-352 issues PUT/DELETE against /v1/me/tracks, and neither path nor LockCardWindow.swift:75-77 (`panel.ignoresMouseEvents = false`) consults lock state. But the blast radius is small: SpotifyAccount.swift:125 requests only `user-library-read user-library-modify`, so the ceiling is toggling the currently-playing track in Liked Songs — reversible, no data exfiltration beyond the heart's filled/hollow state, and only for the subset of users who opted into connecting Spotify. The token claim is also wrong: freshAccessToken (SpotifyAccount.swift:236-266) returns the cached access token while unexpired and coalesces concurrent refreshes into one task, so a press does not 'trigger a refresh-token rotation' each time. | corrected: major — a genuine lock-screen authorization hole (a locked Mac must not permit account writes) that should be fixed before charging money, but it does not stop anyone using or trusting the app: it needs physical access plus a connected Spotify account, and the worst outcome is a like/unlike a bystander can inflict on one song.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) The system's default audio output can be changed from the lock screen, and the change survives unlock
    today: Above the login shield, the output list is one click away and lists every device with output streams — built-in speakers, any connected or paired Bluetooth device, AirPlay/Continuity targets (`AudioOutputs.available()`, :28-49). Selecting one repoints the whole Mac's audio and nothing puts it back on unlock; `screenUnlocked()` (Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchController.swift:266-290) does not touch audio.
    apple: macOS gives no route to sound-output selection from the lock screen at all — Control Center and System Settings are both unreachable behind the shield. The hardware volume keys work while locked (which is why the volume slider here is defensible), but *routing* is not offered, precisely because it is a persistent system-wide change.
    impact: Someone at your locked Mac can push your audio to a Bluetooth speaker or an AirPlay device in the room and press play, or silently leave your output pointed at a device you did not choose so your next unlock has no sound where you expect it. You have no way to turn this off short of disabling the lock card entirely.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/LockScreenCard.swift:339 `private var outputPicker` renders over the card whenever `pane == .output`, opened by the footer glyph at :605-612; :415 `guard AudioOutputs.select(device.id) else { return }`. `AudioOutputs.select` (Sources/IslaKit/Services/AudioOutputs.swift:66-79) writes `kAudioHardwarePropertyDefaultOutputDevice` on `kAudioObjectSystemObject` — a system-wide, persistent setting, not an app-scoped one; the file's own doc admits this at :7-11 ("Switching the *system's* default output is the honest reading of that button"). `SystemVolume.set` is likewise driven from the locked card at :527.
    fix: Same `isLocked` parameter as the finding above: when locked, drop the output glyph from `footer` (:598-603) and never allow `pane = .output`, leaving the transport, scrubber and volume — the three things macOS's own media keys already permit while locked. If output switching on the lock screen is wanted as a feature, make it a Settings toggle in the Music section next to "Show on Lock Screen", default off, and record it in `checklist.md` §"Capabilities that exceed the parity design's non-goals" alongside the other system-wide capabilities already listed there.
    challenges: Facts confirmed: LockScreenCard.swift:339/415 and AudioOutputs.swift:66-79 write kAudioHardwarePropertyDefaultOutputDevice on kAudioObjectSystemObject from the locked card, SystemVolume.set is driven at :527, and screenUnlocked (NotchController.swift:266-290) never restores it. But the user-visible consequence is small and self-announcing: the change is audible, one click in System Settings or the same picker undoes it, `available()` (AudioOutputs.swift:28-49) only lists devices already paired/configured on that Mac so there is no eavesdropping vector, and macOS itself already lets anyone at a locked Mac change volume with the hardware keys. | corrected: minor — same lock-screen inertness violation as the Spotify heart, but local, reversible in one click, and with no data exposure; worth gating behind the lock state, not something a user would file a complaint about.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) One all-or-nothing lock-screen switch, and the comment justifying its default is factually wrong
    today: By default, every locked Mac shows the full track title, artist, album art with a source badge, a scrubber with elapsed/remaining time, and — if lyrics are on — the running words, to anyone in the room. There is no way to keep the island present but anonymous.
    apple: macOS's lock screen shows a clock, a wallpaper and a password field; it does not show a now-playing widget, so the comment's premise is false. Where Apple *does* put media on a lock screen (iOS), it ships per-capability gates under Allow Access When Locked, and separately lets notification previews be hidden while locked (Settings → Notifications → Show Previews → When Unlocked) precisely so a surface can be present without disclosing content.
    impact: You cannot have the island on your lock screen without also broadcasting what you are listening to. For anyone in an open-plan office or a shared flat the only available answer is to turn the whole feature off, which is the feature people install this app for.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/Model/NotchViewModel.swift:289-300 — `showOnLockScreenKey`, defaulting to on, with the justification "it shows nothing the lock screen does not already show — the same track macOS puts in its own now-playing widget there." `Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchController.swift:171` gates the entire lock behaviour on that one flag, so the pill, the card, the metadata, the lyrics, the scrubber, the output picker and the volume are a single switch. The card's header prints title and artist verbatim (Sources/IslaKit/UI/LockScreenCard.swift:150-166) and `lyricsPane` (:255-283) prints the words. The only other lock-screen setting is cosmetic — glass vs solid (Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift:165-181).
    fix: Split the one flag into two rows in the Music section of `SettingsPane`: "Show on Lock Screen" (the pill and card, as today) and "Hide Track Details When Locked" (default off). When the second is on, `LockScreenCard.header` renders `localized("Now Playing")` with the generic `music.note` placeholder instead of `track.title`/`track.artist`/`media.artwork`, and `lyricsPane` is suppressed — the transport, scrubber and volume stay, which is exactly the macOS media-key contract. Correct the doc comment at NotchViewModel.swift:291-295 in the same change; it is currently asserting a macOS behaviour that does not exist.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) On unlock the private SkyLight space is destroyed while the notch panel is still in it
    today: The notch panel is moved out of its normal spaces by `SLSSpaceAddWindowsAndRemoveFromSpaces` at lock, and at unlock the space it now belongs to is destroyed with it still a member. It is left relying on the bare `panel.orderFrontRegardless()` at LockScreenPresence.swift:113 to rebind, with no `SLSRemoveWindowsFromSpaces` behind it. `startGeometryWatchdog` (NotchController.swift:379-391) only compares frames, so nothing repairs space membership.
    apple: Apple's own compositor clients tear a private space down in the reverse of the order they built it: remove every window, then hide, then destroy. Leaving a window assigned to a destroyed space is undefined and is exactly the class of bug that shows up as "the thing vanished after I unlocked" or "it is now floating over my full-screen app."
    impact: After the first lock/unlock cycle the island can go missing, or sit above windows it should be behind, until the panel is rebuilt by a display change. It is intermittent and machine-dependent, which is the worst kind of bug to get a support ticket for.
    evidence: Both windows are lifted into one shared space: `NotchController.screenLocked()` calls `lockPresence.apply(to: panel, locked: true)` at Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchController.swift:223, then `lockCard.present(...)` at :230, which calls `presence.apply(to: panel, locked: true)` for the card (Sources/IslaKit/Notch/LockCardWindow.swift:98). `SkyLight.lift` (Sources/IslaKit/Notch/LockScreenPresence.swift:184-192) creates `space` only if it is 0, so both window numbers land in the same space. On unlock, `NotchController.screenUnlocked()` runs `lockCard.dismiss(presence:)` first (:269) and `lockPresence.apply(to: panel, locked: false)` second (:270). The card's teardown, `SkyLight.lower` (LockScreenPresence.swift:194-200), does `removeWindows([card],[space]); hideSpaces; spaceDestroy(space); space = 0`. The panel's call then hits `guard space != 0 else { return }` at :195 and returns immediately — `SLSRemoveWindowsFromSpaces` is never called for the notch panel, contradicting the class's own stated contract at :99-102 ("the window is removed from the custom space and re-ordered, which rebinds it to the active space").
    fix: Make `SkyLight` reference-count its members instead of assuming one window. Keep a `private var members: Set<Int>`; `lift` inserts, `lower` removes the given window number and only calls `hideSpaces`/`spaceDestroy`/`space = 0` once `members` is empty. Alternatively, swap the two lines in `NotchController.screenUnlocked()` so the panel leaves before the card destroys the space — but that only papers over it, because `LockCardWindow.dismiss` legitimately runs on its own from `reposition`/screen-change paths. Also delete the now-dead `LockScreenPresence.lockedLevel` (:36-38) and the three tests in `Tests/IslaKitTests/LockScreenPresenceTests.swift:10-28` that exercise it — `apply(to:locked:)` no longer sets `panel.level` on the lock side, so those tests currently certify a lock-screen contract the shipping code abandoned.
    challenges: The call ordering is exactly as described — NotchController.swift:269-270 dismisses the card first, LockCardWindow.swift:105 routes that to SkyLight.lower, and LockScreenPresence.swift:194-200 destroys the shared space and zeroes `space` so the panel's own lower() hits `guard space != 0` at :195 and returns without ever calling SLSRemoveWindowsFromSpaces. But no user-visible symptom is demonstrated: `panel.level` is reset and `orderFrontRegardless()` still runs (:110-113) on a window with `.canJoinAllSpaces`, and the lock/unlock path is the single most-iterated path in this repo (62c7db1, 2352554, f75ccb2, d30dd6a, plus the stale-lock repair paths at NotchController.swift:97 and :148) with the island still shipping in v0.1.0 — an island that vanished after every unlock would not have survived that. | corrected: minor — a real SPI-hygiene defect (the shared space is refcounted by neither owner, and the current order orphans the window that has to survive) whose failure mode is unproven. It becomes a blocker only if the hardware lock/unlock gate at checklist.md:116-117 actually shows the island missing after unlock; until then it is a latent robustness fix, not an observed bug.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The shipped build is unsigned, tells users to strip quarantine, and therefore keeps the Spotify refresh token in plaintext on disk
    today: Every user of v0.1.0 is on the file backing, not the keychain. Their Spotify OAuth refresh token — a long-lived credential with `user-library-read user-library-modify` — sits in a plaintext JSON file readable by any process running as that user, with no code-signature identity gating it. Settings does say so honestly (`storageNote`, Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift:320-326), which is more than most apps do, but the disclosure does not change the exposure.
    apple: Apple's baseline for direct-download Mac software is Developer ID signing plus notarization plus stapling; asking a user to run `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` is asking them to disable Gatekeeper's provenance check on your behalf. Credentials belong in the data-protection keychain scoped to the team identity, which is exactly what `TokenStore` already implements for signed builds.
    impact: Users see the "Apple could not verify" panic dialog on first launch and are coached to defeat it — which trains exactly the habit malware relies on. Meanwhile anyone who gets code execution as that user, or restores an unencrypted backup, walks off with a working Spotify credential. Every paid competitor (NotchNook, Alcove, MediaMate, DynamicLake) ships notarized; this is the single loudest signal that Isla is a hobby build.
    evidence: README.md:21-31 — "The build is **not yet notarized by Apple**" followed by the instruction `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/Isla.app`. `Scripts/bundle.sh:157` signs with `--sign -` (ad-hoc) unless a `SIGN_IDENTITY` is supplied. `Sources/IslaKit/Services/TokenStore.swift:27-31` states the file fallback "never applies to a build anybody ships" — but README.md:81-84 confirms it applies to "today's direct-download releases". `writeFileValues` (:152-167) writes `spotify-credentials.json` into Application Support and chmods it 0600; the value is the raw refresh token, unencrypted.
    fix: Get a Developer ID Application certificate and run the path `Scripts/release.sh` already implements (it verifies the identity at :29, notarizes at :62-63, staples, and signs the DMG at :87). Until that lands, stop shipping the plaintext fallback silently: encrypt `spotify-credentials.json` with a symmetric key held as a generic password in the *login* keychain (one prompt at connect time, none afterwards) using CryptoKit's `AES.GCM`, and change `storageNote` at SettingsPane.swift:323 to say so. Add an auto-update channel at the same time — an unsigned app with no updater means a security fix never reaches the people who already installed it.
    challenges: Every fact checks out: README.md:21-31 instructs `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine`, Scripts/bundle.sh:152-157 ad-hoc signs with `--sign -`, README.md:81-84 confirms today's releases are on the file backing, and TokenStore.swift:152-167 writes the raw refresh token as plaintext JSON at 0600 (its own doc at :27-31 claiming this 'never applies to a build anybody ships' is now stale). The exposure it buys, though, is thin: the token carries only `user-library-read user-library-modify` (SpotifyAccount.swift:125), and any process that can read a 0600 file in your Application Support folder is already running as you and has far richer targets. Settings discloses it (SettingsPane.swift:320-326) and so does README.md:76-84. | corrected: minor for the token exposure. The unsigned/un-notarized distribution is separately a major adoption problem for a paid product — but it is already an open release gate (checklist.md:127), not a new finding, and it is honestly disclosed rather than hidden.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/M) Privacy Mode covers Clipboard and Translate but not the Shelf, which renders full file previews
    today: Open the panel on the Shelf tab while screen-sharing or on camera and every dropped file's name and thumbnail is fully visible. The only defence is the global "Hide From Screen Recording" switch, which covers capture but not a camera, a person behind you, or a phone pointed at the screen — which is precisely the threat model `PrivacyMode`'s own doc describes at PrivacyMode.swift:5-11 ("for screens that somebody else is watching").
    apple: Apple's per-surface privacy controls are exhaustive over the surfaces that can leak — Hide Alerts, Show Previews, hidden Photos album, Safari's private-window blur. A privacy feature that covers two of the three content tabs teaches users a promise it does not keep.
    impact: Someone who turned on "All" in Privacy expecting the panel to be safe on a shared screen still shows a thumbnail grid of their screenshots — arguably the most visually revealing thing in the app — the moment they touch the Shelf tab.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/Model/PrivacyMode.swift:27 — `case clipboard, translate` is the entire `Section` enum. Sources/IslaKit/UI/NotchContentView.swift:465 builds `ShelfPane(shelf: vm.shelf, isTargeted: vm.isDropTargeted)` with no `privacy` argument, and `ShelfPane` (Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift:3-5) has no privacy property at all. The shelf holds live QuickLook renders of every card: `ShelfItem.icon` is "replaced by a real preview once QuickLook renders one — a shelf of identical PNG icons is useless when what it holds is screenshots" (Sources/IslaKit/Services/ShelfStore.swift:11-19), and clipboard screenshots land there automatically via `ScreenshotVault` when saving is on.
    fix: Add `case shelf` to `PrivacyMode.Section` (PrivacyMode.swift:27) — the `knownSectionsKey` upgrade logic at :63-81 was written for exactly this and will keep existing "cover everything" users covered. Pass `privacy: vm.privacy` into `ShelfPane` at NotchContentView.swift:465, and in the card view draw `SpoilerField(seed:)` over the thumbnail and filename when `privacy.hides(.shelf, item.id.uuidString)`, with the same `RevealEye` affordance `ClipRow` uses (Sources/IslaKit/UI/ClipboardPane.swift:67-69). Add the section symbol to `privacySymbol(for:)` (SettingsPane.swift:356) and the strings to both `.lproj` tables. Extend `Tests/IslaKitTests/PrivacyModeTests.swift` to cover the new-section upgrade.
- [DISPUTED] (minor/S) Turning on "Show Lyrics" silently sends your listening to three third parties, one of them an unofficial Chinese endpoint
    today: The switch is off by default and there is a "Clear Lyrics Cache" row (SettingsPane.swift:183-192), which is good. But the toggle itself explains nothing, and the app has no privacy policy, no About-panel disclosure and no first-run sheet. A user turning on lyrics has no way to learn that their now-playing metadata goes to a Chinese music service's undocumented endpoint.
    apple: Apple pairs any switch that sends data off-device with an explanatory footer naming what leaves and where it goes (Siri & Dictation, Analytics & Improvements, Safari's "Search Engine Suggestions"), and it names the third party when there is one. The word for what is missing here is the sentence under the switch.
    impact: A privacy-conscious user — the exact person who chose an open-source island over a paid one — enables lyrics and unknowingly starts reporting their listening history to three parties, one of which they would very likely decline if asked.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift:133-142 is a bare `toggleRow` titled "Show Lyrics" with no `noteRow` under it — even though `noteRow` exists and is already used for the Spotify storage disclosure at :215/:489-497. `Sources/IslaKit/Model/NotchViewModel.swift:245-249` admits internally: "turning it on sends what is currently playing — title, artist, album, and for Spotify the track id — to three third-party services. That is listening history leaving the machine." The three are `raw.githubusercontent.com` (LyricsStore.swift:347), `lyrics.kugou.com` (:359 and :385, described in its own comment at :356-358 as "Unofficial and keyless"), and `lrclib.net` (:410, :457). Only the LRCLIB calls send an identifying User-Agent (:421-426); the Kugou and GitHub calls do not. `LockScreenCard.swift:138-146` fires the same lookups from the locked card, so an unattended locked Mac keeps talking to them.
    fix: Add a `noteRow` directly under the Show Lyrics toggle at SettingsPane.swift:142, worded as one plain sentence: "Looks up words at lrclib.net, the amll community database and lyrics.kugou.com. The track title, artist, album and length leave your Mac; nothing else does." Localize it in both `.lproj` tables — `test-localizations.sh` will enforce the pair. Consider giving the Kugou path the same identifying User-Agent the LRCLIB path sets (:421-426), so the requests are attributable rather than anonymous, and consider a per-source sub-toggle so a user can keep LRCLIB and drop Kugou.
    challenges: The in-app half is exact: SettingsPane.swift:133-143 is a bare toggleRow with no noteRow, while noteRow exists at :489 and is used for the Spotify disclosure at :215; NotchViewModel.swift:245-249 carries the internal admission verbatim; the three hosts are LyricsStore.swift:347 (raw.githubusercontent.com), :359/:385 (lyrics.kugou.com, 'Unofficial and keyless' at :355-357) and :410/:457 (lrclib.net), with a User-Agent set only on the LRCLIB requests (:422, :463); LockScreenCard.swift:144-149 re-fires the lookup from the locked card behind `guard NotchViewModel.showLyricsEnabled`. But 'a user has no way to learn' is refuted by README.md:58-68, a 'Privacy — what leaves the machine, and when' section that names all three hosts, says it is listening history leaving the Mac, and notes the on-disk cache. | corrected: The disclosure is missing where the choice is made — the 'Show Lyrics' toggle (SettingsPane.swift:133-143) has no note under it, there is no About-panel or first-run disclosure, and the two keyless endpoints send no identifying User-Agent. It is not undisclosed: README.md:58-68 names lrclib.net, raw.githubusercontent.com and lyrics.kugou.com and describes exactly what is sent. ;; The in-app half is exactly right: SettingsPane.swift:132-142 is a bare toggleRow titled 'Show Lyrics' with no noteRow under it even though noteRow exists (:489-497) and is used for the Spotify storage note (:215); the three hosts are raw.githubusercontent.com (LyricsStore.swift:347), lyrics.kugou.com (:359, :385, 'Unofficial and keyless' at :356-358) and lrclib.net (:410, :457); NotchViewModel.swift:245-249 admits the leak internally; LockScreenCard.swift:138-146 fires the same lookups from the locked card; en.lproj/Localizable.strings has no disclosure string. But two details are wrong. (1) 'no way to learn' and 'silently': README.md:58-68 is a section headed 'Privacy — what leaves the machine, and when' that names all three hosts including lyrics.kugou.com by name, and the same disclosure is in checklist.md:47-48 — the app's only distribution page states it. (2) 'the Kugou and GitHub calls do not [send an identifying User-Agent]': the session is a plain URLSessionConfiguration.ephemeral with no httpAdditionalHeaders (LyricsStore.swift:83-88), so CFNetwork sends its default User-Agent carrying the app name, version and Darwin build on every one of those requests — those calls identify the app too, just not deliberately. | corrected: Turning on 'Show Lyrics' sends now-playing metadata to three third parties including lyrics.kugou.com, an unofficial keyless Chinese endpoint, and the app's UI never says so — the toggle carries no note, there is no first-run sheet and no About-panel disclosure, so the disclosure exists only in README.md:58-68 and checklist.md, which a user who installed the DMG may never read. All three requests carry CFNetwork's default app-identifying User-Agent; only LRCLIB gets an explicit one. ;; The in-app gap is real — SettingsPane.swift:133-142 is a bare toggleRow with no noteRow, even though noteRow is used for the Spotify disclosure at :215 — and the three endpoints are as described (LyricsStore.swift:347, :359/:385, :410/:457), with LockScreenCard.swift:138-146 firing the same lookups while locked. But 'a user has no way to learn' is false: README.md:58-69, under the heading 'Privacy — what leaves the machine, and when', names lrclib.net, raw.githubusercontent.com and lyrics.kugou.com explicitly and says it is listening history leaving the Mac; checklist.md:46-48 repeats it. The switch is off by default (NotchViewModel.swift:245-249) and the cache is clearable (SettingsPane.swift:183-192). | corrected: polish — the disclosure exists and is unusually candid, it is just not repeated at the point of decision; the fix is one noteRow under the Show Lyrics toggle.

## code-health
verdict: The native seam is unusually well-reasoned for a solo project: the comments name the bug that motivated each line, `NotchGeometry` already survives an empty `NSScreen.screens`, `PointerWatcher` samples instead of trusting event routing, `NowPlayingFeed` hops off the pipe's dispatch thread with `Task { @MainActor in }`, and 214 tests pass.
