That is above the bar of most paid competitors. But it is not yet Apple-native in the one way that matters most: the seam has one reachable hard crash (dropping a Mail/Photos file promise on the Shelf traps the process — verified by running the exact `MainActor.assumeIsolated`-on-a-background-`OperationQueue` pattern, SIGTRAP/exit 133), and one everyday correctness bug that affects every user on an external display or a pre-2021 Mac (the compact island is drawn at menu-bar height but only its top 8 pt take a click or a hover). Structurally, `NotchController` is a 1115-line god object with 14 `setOpen` call sites, four `DispatchWorkItem` fields, a generation counter, a 2 Hz watchdog, a 60/8 Hz sampler — and zero tests; the elaborate invariants in its comments are held by nothing but the comments. Apple ships this layer as `NSWindowController` + a small explicit presentation state machine precisely because open/close/lock/rebuild is where "not quite native" bugs breed. Fix the crash, the 8 pt hit region, the two-window SkyLight space, and the settings-slider rebuild, and this clears the native bar; give the open state a real machine with tests and it stays cleared.
- [CONFIRMED] (blocker/S) Dropping a Mail attachment, Photos item, or Safari image on the island kills the app
    today: `performDragOperation` accepts the drag, calls the promises in, and the first delivered file traps the main-actor assumption on a non-main thread. The app vanishes; the drag looks like it worked for a frame. Every other off-thread hop in the codebase gets this right (NowPlayingFeed.swift:216 and :220 both use `Task { @MainActor in … }`), which is what makes this an isolated oversight rather than a pattern.
    apple: Finder, Mail and Notes all accept file promises and none of them terminate; Apple's own sample (`NSFilePromiseReceiver`) delivers on a background queue and hops to main with an explicit dispatch. `assumeIsolated` is documented as a precondition — it is for asserting an isolation you already know holds, never for acquiring one.
    impact: The Shelf is the tab that has to beat NotchDrop and NotchNook, and its most-advertised input — drag an attachment out of Mail, an image out of Safari, a photo out of Photos — quits the app instantly. A first-run user tries exactly this.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchRootView.swift:226-238 — `let queue = OperationQueue()` … `receiver.receivePromisedFiles(atDestination: destination, options: [:], operationQueue: queue) { url, error in MainActor.assumeIsolated { … self.enqueuePromisedFile(url) } }`. The SDK header is explicit (MacOSX.sdk/…/NSFilePromiseReceiver.h:37): "The reader block is called on the supplied operationQueue." The comment at NotchRootView.swift:225-226 says the background queue is deliberate. I compiled and ran the identical pattern (`-swift-version 5`, fresh `OperationQueue`): the process dies with SIGTRAP, exit 133, no message. This path is reached only via promises — NotchRootView.swift:74-76 registers `NSFilePromiseReceiver.readableDraggedTypes` precisely so "Mail attachments, Photos items, and images dragged out of a browser" work, and NotchRootView.swift:203 takes the concrete-URL branch first, so promise-only drags always land here.
    fix: Replace `MainActor.assumeIsolated { … }` with `Task { @MainActor [weak self] in … }` (or `DispatchQueue.main.async { [weak self] in }`) inside the reader block, and capture `self` weakly — today it is captured strongly at NotchRootView.swift:237. Add a `ShelfDropTests` case that invokes the reader block from a background `OperationQueue` so the trap can never come back; nothing in Tests/IslaKitTests exercises `performDragOperation` at all today.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) On any display without a notch, three quarters of the drawn island is dead to clicks and hovers
    today: The app paints an opaque pill across the middle of the menu bar at full bar height, then refuses events on everything below its top 8 pt: `hitTest` returns nil (NotchRootView.swift:89) and `ignoresMouseEvents` stays true (NotchController.swift:759). The comment at NotchGeometry.swift:273-282 justifies the shallow strip by wanting status items underneath to stay clickable — but the justification is mis-scoped: under the drawn pill there is nothing of the menu bar left to see, so the trade buys nothing there and costs the whole affordance.
    apple: Apple's rule is that the hit region is at least the drawn control, never less — HIG's 28×28 pt minimum target, and Control Center/menu-bar extras answer anywhere inside the shape they draw. Nothing in macOS draws a filled surface that ignores most of itself.
    impact: Every user on an external monitor and every pre-2021 Mac — the exact users Boring Notch and Peninsula court — sees an island that mostly does not respond. Clicking its middle does nothing; hovering it does not even light it. It reads as broken, not as restraint. This is also the app's own stated invariant (commit d160909, "every point of the compact island opens it") failing on the height axis.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchGeometry.swift:283 `var collapsedDepth: CGFloat { isPhysical ? notchSize.height : 8 }`; the synthetic notch's drawn height is `max(menuBarHeight, NSStatusBar.system.thickness, 24)` (NotchGeometry.swift:187) — 24–38 pt. The island is *drawn* at that height: NotchContentView.swift:227 `.frame(width: size.width, height: vm.geometry.notchSize.height)`. But every region that answers is cut from `collapsedDepth`: NotchController.swift:1080 `size = CGSize(width: vm.bodySize.width, height: vm.geometry.collapsedDepth)` → `rootView.activeRect` (:1103) and `pointer.interactiveRect` (:1111); `collapsedHoverRect` (NotchGeometry.swift:308-315) and `collapsedIslandRect` (:299-306) are `collapsedDepth` tall too, so hover-to-open and the hover lift are confined to the same 8 pt.
    fix: Separate the two concepts in `NotchGeometry`: keep the shallow 8 pt approach strip for the *empty* band beside the pill, and give the pill's own footprint its full `notchSize.height` in `collapsedIslandRect`, `collapsedHoverRect` and the `applyActiveRect(open: false)` size, on synthetic notches as well. Then extend Tests/IslaKitTests/CompactHitAreaTests.swift — today it passes `geometry.collapsedDepth` in as the height (line 19) and only ever asserts on `.width`, so it is structurally blind to this; assert `clickable.height == geometry.notchSize.height` with `isPhysical` forced false.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) Unlocking destroys the lock-screen space while the notch panel is still inside it
    today: `SLSRemoveWindowsFromSpaces` is never called for the notch panel, even though `SLSSpaceAddWindowsAndRemoveFromSpaces(…, 7)` already removed it from every ordinary space. The space is destroyed with the panel still assigned to it. The code's own comment (LockScreenPresence.swift:99-102) states that the removal is what "rebinds it to the active space" — so the panel is relying on the trailing `orderFrontRegardless()` (:113) to recover from an orphaned state rather than on the documented undo. `rebuild()` mid-lock has the mirror problem: the old panel is dropped at NotchController.swift:592-594 without ever being lowered out of the space.
    apple: Apple has no public equivalent, but the invariant is ordinary resource discipline: a shared handle lifted by N owners is released when the last one lets go. Every private-SPI window trick in shipping notch apps ref-counts the space for exactly this reason.
    impact: After an unlock the island can be missing or mis-ordered on the desktop until some later event re-fronts it — precisely the class of symptom the file already carries three separate repair paths for (NotchController.swift:93-100, :146-150, :368-431). Users report it as "the island disappears until I lock and unlock again".
    evidence: `SkyLight` keeps one space in a single field: Sources/IslaKit/Notch/LockScreenPresence.swift:151 `private var space: UInt64 = 0`. `lift` creates it once and adds a window (:184-192); `lower` removes *that* window, hides, destroys, and zeroes it (:194-200). Two different windows are lifted into it: the notch panel at NotchController.swift:223 `lockPresence.apply(to: panel, locked: true)`, and the card's own panel at LockCardWindow.swift:98 `presence.apply(to: panel, locked: true)`. On unlock, NotchController.screenUnlocked() runs `lockCard.dismiss(presence:)` first (:269) — which lowers the card and destroys the space — and only then `lockPresence.apply(to: panel, locked: false)` (:270), where `guard space != 0 else { return }` returns immediately.
    fix: Make `SkyLight` ref-count: keep `private var members: Set<Int>`; `lift` inserts the window number, `lower` removes it and only calls `hideSpaces`/`spaceDestroy` when the set empties. Independently, reverse the order in `screenUnlocked()` so the notch panel is lowered before `lockCard.dismiss`, and lower the outgoing panel in `rebuild()` before discarding it. `LockScreenPresenceTests` can cover the ref-count with a fake, since `lockedLevel(base:shield:)` shows the file already isolates arithmetic for testing.
    challenges: The mechanism is exactly as described — LockScreenPresence.swift:151 holds one `space` field; `lower` (:194-200) removes only the passed window, then hides, destroys and zeroes; NotchController.screenUnlocked() calls `lockCard.dismiss(presence:)` (:269) before `lockPresence.apply(to: panel, locked: false)` (:270), so the notch panel's `lower` hits `guard space != 0 else { return }` and `SLSRemoveWindowsFromSpaces` is never called for it. But no user-visible failure is demonstrated. The lock card is presented on every lock by default (LockCardWindow.present is unguarded, NotchController.swift:228-230; LockCardStyle has only .glass/.solid), so this exact ordering runs on every single unlock of a shipped v0.1.0 that passed manual lock/unlock validation — if it orphaned the panel, the island would be missing after every unlock. The trailing `orderFrontRegardless()` (:113) evidently rebinds it. Same for the mid-lock `rebuild()` leak: the stale window number sits in a space that is destroyed on the next unlock. | corrected: minor — a latent SPI-ordering fragility, not a live defect: `lower(windowNumber:)` should call `SLSRemoveWindowsFromSpaces` for the window before destroying the shared space (and `rebuild()` should lower the old panel), so recovery rests on the documented undo instead of on `orderFrontRegardless()` happening to rebind an orphaned window. No reproducible symptom today.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) The Panel Width slider destroys and recreates the panel synchronously, once per point of travel
    today: The `NSHostingView` being destroyed is the one currently executing the slider's own value setter, and its `@State private var bodyWidth` (SettingsPane.swift:16) dies with it. `build()` re-opens the new panel only if the pointer happens to satisfy `pointerOnTarget || (wasOpen && pointerOnBody)` (NotchController.swift:849-856), and the in-flight drag gesture belongs to a view that no longer exists.
    apple: System Settings sliders that change window geometry (Displays' scaling, Accessibility's cursor size) apply live and continuously; nothing in macOS tears down and rebuilds the window hosting the control you are dragging. Where a rebuild really is needed, AppKit apps commit on `NSControl` action with `isContinuous = false`.
    impact: Setting the panel width — the one dimension the product deliberately exposes — feels broken: the slider stutters or drops the drag, and the panel flickers as it is rebuilt beneath the cursor. It is the first thing a paying user touches in Settings.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/UI/SettingsPane.swift:328-343 — the binding's `set` writes the default and then calls `(NSApp.delegate as? AppDelegate)?.refreshGeometry()` directly, with no debounce and no `editing`-ended gate. That reaches AppDelegate.swift:105-107 → NotchController.swift:484-486 `func refreshGeometry() { rebuild() }` → NotchController.swift:575-598, which does `panel?.orderOut(nil)`, `panel?.contentView = nil`, `panel = nil`, `viewModel = nil`, `cancellables.removeAll()`, then a fresh `NotchPanel` + fresh `NSHostingView(rootView: NotchContentView(vm:))` (:612-616). The slider's range is 480…620 (SettingsPane.swift:92), and the setter guards only on whole-point changes (:333), so a full drag is up to 140 complete window-and-view-hierarchy teardowns.
    fix: Either (a) coalesce: keep the `@State` and the default write in the setter, and route the rebuild through the existing `DebouncedWrite` (Sources/IslaKit/Services/DebouncedWrite.swift) at ~120 ms, or (b) better, stop rebuilding at all for width — the window is already cut to `NotchMetrics.maximumBodyWidth` and never resized (NotchGeometry.swift:206-219), so width only needs `applyActiveRect` + the four `pointer.*Rect` assignments re-run. Make `bodyWidth` a `@Published` on `NotchViewModel` and add `NotchController.refreshWidth()` that re-cuts rects in place; the geometry rebuild is only genuinely required when the *display* changes.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/XL) NotchController owns lifecycle, geometry, timing and the lock transition, and `isOpen` deliberately lags intent
    today: Correctness is held by ordering conventions restated in prose (`collapseNow()`'s header at :935-940 exists purely because a deferred collapse used to land after the lock reconfigured the panel). Three separate "repair" branches (:93-100, :146-150, :368-431) exist to recover from states the design cannot rule out. There is no type that names the states — collapsed / opening / open / closing / locked / rebuilding — so contradictory combinations are representable and only avoided by call-order discipline.
    apple: AppKit's own presentation controllers keep window lifecycle (`NSWindowController`), layout, and transient presentation state in separate objects, and model transitions as an explicit enum with rejected transitions rather than as a set of independent booleans plus generation counters.
    impact: Not a specific reproducible bug today — I could not name a reachable same-pass close-then-open — but this is why the lock/unlock/sleep/space-change matrix has needed a repair path per symptom, and why the next feature that opens the panel is likely to reintroduce one. It is the main reason a reviewer cannot tell whether the app is correct under every system state.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchController.swift is 1115 lines with 14 `setOpen(...)` call sites (lines 75, 158, 176, 183, 465, 522, 542, 562, 635, 648, 660, 754, 855, 871), four deferred-work fields (`closeActiveRectWork`, `collapseCheckWork`, `peekWork` at :17-19, plus `openGeneration` at :39), a 2 Hz `geometryWatchdog` (:379-431) and a 60/8 Hz `PointerWatcher` with two more clocks of its own (`awaitingSince`, `graceUntil`). The load-bearing hazard: `setOpen` gates on `guard let vm = viewModel, vm.isOpen != open else { return }` (:896) while its own close path *never sets* `vm.isOpen = false` — it defers that to the next run-loop pass (:922-926), a fact its caller `toggle()` has to work around explicitly (:536-541). So during that window `vm.isOpen` is `true` while the intent is closed: a `setOpen(true)` arriving in the same pass hits the guard, returns without bumping `openGeneration`, and the queued `collapse()` then closes a panel the caller believes it opened — skipping `applyActiveRect(open: true)` and `media.setActive(true)` too.
    fix: Introduce `enum PanelPresentation { case collapsed, opening, open, closing, locked }` on `NotchViewModel`, set eagerly at the top of `setOpen` (so intent and rendered state are two fields, not one), and make `collapse()`/`applyActiveRect` read the intent rather than the rendered flag. Then split the file at its natural seams: `NotchPresentation` (panel + hosting view + `applyActiveRect`/`applyLockedActiveRect`/rect arithmetic), `LockTransition` (`screenLocked`/`screenUnlocked`/`LockScreenPresence`/`LockCardWindow` + the wake and space-change repairs), leaving `NotchController` as the ~250-line coordinator. `teardown()` should then also be provably the inverse of `install()` — today it misses `geometryWatchdog?.invalidate()` and `lockCard.dismiss(...)` (NotchController.swift:433-449).
    challenges: The structural facts hold: NotchController.swift is 1115 lines with 14 `setOpen` call sites, three deferred-work fields plus `openGeneration` (:17-19, :39), a 2 Hz geometry watchdog (:379-431), three repair branches (:93-100, :146-150, :368-431), and `setOpen`'s close path really does defer `vm.isOpen = false` to the next pass (:919-926) while guarding on `vm.isOpen != open` (:896) — with `toggle()` (:536-541) explicitly documenting the workaround. But I could not find a reachable same-pass `setOpen(false)` → `setOpen(true)` sequence: every other caller (pointer sampler ticks, hotkey/Escape events, `onDrop`, `sneakPeek`, `setKeyboard`, `build`) lands in a later run-loop pass, and the one transition that reconfigures in the same breath already uses `collapseNow()` (:935-940). So the named 'load-bearing hazard' is theoretical, and the rest of the finding is maintainability, which no user names. | corrected: minor — a maintainability/regression-risk finding, not a user-facing one: the highest-traffic file in the app encodes collapsed/opening/open/closing/locked/rebuilding as loose flags plus call-order conventions, which is why three repair branches exist, but the specific `setOpen` guard race has no demonstrated trigger.
- [DISPUTED] (minor/S) A glitched notch reading during a display change sticks for the rest of the session
    today: If the glitchy zero-inset window that the code itself documents (NotchGeometry.swift:99-112) coincides with a `didChangeScreenParametersNotification` rebuild — plugging in a monitor, a resolution change, closing the lid — the notched display is remembered as *not* notched (`rememberedNotches[id] = nil`, :171) and stays synthetic until the next screen-parameters change happens to arrive.
    apple: System UI that depends on `safeAreaInsets` re-reads it on every layout pass rather than caching a one-shot decision, and treats a nil `auxiliaryTopLeftArea` on a display that reports a top inset as a transient, not as an answer.
    impact: The island silently degrades on real notch hardware: it re-anchors to screen-centre instead of the cutout, and — via finding `synthetic-notch-8pt-hit-region` — its hit region drops from the full notch height to 8 pt. The user sees "it went small and stopped responding after I plugged in my monitor" and has to quit and relaunch.
    evidence: `NotchGeometry.current()` (Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchGeometry.swift:130-191) treats a display as notched only when `safeAreaInsets.top > 0` **and both** `auxiliaryTopLeftArea` and `auxiliaryTopRightArea` are non-nil (:145-147); otherwise it falls through to the synthetic 180 pt pill at `screen.frame.midX` (:185-190). The rescue is `rememberedNotches` (:113-121), which is empty on a display's first reading. The only re-check is `verifySyntheticNotch()` (:328-340), which fires at +1.5 s and +5.0 s — and it is called exactly once, from `install()` (NotchController.swift:124). `rebuild()` (:575-598) does not call it, and the geometry watchdog (:379-431) compares only frames and view sizes, never `isPhysical`.
    fix: Call `verifySyntheticNotch()` at the end of `build()` rather than from `install()`, so every rebuild re-checks; and add `isPhysical` to what the geometry watchdog compares (NotchController.swift:386-389 already samples every 2 s and has the fresh geometry available via `NotchGeometry.current()`), rebuilding when a display that is drawing synthetic starts reporting a real cutout. Both are cheap because a matching reading is skipped.
    challenges: The architectural gap is real: `verifySyntheticNotch()` (NotchGeometry-driven, NotchController.swift:328-340) is called from exactly one place, install() at :124 (grep confirms two hits total, definition and that call); rebuild() at :575-598 never re-checks, and the watchdog at :379-431 compares only window frame, contentView bounds and hosted frame — never `isPhysical`. But the failure scenario is narrower than stated. `decide()` at NotchGeometry.swift:113-121 rescues a glitched zero reading whenever the display has a remembered entry with an unchanged frame — which is the ordinary case for a rebuild caused by an external monitor arriving, since the built-in display's frame does not move. The claim's stated reason for the rescue failing ("empty on a display's first reading") does not apply at rebuild time; the fall-through needs a real screen-frame change or a display never yet seen as notched. Line 171 also erases the entry (`rememberedNotches[id] = nil`) rather than recording a negative. And the title's "sticks for the rest of the session" contradicts the body: `screenParametersChanged()` (:295-307) calls `current()` and rebuilds whenever `matches()` disagrees, and `matches` compares `isPhysical` (NotchGeometry.swift:201), so the next clean parameters notification recovers it. | corrected: There is no re-verification of a synthetic reading after a rebuild — `verifySyntheticNotch()` runs only from `install()` (NotchController.swift:124), and neither `rebuild()` nor the geometry watchdog re-reads `isPhysical`. So a display whose frame changed at the same moment its safe-area insets glitched to zero (a resolution/mode change, or a display not yet in `rememberedNotches`) can be drawn with the synthetic 180 pt pill until the next `didChangeScreenParametersNotification` arrives with a clean reading. It is recoverable, not permanent, and the `rememberedNotches` rescue already covers the common same-frame glitch. ;; The code gap is real: `verifySyntheticNotch()` (NotchController.swift:328-340, +1.5 s/+5.0 s) is called only from `install()` (:124); `rebuild()` (:575-598) never re-arms it, and the watchdog (:379-431) compares only frames and view sizes. But the trigger list is wrong for most of its cases. `NotchGeometry.decide` (:113-121) returns `.physical` from `rememberedNotches` whenever the screen frame is unchanged, and `rememberedNotches[id] = nil` (:171) only runs when there is no memory *or* the frame changed — so plugging in a monitor (built-in stays at origin (0,0), frame unchanged) and closing the lid (the built-in leaves `NSScreen.screens` entirely, and only the external's id is cleared) are both already rescued. Recovery is also not 'until the next screen-parameters change happens to arrive' by accident: `matches()` includes `isPhysical` (:201), so the next `didChangeScreenParametersNotification` with a good reading rebuilds correctly. | corrected: `rebuild()` does not re-run `verifySyntheticNotch()`, so a glitched zero-inset reading can leave a notched display drawn synthetic until the next screen-parameters notification. It only bites when the notched display's frame *also* changed (a resolution/scaling change on that display, or an arrangement change that moves the menu bar off it) or its remembered entry was already cleared; the `rememberedNotches` guard already covers the frame-unchanged cases, including plugging in a monitor and closing the lid. ;; Severity is right but the trigger set is inflated. `verifySyntheticNotch()` is indeed called from exactly one place (NotchController.swift:124, `install()`); `rebuild()` (:575-598) never re-checks, and the watchdog (:379-431) compares frames and view sizes, never `isPhysical` — so a bad reading does stick. However `NotchGeometry.decide` (NotchGeometry.swift:113-121) rescues a zero reading whenever `remembered.frame == screenFrame`, and `rememberedNotches` is populated on every successful read (:163-165). The built-in display is read correctly at launch, so plugging in an external monitor or closing the lid leaves its frame unchanged and the rescue fires; only a glitch coinciding with a genuine frame change of the notched display itself falls through to the synthetic 180 pt pill at :185-190. | corrected: still minor, narrower scope: a glitched zero inset sticks for the session only when it coincides with a real frame change on the notched display (a resolution/mode change on that display), not on any screen-parameters event. When it does happen the display degrades to the synthetic pill — which then also inherits the 8 pt hit region from the synthetic-notch finding.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/L) The notch layer's two biggest behaviours — the controller and the pointer state machine — have essentially no coverage
    today: 214 tests pass, but they concentrate on pure functions (`NotchGeometry.decide`, `LockCardWindow.frame`, `LyricSweep`, `NDJSONBuffer`) and on view-model flags. Every invariant the controller's comments describe — the pin dropping on close, the peek not stealing an open panel, the lock path collapsing synchronously before reconfiguring, the rebuild restoring the tab before cutting rects — is asserted only in prose.
    apple: Not an Apple API question; it is the discipline the project's own CLAUDE.md sets ("a red build is not a change to commit", contract tests that assert removals stay removed). The `TabContractTests` pattern shows the team already knows how to freeze an invariant.
    impact: Indirect but real: this is the file where a regression is invisible from the outside — a panel that opens with the wrong rect, or a lock that leaves the card dead — and it is the file most likely to be edited next. Every fix in this report lands in untested code.
    evidence: `grep -rn NotchController Tests/` returns nothing: no test constructs a `NotchController`, so `setOpen`, `collapse`, `collapseNow`, `scheduleCollapseIfPointerAway`, `sneakPeek`, `screenLocked`, `screenUnlocked`, `rebuild` and `applyActiveRect` are all untested. Tests/IslaKitTests/PointerWatcherTests.swift contains exactly one test, for `start()` sampling synchronously; nothing covers `tick()`'s dwell, hysteresis (`warmZone`/`coolZone`), `graceUntil`, or the `closeRect`-vs-`openRect` swap at PointerWatcher.swift:202. Where the notch layer *is* tested, some assertions are tautologies: Tests/IslaKitTests/AppSurfaceContractTests.swift:41 is `XCTAssertEqual(GlobalHotKey.defaultKeyCode, GlobalHotKey.defaultKeyCode)`, which can never fail.
    fix: `NotchController` is already `@MainActor` and takes no constructor arguments; the blocker to testing it is that `build()` needs a real screen. Extract the open/close decision into a testable `OpenStateMachine` (see `notchcontroller-has-no-state-machine`) and cover: close-then-open in one pass, lock while open, peek arriving during a drag, rebuild restoring `wasOpen`. For `PointerWatcher`, inject the clock and the pointer read (`() -> CGPoint`, `() -> Date`) so `tick()`'s dwell and grace can be driven deterministically. Delete or fix the tautology at AppSurfaceContractTests.swift:41.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/M) The panel window is still sized for the removed teleprompter, and the tab-height machinery it justified is now constant
    today: The panel is a 700×444 window at `statusWindow + 1` on every space, 236 pt taller than anything it can draw. Because `openBodySize` no longer varies, the whole "cut for the tab that will be showing" apparatus is constant-folded: the `vm.$tab` subscription and `refreshOpenRects()` (NotchController.swift:773-784, :1006-1010), the `pointer.closeRect` re-cut at :707, and `tallExpandedSize`/`expandedHoverRect` (NotchGeometry.swift:40-42, :348) are all dead or no-ops. Comments across NotchGeometry, NotchViewModel and NotchController still explain themselves in terms of the teleprompter and the notes pane (e.g. NotchViewModel.swift:191-194, NotchController.swift:604-607, :653-657, :884-894).
    apple: Not a HIG matter — this is the project's own "removed features stay removed" rule (CLAUDE.md), which `TabContractTests` enforces for tabs but nothing enforces for the geometry the removed tabs left behind.
    impact: No direct symptom I can demonstrate — the extra window area is transparent and `hitTest` returns nil outside `activeRect`. The cost is to the next person to change this layer, who must reason about a tab that does not exist and about height-varying rects that can no longer vary. That is exactly the tax that produced the two rect bugs above.
    evidence: Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchMetrics.swift:24 still defines `static let teleprompterBody = CGSize(width: 620, height: 400)`; NotchGeometry.swift:39 `static let tallBodyHeight = NotchMetrics.teleprompterBody.height` feeds `maxBodyHeight` (:46), which pins `NotchMetrics.maximumWindow = CGSize(width: 700, height: 444)` (NotchMetrics.swift:25) — asserted in NotchGeometry.swift:217. But the tallest body any surviving tab asks for is `standardBodyHeight = 208`: `NotchViewModel.openBodySize` is now unconditionally `geometry.expandedSize` (Model/NotchViewModel.swift:195). The Notes/Teleprompter tabs were withdrawn on 2026-08-22 per CLAUDE.md.
    fix: Delete `NotchMetrics.teleprompterBody`, collapse `tallBodyHeight`/`maxBodyHeight`/`tallExpandedSize` to `standardBodyHeight`, recompute `maximumWindow` to 700×252, and drop `NotchViewModel.openBodySize` in favour of `geometry.expandedSize` at the four call sites. Keep the `vm.$tab` re-cut only if a future tab will vary in height; otherwise remove it and `refreshOpenRects()`. Add the removal as a dated Withdrawn amendment to docs/plans/2026-08-18-dynamic-island-parity-design.md, per the project's amendment rule.

## product-shape
verdict: Isla is an excellent *music* app wearing a notch-utility costume, and the costume is the problem. The Music surface is genuinely world-class — three-tier word-synced karaoke (TTML/KRC/LRCLIB), a real position clock with seek-intent gating, shuffle/repeat, an empty state with launch affordances, a lock-screen card with output switching. Nothing in the paid field ships word-level karaoke, and the project's own research says so (`docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:16`). But the *product shape* around it is upside-down in three ways. (1) The island does not exist unless music is playing: `CompactMediaActivity` has exactly three cases — hidden/paused/playing (`Sources/IslaKit/Model/CompactMediaActivity.swift:8-19`) — and with nothing playing the header renders `Color.clear` at notch size (`Sources/IslaKit/UI/NotchContentView.swift:152-155`). There is no IOKit, EventKit, CoreBluetooth, AppIntents or UserNotifications anywhere in `Sources/IslaKit`, so charging, AirPods, Focus, timers, screen recording, AirDrop and Shortcuts are all absent. That is the entire "Live Activity" grammar the category is named after, and it is at zero. (2) Apple's Dynamic Island is one contextual surface; Isla's open state is a sticky five-icon tab rail (`NotchContentView.swift:430`, `NotchViewModel.swift:39-41`) that shows the same thing regardless of what the Mac is doing — that is a menu-bar app's information architecture pinned to the notch. (3) Two of the four content tabs are things the project's own strategy document explicitly told it not to build ("File shelf / tray / AirDrop / clipboard … Not music; skip" — battle plan line 97), while the move it ranked as a category-winner and said *no notch app has* — output routing and volume in the expanded island, Move 5, line 72-75 — is unbuilt: `SystemVolume`/`AudioOutputs` are wired only into `LockScreenCard.swift:419,527`, never into `MediaPane`. Against the worth-paying bar: today the honest pitch is "free, MIT, and the best lyrics on the Mac" — but lyrics are default-off (`NotchViewModel.swift:250-252`) and are not mentioned once in the README's "What Isla does" list (README.md:36-56). Nobody who already paid $25 for NotchNook has been given a reason to look. The cut I would make: delete Translate, demote Clipboard, and spend the reclaimed rail on ambient activities plus a volume/output row in Music.
- [DISPUTED] (blocker/L) The island does not exist unless music is playing — zero ambient/event states
    today: A user who is not streaming music sees nothing at the notch, all day, every day. Charging the MacBook, connecting AirPods, switching Focus, starting a timer, running a Shortcut, having the camera or mic go live, receiving an AirDrop, filling the disk — none of these produce any island state. The app is a black rectangle the exact size of the camera housing that only becomes a product when Spotify is open.
    apple: The Dynamic Island's entire premise is that it is a *live* surface: on iPhone it shows charging, Face ID, timers, Focus changes, screen recording, AirDrop, Shortcuts, silent-mode, and Live Activities from any app, and collapses to a nothing only when nothing is happening. On the Mac, Apple ships the equivalent moments as system HUDs and menu-bar indicators (the orange/green camera-mic dot, the charging chime + battery menu, Focus in Control Center). Every paid competitor implements at least charging + AirPods (Alcove, NotchNook, DynamicLake Pro, Boring Notch, Peninsula).
    impact: For the large majority of a workday the app appears not to be running at all — the single most common review complaint in this category, and the reason someone who bought NotchNook will not switch. It also collapses the value story to 'a music widget', which is not what people install a notch app for.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Model/CompactMediaActivity.swift:8-19` — the entire collapsed-state vocabulary is `enum CompactMediaActivity { case hidden, paused, playing }`, built only from media: `NotchViewModel.swift:214-216` `CompactMediaActivity(hasTrack: media.track != nil, isPlaying: media.isPlaying)`. With no track, `NotchContentView.swift:152-155` renders `Color.clear.frame(width: vm.geometry.notchSize.width, ...)` and `NotchContentView.swift:78` drops the shadow to `opacity(... : 0)` — the island is literally invisible on notch hardware. The whole of `Sources/IslaKit` imports only AppKit, Carbon, Combine, Compression, CoreAudio, CoreGraphics, CryptoKit, Foundation, FoundationModels, ObjectiveC, QuartzCore, QuickLookThumbnailing, Security, ServiceManagement, SwiftUI, UniformTypeIdentifiers — **no IOKit** (battery/charging), **no CoreBluetooth/IOBluetooth** (AirPods), **no EventKit** (calendar; removed 2026-08-20 per `checklist.md:22`), **no AppIntents** (Shortcuts), **no UserNotifications**, **no ScreenCaptureKit**. `AudioOutputs.available()` is called only from `LockScreenCard.swift:457` — plugging in AirPods while unlocked shows nothing. The one ambient behavior that exists is `sneakPeek()` (`NotchController.swift:1018-1044`), and it fires only on a track change.
    fix: Generalize the collapsed state from `CompactMediaActivity` into an `IslandActivity` protocol with a priority-ordered queue in `NotchViewModel` (leading accessory, trailing accessory, dwell duration, priority), and re-express media as one conformer so nothing about the existing pill regresses. Then ship the three zero-permission wins first: (a) **Charging/battery** via `IOPSNotificationCreateRunLoopSource` + `IOPSCopyPowerSourcesInfo` — plug in → pill widens with a bolt and percentage, unplug and low-battery likewise; (b) **Audio output changed** via a `kAudioHardwarePropertyDefaultOutputDevice` listener on the `AudioOutputs` code that already exists — 'AirPods Pro' with the symbol `AudioOutputs.swift:112-114` already computes; (c) **Focus / Do Not Disturb** via the `com.apple.donotdisturb.state` / `NSWorkspace` notification pair. Each is a peek using the exact `sneakPeek()` machinery in `NotchController.swift:1018` — no new window, no new permission, no new entitlement. Record each as a capability in `checklist.md` per the project's own rule.
    challenges: Core is exact: Sources/IslaKit/Model/CompactMediaActivity.swift:8-19 is the whole collapsed vocabulary (hidden/paused/playing), built only from media at Sources/IslaKit/Model/NotchViewModel.swift:214-216; with no track NotchContentView.swift:153-154 renders Color.clear at notch size and :78 zeroes the shadow. The import census holds — no IOKit, no CoreBluetooth/IOBluetooth, no EventKit, no AppIntents, no UserNotifications, no ScreenCaptureKit, no Intents (grep for IOPSCopy|batter|charg|bluetooth|focusStatus|EKEvent|AppIntent|UNUserNotification|thermalState over Sources/IslaKit returns only 4 unrelated comment hits). AudioOutputs.available() is called only from LockScreenCard.swift:457, and the only ambient behavior is sneakPeek() (NotchController.swift:1018), fired solely from the $track key change at :810-821. What is wrong is the Apple half of the trigger list and the 'Live Activities' framing. | corrected: Keep the finding, fix the vocabulary. There is no ActivityKit on macOS — third-party Macs apps cannot originate Live Activities (macOS 26 only mirrors an iPhone's via Continuity), so every ambient state here must be self-built, not adopted from an Apple API. Of the eight triggers listed: charging (IOKit IOPowerSources), AirPods (a kAudioHardwarePropertyDevices listener the app already has the CoreAudio code for), mic-live (kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceIsRunningSomewhere), disk pressure (URLResourceValues) and Shortcuts (the app can vend App Intents, macOS 13+) are all genuinely available with no entitlement. But 'receiving an AirDrop' has no public observation point on macOS (sharingd is private; only a ~/Downloads FSEvents heuristic exists); 'switching Focus' yields at most a Bool via INFocusStatusCenter with user authorization — the Focus mode's name is not public; 'starting a timer' has no system source (the Clock app exposes no API), so it would be an Isla-owned timer, not a mirror; and camera-live needs the grey CoreMediaIO DAL property and duplicates Apple's own menu-bar indicator. Accurate version: 'the island has zero non-media states, and the achievable set is charging/battery, audio-device changes, mic-in-use, disk pressure, an Isla-owned timer and an App Intents surface — not Focus modes, AirDrop receipt or Live Activities.' ;; Facts all check out: `Sources/IslaKit/Model/CompactMediaActivity.swift:8-19` is literally `case hidden, paused, playing`, built only from media at `Sources/IslaKit/Model/NotchViewModel.swift:214-216`; with no track `Sources/IslaKit/UI/NotchContentView.swift:152-155` renders `Color.clear` at notch size and `:78` zeroes the shadow; `grep -hoE '^import [A-Za-z]+' Sources/IslaKit | sort -u` returns exactly the 16 frameworks listed — no IOKit, CoreBluetooth, EventKit, AppIntents, UserNotifications or ScreenCaptureKit; `AudioOutputs`/`SystemVolume` are referenced only from `Sources/IslaKit/UI/LockScreenCard.swift`; `sneakPeek()` (`Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchController.swift:1018-1044`) is the only ambient behavior and fires on track change. But 'blocker' is wrong: nothing here stops a user using or trusting the app — click the notch or press ⌥⌘I and the product works, and the panel's whole job (music) functions. It is also a deliberate, documented strategy, not an oversight: `docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:104` defers multi-activity ('for a music-first island there is no second activity class yet') and `:98` rules out the widget zoo. The competitive cost is real (charging/AirPods glyphs are the single most-recognized Dynamic Island behavior and Alcove/NotchNook/MediaMate ship them), which is a major gap, not a blocker. | corrected: major — the island has no non-music state, so on notch hardware it is invisible whenever nothing is playing; that costs the product its 'always there' identity versus paid competitors, but it does not stop the app working.
- [DISPUTED] (major/L) A five-tab rail is a menu-bar app's IA transplanted onto a surface that should have none
    today: Opening the island shows a vertical rail of five SF Symbols and whichever pane was last selected, identical whether music is playing, a file is being dragged, or nothing at all is happening. Settings — a scrolling list of toggles, sliders and a Spotify OAuth button — is one of the five destinations *inside* the notch panel.
    apple: Apple's Dynamic Island has no tabs, no rail, and no persistent navigation. It is a single contextual surface whose content is decided by what is happening now; expanding it enlarges the *current* activity rather than revealing a chooser. Apple's own HIG framing (quoted at battle plan line 83) is that "the expanded view is explicitly an enlarged version of the compact presentation." macOS puts configuration in a Settings window, not in an ephemeral hover surface.
    impact: The island reads as a small floating app rather than as part of the system — the exact tell that separates 'utility someone installed' from 'feature the Mac has'. It also costs the surface its best moment: the panel opens to the wrong thing (last tab) at the one instant the user's intent is most knowable (a file mid-drag, a track just changed, AirPods just connected).
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/UI/NotchContentView.swift:430` `Rail(vm: vm, tabs: NotchViewModel.Tab.contentTabs, footer: NotchViewModel.Tab.utilityTabs)`; `NotchViewModel.swift:39-41` `contentTabs = [.media, .shelf, .clipboard, .translate]`, `utilityTabs = [.settings]`. `tab` is a plain sticky `@Published` (`NotchViewModel.swift:52`) — the panel reopens on whatever tab you left it on. There is contextual routing, but only three narrow cases: `accept(urls:)` → `.shelf` (`NotchViewModel.swift:415-419`), `receivedScreenshot` → `.shelf` (`:408-411`), and `NotchController.translate(_:)` → `.translate` (`NotchController.swift:452-475`). Nothing routes on what the *system* is doing. The project's own research doc says of exactly these tabs: `docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:97` — "**File shelf / tray / AirDrop / clipboard.** … Not music; skip." They shipped anyway. `SettingsPane.swift` is a 524-line scrolling preferences form living inside the island.
    fix: Make the open state contextual and let the rail be an escape hatch rather than the primary IA. Concretely: (1) `NotchViewModel.tab` becomes derived — on open, choose the pane by live context (drag in flight → Shelf; audio playing or paused in the last N minutes → Music; an ambient activity live → that activity's detail; else last-used), with the rail still available to override. (2) Move `SettingsPane` out of the rail entirely into a proper `Settings` scene or a small window opened from the panel — an accessory app can own one preferences window without gaining a Dock icon (`NSApplication.setActivationPolicy` need not change; use `NSWindow` with `.floating` and `orderFrontRegardless`). That reclaims a rail slot and stops the notch being a preferences form. (3) Reserve the freed slots for the activity model from `no-ambient-live-activities` rather than for more tabs.
    challenges: The structural half is exact: Sources/IslaKit/UI/NotchContentView.swift:430 is `Rail(vm: vm, tabs: NotchViewModel.Tab.contentTabs, footer: NotchViewModel.Tab.utilityTabs)`, NotchViewModel.swift:39-41 declares `contentTabs = [.media, .shelf, .clipboard, .translate]` / `utilityTabs = [.settings]`, SettingsPane.swift is 524 lines wrapping a `ScrollView` (:31) inside the panel, and nothing anywhere routes on system state. But two supporting facts are wrong. (a) `tab` is NOT sticky on the primary route: click-to-open calls `vm.select(.media)` at Sources/IslaKit/Notch/NotchController.swift:511 ("A hover always landed on Music… opening it to glance at a track showed whatever had been left behind") and the hover route does the same at :747 — only the ⌥⌘I `toggle()` (:534-545) leaves the last tab in place. (b) The panel is not "identical … [when] a file is being dragged": `onDragEntered` sets `vm.tab = .shelf` and opens at NotchController.swift:631-635, a fourth contextual route the claim's enumeration of "only three narrow cases" misses. | corrected: A five-tab rail is the island's IA and Settings is a 524-line scrolling form inside it (NotchContentView.swift:430, SettingsPane.swift:31), and nothing routes on system state. But the panel is not sticky-tabbed: click and hover both force Music (NotchController.swift:511, :747), and four deliberate routes do change the tab (drag→Shelf :633, screenshot→Shelf NotchViewModel.swift:408-411, drop→Shelf :415-419, ⌥⌘T→Translate NotchController.swift:452-475). Only ⌥⌘I reopens on the last tab. ;; The structural half is exact: NotchContentView.swift:430 always renders Rail(tabs: contentTabs, footer: utilityTabs), NotchViewModel.swift:39-40 gives the five tabs, and SettingsPane.swift is 524 lines of scrolling form living inside the notch as one of them (NotchContentView.swift:471-476). The plan quote is real (docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:97). But two load-bearing details are refuted by the code. (1) 'a file is being dragged' is not identical: NotchController.swift:633 sets vm.tab = .shelf on onDragEntered, before the drop, and ShelfPane takes isTargeted (NotchContentView.swift:465). (2) 'tab is a plain sticky @Published — the panel reopens on whatever tab you left it on' is false for the primary routes: islandClicked() calls vm.select(.media) at NotchController.swift:511 and the hover/pointer path does the same at :747, each with a comment stating exactly this reasoning ('the other tabs are somewhere to go once it is open, not somewhere to arrive'). Only the ⌥⌘I path (toggle(), :534-545) leaves the last tab selected. | corrected: 'Opening the island by click or hover always lands on Music; ⌥⌘I alone reopens on the last tab. The IA complaint survives without the stickiness claim: a five-icon rail is drawn on every open regardless of system state, Settings is a 524-line scrolling preferences form inside the notch, and the only contextual routing is drag→shelf (NotchController.swift:633), drop→shelf (NotchViewModel.swift:415-419), screenshot→shelf but only if already open (:408-411), and ⌥⌘T→translate (NotchController.swift:452-475) — nothing routes on what the system is doing.' ;; Structurally accurate — `Sources/IslaKit/UI/NotchContentView.swift:430` renders `Rail(vm:tabs:footer:)` over `contentTabs = [.media, .shelf, .clipboard, .translate]` / `utilityTabs = [.settings]` (`Sources/IslaKit/Model/NotchViogModel.swift:39-41`, verified at NotchViewModel.swift:39-41), `tab` is a sticky `@Published` with no reset on close (`NotchViewModel.swift:52`; the only writes are `NotchViewModel.swift:410,417` and `NotchController.swift:633`, all shelf), and `SettingsPane.swift` is a 524-line scrolling form with Launch at Login, sliders, a Spotify OAuth button and a Privacy section. But this is an IA-direction critique, not a defect a user names: multi-tab trays are category-normal (NotchNook, boring.notch), and with no Dock icon, menu-bar item or window (a deliberate constraint per CLAUDE.md and README:47-49) there is nowhere else Settings *could* live. The only concrete user-visible symptom is the sticky tab — click the notch expecting music and land on whatever you left open, including the preferences form. | corrected: minor — the sticky tab can reopen the panel onto Settings instead of Music, and nothing routes on system state; the rail itself is a defensible IA choice, not a user complaint.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) No volume/brightness HUD replacement, and no output routing in the expanded island — the code for both already exists but is locked to the lock screen
    today: Pressing the volume keys shows macOS's own centered HUD, unchanged. In the expanded Music pane there is no way to change volume or pick an output device — the user must go to Control Center or the menu bar. The output picker and volume slider Isla already built are reachable only by locking the Mac.
    apple: Apple's own expanded Now Playing widget (Control Center, Lock Screen) carries a volume slider and an AirPlay routing button as part of the canonical media grammar. macOS's volume/brightness HUD is a floating centered panel that predates the notch and has never been updated for it.
    impact: This is the single most-marketed feature of the paid field — MediaMate is essentially *only* this, and Alcove, NotchNook and DynamicLake Pro all lead with a notch-anchored HUD. A prospective switcher's first test is 'press volume up and see what happens'; Isla fails it, then fails the second test (change output without leaving the island) despite having the code.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/SystemVolume.swift` reads and writes `kAudioDevicePropertyVolumeScalar`; `Sources/IslaKit/Services/AudioOutputs.swift` lists devices and switches the system default (`select(_:)`). Both are referenced from exactly one file: `Sources/IslaKit/UI/LockScreenCard.swift:415,418-419,457-459,527,530`. `MediaPane.swift`'s control row (`:317-372`) is shuffle / previous / play-pause / next / repeat — `rg -n "volume|output|AirPlay|route" Sources/IslaKit/UI/MediaPane.swift` returns nothing. There is no volume-key interception anywhere: the only `NSEvent` monitors in the tree are `NotchController.swift:553` (a global *click* monitor for the pinned panel) and `ScrollWheelWatch.swift:28` (a local scroll monitor). The project's own plan ranks this as Move 5 (`docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:72-75`): "Apple's own expanded media widget includes an AirPlay output-routing button … **no notch app has it**", and line 102 explicitly permits "a volume HUD as part of Move 5's volume work" while ruling out brightness.
    fix: Two separable steps, cheap one first. (a) **Move 5, one afternoon:** add an output/volume row to `MediaPane`'s control column reusing `AudioOutputs.available()/current()/select()` and `SystemVolume.current()/set()` exactly as `LockScreenCard.swift:412-460,520-532` already does, honoring the same 'absent rather than dead' rule when a device publishes no volume (`SystemVolume.swift:9-14`). (b) **Volume HUD:** register an `AudioObjectAddPropertyListenerBlock` on `kAudioDevicePropertyVolumeScalar`/`Mute` for the default output — this needs no permission and no event tap — and peek the island with a level bar for ~1.2s using the existing `sneakPeek()` path. Ship this *alongside* the system HUD first (it is honest and costs nothing); only later offer an opt-in 'Replace the system HUD' toggle, documenting that suppression is the part that costs Accessibility. Brightness stays out, per the plan's own line 102.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) Translate is a feature looking for a home — English↔Russian only, dead on most of the supported install base, and the only tab that steals the keyboard
    today: On any Intel Mac, and on every Mac running macOS 15–25, the Translate tab is a permanent error message ('Translation needs macOS 26 or newer.'), and ⌥⌘T does nothing visible but open the panel onto it. On a supported Mac it translates between exactly two languages, chosen for it by a Cyrillic-detection heuristic.
    apple: Apple ships translation as a system-wide Service, a Safari page action, and a Translate app — reachable everywhere from the selection context menu, in 20+ languages, with an explicit language picker. Apple would not put a two-language translator in a transient hover surface, and would not ship a top-level destination that is inert on a large fraction of supported hardware.
    impact: 20% of the rail, one of two global shortcuts, the app's only keyboard-stealing behavior, and its only Services entry are spent on something most users cannot use and almost no user asked a notch app for. Worse, it is the one tab that can dim the title bar and stop the caret in whatever the user was typing in.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/Translator.swift:82-87` hardcodes the route: any Cyrillic → English, everything else → Russian. There is no other language pair and no picker. `Translator.swift:107` `guard #available(macOS 26.0, *) else { return .needsNewerSystem }`, and availability further requires an eligible device with Apple Intelligence on (`:108-118`, cases `.deviceNotEligible` / `.appleIntelligenceNotEnabled`). `README.md:10` states support for "**macOS 15+** (Sequoia), Apple Silicon or Intel" — Apple Intelligence never runs on Intel. The tab occupies one of four content slots (`NotchViewModel.swift:39`) and is the only one that takes the keyboard from the frontmost app (`NotchViewModel.swift:32` `var needsKeyboard: Bool { self == .translate }`; `NotchPanel.swift:28` `canBecomeKey: Bool { acceptsKeyboard }`). It also owns one of only two global hotkeys (`AppDelegate.swift:49-55`) and the app's only Services-menu entry (`AppDelegate.swift:56,74-81`).
    fix: Cut the Translate tab. Keep the capability where it already belongs and costs nothing: the ⌥⌘T hotkey and the 'Translate in Isla' Service can render into a transient *peek* activity (the same `sneakPeek` surface from the ambient model) rather than a rail destination — text in, text out, auto-dismiss, no keyboard claim. That deletes `needsKeyboard` entirely, which lets `NotchPanel.canBecomeKey` become a hard `false` and removes the whole `acceptsKeyboard`/`orderOut`+`orderFrontRegardless` key-resign dance (`NotchPanel.swift:12-29`). Record the removal as a dated **Withdrawn** amendment in `docs/plans/2026-08-18-dynamic-island-parity-design.md` §Translate and in `checklist.md`, and update `TabContractTests.swift:9,34,37` the way Snippets/Calendar/Notes were handled. If the two-language pair is a personal need, it belongs behind the Service, not on the rail.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/M) Clipboard history is a checkbox feature: 40 items, session-only, no search, no keyboard route
    today: Copy forty things and the forty-first evicts the first. Quit or reboot and everything is gone. Finding an item copied twenty minutes ago means scrolling a list with the mouse; there is no way to search it and no way to touch it from the keyboard.
    apple: macOS itself has no clipboard history, so the bar is set by what people already run — Raycast, Maccy, Paste, Alfred — all of which persist across launches, search as you type, and are driven entirely from the keyboard. Apple's own analogous surfaces (Spotlight, the emoji picker) are keyboard-first by default.
    impact: Anyone who wants clipboard history already has a better one bound to a shortcut they have muscle memory for. Isla's version cannot replace it, so it is a tab they will click once and never again — while consuming 25% of the content rail and running a 2 Hz pasteboard poll (`ClipboardStore.swift:101-108`) for the privilege.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/ClipboardStore.swift:78` `private let limit = 40`, enforced at `:274`. Nothing in the file writes to `UserDefaults` or disk (`rg -n "defaults|UserDefaults|persist" Sources/IslaKit/Services/ClipboardStore.swift` returns one unrelated comment at `:150`) — history dies with the process, unlike `ShelfStore` which persists to `defaults` under `shelf.urls` (`ShelfStore.swift:36`). `Sources/IslaKit/UI/ClipboardPane.swift` has no `TextField`, no filter, no pinning; it is a `LazyVStack` of rows (`:19-23`) plus a Clear button (`:32-40`). The panel cannot take the keyboard unless the Translate tab asks (`NotchPanel.swift:28`, `NotchViewModel.swift:32`), so the list is mouse-only: no type-to-filter, no arrow keys, no Return-to-paste.
    fix: Pick one of two honest positions and commit. **Either** make it real: persist to `~/Library/Application Support/Isla/clipboard.json` via the existing `DebouncedWrite` (as `ShelfStore` does), raise the cap, add a search field, and give the pane a keyboard route — a third hotkey that opens the panel *and* sets `wantsKeyboard`, with type-to-filter, ↑/↓ and Return-to-paste (the `NotchPanel.sendEvent` dispatch at `:59-90` already proves an inactive panel can be driven from the keyboard). **Or** cut the tab and keep only the part the notch is uniquely good at: a copied screenshot becoming a Shelf card, which `ClipboardStore.onImage` (`:67`) → `ScreenshotVault` already does. Given the strategy doc's own line 97, and that Raycast/Maccy own this job, the cut is the stronger move — it frees a rail slot for an ambient activity and removes a 2 Hz timer.
- [CONFIRMED] (major/S) The one thing no competitor ships — word-synced karaoke — is default-off and absent from the product's own feature list
    today: A new user installs Isla, plays a song, opens the island, and sees a competent but unremarkable player. To reach the differentiator they must find Settings → Music → Show Lyrics with no in-product hint that lyrics exist at all, and then discover the caption row is tappable to reach the stage (`MediaPane.swift:52-58`).
    apple: Apple never hides its headline feature behind a preference. When a capability needs consent (Siri history, Apple Music Sing, personalization), Apple asks *at the moment of value* with a one-line explanation and a button, then the feature is on. The pattern is 'offer in context', not 'off in Settings'.
    impact: The single reason to switch from a $25 app is invisible on first run and unmentioned in the README, so it does not convert anyone. Every review, screenshot and word-of-mouth comparison will be made against the feature set the user actually saw — which is the same feature set the competitors have, minus their ambient states.
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/Services/WordSyncedLyrics.swift` parses TTML (amll-ttml-db, per-syllable `<span begin=… end=…>`) and Kugou KRC; `LyricsStore.swift:1-16` adds LRCLIB with on-disk caching capped at 500 tracks; `LyricSweep` + `KaraokeText` + `LyricRow` render one shared timeline across the island stage and the lock card. The project's own competitive teardown calls this "the moat" and states no competitor has it (`docs/plans/2026-08-21-music-first-battle-plan.md:16-17`). Yet `NotchViewModel.swift:243-252` — `static var showLyricsEnabled { UserDefaults.standard.bool(forKey: showLyricsKey) }` — defaults to **off**, and `README.md:36-56` ("What Isla does") lists click-to-open, hover, hotkeys, the lock screen, no-Dock-icon, the five tabs, and materials — **lyrics are never mentioned**. They appear only in the Privacy section (`README.md:65-68`) as a network-egress warning.
    fix: Keep the privacy posture, change the offer. In `MediaPane`, when `!NotchViewModel.showLyricsEnabled` and a track is playing, put the empty caption slot (`MediaPane.captionHeight`, `:44`) to work as a one-time inline offer: 'Show lyrics for this track? Isla will send the title, artist and album to lrclib.net.' with Turn On / Not Now — consent at the moment of value, still explicit, still user-granted, still off until pressed. Then lead the README's 'What Isla does' with it: word-by-word karaoke on the island *and* over the lock screen, sourced three ways, cached, with a kill switch. Ship a GIF of the sweep as the repo's first image; it is the only asset that will make a NotchNook owner click.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) The Shelf has no AirDrop and no Quick Look — the two things that make a notch shelf worth dragging to
    today: A file dragged to the notch can be dragged back out, copied, opened, or revealed in Finder. To send it to a phone or another Mac the user drags it out again and into Finder's or the Share menu's AirDrop.
    apple: macOS puts Share (with AirDrop first) in every Finder context menu, on every toolbar, and behind ⌘-space Quick Look on any selection. `NSSharingServicePicker` and `QLPreviewPanel` are public, permission-free, and the expected affordances on anything that holds files.
    impact: 'Drag to the notch, AirDrop to my phone' is the single sentence NotchDrop and NotchNook sell the shelf on, and it is the one job a notch shelf does better than the Desktop. Without it the Shelf is a slower Finder window that happens to live at the top of the screen — which undercuts the case for the tab existing at all (see the strategy doc's line 97).
    evidence: `Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift:191-197` — the card's entire context menu is `Copy` / `Open` / `Show in Finder` / `Remove from Shelf`. `rg -n "NSSharingService|AirDrop|QLPreview" Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfPane.swift Sources/IslaKit/UI/ShelfDragSource.swift` returns nothing. Drag-in (`NotchRootView.swift:148-241`, including `NSFilePromiseReceiver` support) and drag-out (`ShelfDragSource`, multi-file selection via `ShelfStore.dragURLs`) are both well built; the shelf simply cannot *do* anything with what it holds beyond opening or revealing it. No rename, no space-to-preview.
    fix: Add two rows to the `ShelfCard` context menu and one to the footer: `NSSharingServicePicker(items: shelf.dragURLs(startingAt: item))` shown from the card's frame (AirDrop, Messages, Mail all come free), and a `Quick Look` row calling `QLPreviewPanel` / `NSWorkspace`. Both are public API, need no entitlement, and honor the existing selection model. If the Shelf tab is kept at all, this is what justifies it; if it is cut, AirDrop-on-drop (drag a file to the notch → island offers 'AirDrop' as a peek) is the version worth keeping.
- [CONFIRMED] (minor/S) An app with no Dock icon, no menu-bar item, no window, no onboarding — and an invisible island until music plays
    today: Double-click Isla.app. Nothing appears — no Dock bounce that persists, no menu-bar item, no window, no notification, and (if no music is playing) no visible island. There is no way to learn that ⌥⌘I exists, that the notch is clickable, that lyrics exist, or that the app is even running, except by reading the README.
    apple: Even Apple's most invisible utilities announce themselves once: a first-run panel, a menu-bar item, or a system notification. macOS's own accessory surfaces (Control Center modules, Stage Manager) are discoverable by hovering somewhere the user already looks. Apple never ships a launch that produces zero feedback.
    impact: A meaningful share of first-time users will conclude the app failed to launch and delete it — which is fatal for a free app competing on word of mouth, because the install is the only thing you get for free and it is being spent on nothing. It also compounds `no-ambient-live-activities`: the two failures together mean a silent-music user never sees the product at all.
    evidence: `.accessory` policy set once and never changed (`checklist.md:67-75`, `IslaApplication.run()`); `AppDelegate.applicationDidFinishLaunching` (`:17-69`) installs the controller, the two hot keys and the Services provider, and shows nothing. `rg -n "onboard|firstRun|welcome|hasLaunched|tutorial" Sources/ Resources/` returns no matches. With nothing playing, the collapsed island renders `Color.clear` at exactly notch size with zero shadow (`NotchContentView.swift:78,152-155`). The only routes in are a click/hover on the notch, ⌥⌘I / ⌥⌘T (hardcoded at `GlobalHotKey.defaultKeyCode`/`translateKeyCode`, with no rebinding UI anywhere in `SettingsPane.swift`), the Services entry, and the `isla://` Spotify callback.
    fix: On first launch only (a `didCompleteFirstRun` default), open the panel to a short Welcome pane — what the island is, that clicking it opens this, that ⌥⌘I works, that lyrics are one toggle away — and auto-dismiss it the same way a translate peek dismisses. Cheap and consistent with the no-window rule, since the panel already opens itself for `DI_OPEN_LYRICS` (`AppDelegate.swift:63-69`) and for ⌥⌘I. Separately, give the two hot keys a rebinding row in `SettingsPane` (they are `RegisterEventHotKey`-based, so re-registration is a one-line unregister/register on `GlobalHotKey`) — ⌥⌘I and ⌥⌘T are both plausible collisions, and today a conflict is unfixable without a rebuild.
